### Other modules

#### `ui/agent.rs`

Source: [`lib/ui/agent.rs`](../lib/ui/agent.rs)

```text
Universal application automation through the existing input, command and render paths.
Domain semantics remain in App::actions/action; no screen-coordinate inference is used.
```

**`impl Ui`**

- **`fn agent_enabled(&self) -> bool`**
  Whether external control was explicitly activated. No endpoint opens by reading this.
- **`fn agent_connected(&self) -> bool`**
  Whether the active control session has received a client request.
- **`fn set_agent_enabled(&mut self, enabled: bool)`**
  Enable or revoke external control. Revocation also discards screenshot resources.

**`impl Runtime`**

- **`fn set_agent_enabled(&mut self, enabled: bool)`**
  Explicitly opt into the same control dispatcher used by native JSON/MCP sessions.
- **`fn control(&mut self, session: &mut agent_core::RpcSession, line: &[u8]) -> Option<String>`**
  Handle one control request without IO. The caller owns one session per connection.

#### `ui/text_surface.rs`

Source: [`lib/ui/text_surface.rs`](../lib/ui/text_surface.rs)

```text
Input/selection overlay for externally laid out rich text. New opt-in UI API.
The caller paints content; the existing textbox engine handles Unicode, clipboard and IME.
```

- **`struct TextSurfaceLine`**
  One visual line of a text editing surface, in absolute physical window coordinates.
  - `start: usize` -- UTF-8 byte range, excluding an explicit newline. Soft wraps share their boundary.
  - `end: usize`
  - `rect: [i32; 4]` -- Hit/selection rectangle [x, y, width, height]. It may cover a different page per line.
  - `carets: Vec<(usize, i32)>` -- UTF-8 byte caret stops and their absolute x coordinates, in logical order.

**`impl Ui`**

- **`fn textbox_surface( &mut self, id: u32, text: &mut String, cap_flags: u32, lines: &[TextSurfaceLine], ) -> u32`**
  Edit one UTF-8 buffer over caller-rendered rich text, without repainting its content.
  Layout may span differently sized/aligned lines and pages; each UTF-8 scalar boundary
  needs a caret stop. Text changes report EV_ACT and request a fresh layout on the next
  pass. Invalid ranges reject the call without consuming input. The caller owns scroll
  positioning and document history. Ordinary textboxes are unaffected.
- **`fn textbox_surface_change(&self) -> Option<(usize, usize, String)>`**
  Replacement in the original buffer from the most recent surface call. The range
  respects the actual insertion location even when surrounding characters repeat.

#### `ui/office_gallery.rs`

Source: [`lib/ui/office_gallery.rs`](../lib/ui/office_gallery.rs)

```text
Reusable document-theme thumbnails drawn with the shared UI rasterizer.
This is a visual chooser, independent of an application's document commands or formats.
```

**`impl Ui`**

- **`fn preview_card(&mut self, id: u32, label: &str, draw: Option<crate::DrawRef>, user: usize, version: u32) -> u32`**
  Focusable document/template preview card, naturally 150×194 DIP. The supplied
  drawing is inset into a white page; its document colors never follow the UI theme.
  Hover and selection use the style accent. The full label is exposed to agents.
- **`fn ribbon_theme_tile( &mut self, id: u32, label: &str, theme_color: u32, variant: u32, ) -> u32`**
  A 56×66-DIP theme chooser containing a 52×50-DIP miniature page.
  `theme_color` is a document color, independent of the interface theme.
  `variant` selects one of seven sample arrangements (0..=6); larger values wrap.
  `next_flags(ST_DISABLED | ST_CHECKED)` controls behavior and selection, while
  the document sample remains fully readable. The caller applies its own theme on EV_ACT.

#### `ui/perm.rs`

Source: [`lib/ui/perm.rs`](../lib/ui/perm.rs)

```text
perm -- the app's permissions on the UI side (plat::policy, docs/PERMISSIONS.md): the
manifest built into the app, the files the user hands over and the library's own file use

  permissions(manifest)   (main!) install apps/NAME/PERMISSIONS, which build_rs.py passes
                          as JFGUI_PERMISSIONS; Err(exit code) for a manifest it refuses.
                          The paths on the command line come from the user: with fs.user
                          they may be read and written (folders with everything below).
  grant_user(path)        the same for a path of the file dialog or a drop
  trusted(f)              f on this thread without file checks: the library's own file
                          dialog lists folders, scripts read and write their files

The key of plat::policy stays here: apps cannot widen their own permissions.
```

- **`fn permissions(manifest: Option<&str>) -> Result<(), i32>`**
  (main!) install the app's manifest (None: the app has none, everything stays allowed)

#### `ui/ribbon.rs`

Source: [`lib/ui/ribbon.rs`](../lib/ui/ribbon.rs)

```text
Ribbon groups and typography tiles. Additive native UI component by office_ribbon;
no legacy counterpart. Uses normal focus, semantics, popups and style tokens.
```

- **`struct RibbonSample`**
  Explicit typography for a document sample, independent of the application's theme.
  Sizes and baseline displacement are 24.8 DIP; positive displacement moves downwards.
  - `font: u32`
  - `size: i32`
  - `color: u32`
  - `highlight: Option<u32>`
  - `baseline_shift: i32`
  - `underline: bool`
  - `strike: bool`

- **`struct RibbonMetrics`**
  Style-owned ribbon geometry in DIP. A scrolling ribbon keeps every group's
  original arrangement; a collapsing ribbon can use the compact/flyout layouts.
  - `content_height: i32`
  - `caption_height: i32`
  - `group_gap: i32`
  - `content_gap: i32`
  - `inset_x: i32` -- Zero uses the style's existing uniform `ribbon_padding`.
  - `inset_y: i32`
  - `collapse_groups: bool`

- **`struct RibbonGroup<'a>`**
  One ribbon group. Reserve `id..=id + 3` for its box, overflow and popup.
  - `id: u32` -- Stable base ID, unique across the window.
  - `label: &'a str` -- Visible group caption and accessible overflow name.
  - `width: i32` -- Preferred group width in DIP.
  - `compact_width: i32` -- Smallest width that still displays all controls, in DIP.

**`impl<'a> RibbonGroup<'a>`**

- **`const fn new(id: u32, label: &'a str, width: i32, compact_width: i32) -> Self`**
  Construct a group with a preferred and compact content width.

- **`enum RibbonGroupMode`**
  Presentation of an open ribbon group; pass it to `ribbon_group_end`.
  - `Inline(i32)` -- Preferred or compact inline width in DIP.
  - `Flyout(i32)` -- Group shown in a popup at its preferred width in DIP.

**`impl RibbonGroupMode`**

- **`fn width(self) -> i32`**
  Actual width available to the controls, in DIP.

- **`struct RibbonLayout`**
  Per-frame ribbon layout: shrink groups before moving them into labeled flyouts.

**`impl RibbonLayout`**

- **`fn new(available_width: i32, groups: &[RibbonGroup<'_>]) -> Self`**
  Compute group presentation for an available inner width in DIP.
  Groups collapse from right to left; extremely narrow windows can scroll.
- **`fn with_metrics( available_width: i32, groups: &[RibbonGroup<'_>], metrics: RibbonMetrics, ) -> Self`**
  Compute a layout using the active style's sizing and overflow policy.
- **`fn is_collapsed(&self, index: usize) -> bool`**
  Whether a group is reachable through its labeled overflow button.
- **`fn group_width(&self, index: usize) -> Option<i32>`**
  Width occupied by a group in the ribbon, in DIP.

**`impl Ui`**

- **`fn ribbon_begin(&mut self, id: u32, groups: &[RibbonGroup<'_>]) -> RibbonLayout`**
  Start a style-sized ribbon (110 DIP by default). Pair with `ribbon_end`.
  All groups remain keyboard reachable; a scroll strip is the final fallback.
- **`fn ribbon_group_begin( &mut self, layout: &RibbonLayout, index: usize, group: &RibbonGroup<'_>, ) -> Option<RibbonGroupMode>`**
  Begin a group's controls when visible, or render its overflow button.
  Only call `ribbon_group_end` when this returns `Some`.
- **`fn ribbon_group_end(&mut self, mode: RibbonGroupMode, label: &str)`**
  Finish controls and draw the inline group caption; closes overflow when appropriate.
  Flyouts already have their named opener, so no trailing caption is painted
  over a nested action menu emitted while building their controls.
- **`fn ribbon_group_end_launcher( &mut self, mode: RibbonGroupMode, label: &str, id: u32, action_label: &str, flags: u32, ) -> u32`**
  Finish a group with a separately focusable dialog-launch button in its caption.
  `id` is an additional unique widget ID; `action_label` is its full semantic name.
  Activation is returned to the app, which opens its existing dialog. Disabled
  launchers retain their label and cannot be invoked through keyboard or agents.
- **`fn ribbon_end(&mut self)`**
  Finish the ribbon begun with `ribbon_begin`.
- **`fn ribbon_button_width(&mut self, label: &str, icon: u32, flags: u32) -> i32`**
  Natural action width in DIP, including the style's icon, label and menu
  spacing. Measuring neither consumes next_\* hints nor creates a widget.
  Use it when composing translated ribbon columns before ribbon_begin.
- **`fn ribbon_button(&mut self, id: u32, label: &str, icon: u32, flags: u32) -> u32`**
  Compact ribbon action with clipped drawing, native focus and a full tooltip.
  `ST_ICON_ONLY`, `ST_LARGE`, `ST_CHECKED` and `ST_DISABLED` are supported.
- **`fn ribbon_large_button(&mut self, id: u32, label: &str, icon: u32, flags: u32) -> u32`**
  Large ribbon action: the style draws its icon above a visible caption.
  It is one normal focusable, inspectable button. Use `next_size` to override
  its natural style size; checked/disabled flags keep their ordinary meaning.
- **`fn ribbon_menu_button(&mut self, id: u32, label: &str, icon: u32, flags: u32) -> u32`**
  Ribbon-styled menu opener. `id` is also the popup identifier. Its natural
  dimensions, icon, menu indicator and enabled state come from the style.
- **`fn ribbon_stacked_split_button( &mut self, id: u32, menu_id: u32, label: &str, menu_label: &str, icon: u32, flags: u32, ) -> u32`**
  Large split action with an icon hit area above its menu caption. Both
  parts remain independently focusable and discoverable through semantics.
  Pair the `menu_id` with a normal flyout. Alt+Down/F4 opens the caption menu.
- **`fn ribbon_split_button( &mut self, id: u32, menu_id: u32, label: &str, menu_label: &str, icon: u32, flags: u32, ) -> u32`**
  A large primary action beside an independently focusable dropdown.
  `id` identifies the primary action; `menu_id` identifies both the dropdown
  button and its popup. Both IDs must be unique. `menu_label` is a full semantic
  name, not an unlabeled chevron. Pair with `flyout_begin(menu_id, ..)` and
  `flyout_end` immediately afterwards to provide actual secondary actions.
  Returns primary EV_\* flags plus EV_OPEN while the dropdown popup is open.
  Tab reaches both parts; Enter/Space activate the focused part. Alt+Down or
  unbound F4 on the main part forwards to the existing dropdown keyboard path.
- **`fn ribbon_gallery_begin(&mut self, id: u32)`**
  Begin an inset horizontal gallery on an input-colored surface. The caller
  supplies its size and tiles; finish with `box_end`. The whole frame is painted
  before applying the three-DIP tile inset, including in dark themes.
- **`fn ribbon_tile( &mut self, id: u32, label: &str, preview: &str, typography: u32, selected: bool, ) -> u32`**
  Typography preview tile for a style gallery; selection is exposed semantically.
  `typography` is a `TS_*` style; use `next_size` to set a compact gallery width.
- **`fn ribbon_font_tile(&mut self, id: u32, label: &str, preview: &str, typography: u32, selected: bool, face: Option<(u32, i32, u32)>) -> u32`**
  A gallery tile with an optional document font, 24.8-DIP size and ARGB ink.
  The surrounding control follows the application theme; the sample stays document-colored.
- **`fn ribbon_sample_tile(&mut self, id: u32, label: &str, preview: &str, typography: u32, selected: bool, sample: Option<RibbonSample>) -> u32`**
  Style-gallery tile with explicit highlight, baseline and text decorations.

#### `ui/slider.rs`

Source: [`lib/ui/slider.rs`](../lib/ui/slider.rs)

**`impl Range`**

- **`fn position(&self, value: i32, travel: i32) -> i32`**
  Map a value to the slider's usable pixel travel. RG_LOG applies to positive,
  increasing bounds; all other bounds use linear mapping. Degenerate ranges
  or travel return zero. Values outside the range are clamped.
- **`fn value_at(&self, position: i32, travel: i32) -> i32`**
  Map pixel travel to the nearest integer value, before step snapping. The
  logarithmic inverse uses bounded binary search (at most 31 iterations).
  Keyboard steps and semantic values always remain in the original units.

## agent_core

*transport-independent actions, schema validation, JSON-RPC, MCP and tasks* -- crate root [`lib/agent_core/src/lib.rs`](../lib/agent_core/src/lib.rs)

```text
Transport-independent action contracts and bounded JSON. New shared implementation;
no corresponding module exists in the frozen assembly reference.
```

### `agent_core/src/action.rs`

Source: [`lib/agent_core/src/action.rs`](../lib/agent_core/src/action.rs)

```text
Shared semantic action dispatch. GUI adapters and all external transports call this core.
New library code, independent of native windows, document types and language models.
```

- **`enum ErrorCode`**
  Stable structured failure categories.
  - `Disabled` -- External control has not been explicitly activated.
  - `NotFound` -- The action or resource is unknown.
  - `InvalidInput` -- Parameters fail their declared schema or bounds.
  - `CapabilityUnavailable` -- The platform lacks a required capability.
  - `Unavailable` -- The action is temporarily unavailable (including modal UI states).
  - `RevisionConflict` -- The caller's expected revision differs from the current revision.
  - `Unsupported` -- An operation or atomic transaction is unsupported.
  - `LimitExceeded` -- A bounded collection or response limit was exceeded.
  - `Cancelled` -- A task was cancelled at a defined interruption point.
  - `Internal` -- The host violated its action contract or returned an internal failure.

**`impl ErrorCode`**

- **`fn as_str(self) -> &'static str`**
  Stable wire spelling.

- **`struct ActionError`**
  An actionable failure with structured details; no transport-specific error numbers.
  - `code: ErrorCode` -- Stable error category.
  - `message: String` -- Human-readable diagnosis.
  - `details: Value` -- Structured correction details such as a field pointer or current revision.

**`impl ActionError`**

- **`fn new(code: ErrorCode, message: impl Into<String>) -> Self`**
  Construct a failure without extra details.
- **`fn with_details(mut self, details: Value) -> Self`**
  Attach machine-readable context.
- **`fn to_json(&self) -> Value`**
  JSON form shared by batch, JSON-RPC and MCP results.

- **`struct ActionSpec`**
  A static semantic action contract. Register each implementation once in its host.
  - `name: &'static str` -- Stable, language-independent identifier, e.g. `document.text.replace`.
  - `description: &'static str` -- Description for human and automated discovery.
  - `input: &'static Schema` -- Validated input schema.
  - `output: &'static Schema` -- Validated completed-result schema.
  - `capabilities: u64` -- Bit set of platform services required by this action; defined by the platform layer.
  - `mutation: bool` -- Whether the action can change application or document state.
  - `revision_required: bool` -- Require an expected revision for external mutation calls.
  - `undo: bool` -- Whether the operation is integrated with the application's undo history.
  - `transaction: Option<&'static str>` -- Transaction domain, or None when atomic batches are unsupported.
  - `cancellable: bool` -- Whether the operation can return a cancellable task.
  - `command_id: Option<u32>` -- Optional compatible numeric GUI command identifier.

**`impl ActionSpec`**

- **`const fn read(name: &'static str, description: &'static str) -> Self`**
  Parameterless read action with a general structured-object result.
- **`const fn write(name: &'static str, description: &'static str) -> Self`**
  Revision-checked mutation; the caller can set undo and transaction metadata.
- **`const fn schemas(mut self, input: &'static Schema, output: &'static Schema) -> Self`**
  Replace both schemas while preserving other metadata.

- **`struct ActionContext`**
  Request metadata passed unchanged to registered handlers.
  - `expected_revision: Option<u64>` -- Expected revision supplied by the caller and checked by the dispatcher.
  - `atomic: bool` -- True when executing inside a host transaction.
  - `local: bool` -- True for trusted in-process GUI commands, which may omit an expected revision.

- **`struct Resource`**
  Small metadata link to binary/file data, kept out of state snapshots.
  - `uri: String` -- Opaque application resource identifier.
  - `name: String` -- Friendly name.
  - `mime_type: String` -- Content media type.
  - `size: Option<u64>` -- Exact payload byte count if known.

**`impl Resource`**

- **`fn to_json(&self) -> Value`**
  Metadata only; resource bytes are read separately.

- **`struct ActionResult`**
  Action completion or a deferred host-managed task.
  - `value: Value` -- Completed structured output (Null for deferred work).
  - `revision: u64` -- Current host revision after execution.
  - `task: Option<u64>` -- Task identifier when execution continues asynchronously.
  - `resources: Vec<Resource>` -- Metadata for optional separately readable payloads.

**`impl ActionResult`**

- **`fn completed(value: Value, revision: u64) -> Self`**
  Return a completed result.
- **`fn deferred(task: u64, revision: u64) -> Self`**
  Return a task whose state can be queried or cancelled.
- **`fn to_json(&self) -> Value`**
  Shared structured wire response.

- **`trait Host`**
  Application adapter; the dispatcher never invents domain semantics from pixels.
  - `fn actions(&self) -> &[ActionSpec]`
    All registered action contracts, normally a static slice.
  - `fn revision(&self) -> u64`
    Current document/application revision for optimistic concurrency control.
  - `fn capabilities(&self) -> u64`
    Services currently available from the native or browser platform.
  - `fn availability(&self, _action: &ActionSpec) -> Result<(), ActionError>`
    Check dynamic availability, including modal state, selection and permissions.
  - `fn execute( &mut self, action: &ActionSpec, input: &Value, context: &ActionContext, ) -> Result<ActionResult, ActionError>`
    Execute the same handler used by GUI/keyboard command adapters.
  - `fn supports_transaction(&self, _domain: &str) -> bool`
    Whether a named transaction domain supports reliable rollback.
  - `fn begin_transaction(&mut self, _domain: &str) -> Result<(), ActionError>`
    Begin one undoable transaction. Atomic actions must complete synchronously.
  - `fn commit_transaction(&mut self) -> Result<(), ActionError>`
    Commit all changes in the active transaction.
  - `fn rollback_transaction(&mut self) -> Result<(), ActionError>`
    Restore state and revision to before begin_transaction.
  - `fn inspect(&self, _query: &Value) -> Result<Value, ActionError>`
    Filtered, paginated semantic state; hosts may accept a `sinceRevision` query.
  - `fn task_status(&self, _id: u64) -> Result<Value, ActionError>`
    Query a host-managed asynchronous task.
  - `fn task_cancel(&mut self, _id: u64) -> Result<Value, ActionError>`
    Request cancellation at the task's next defined interruption point.
  - `fn resource_info(&self, _uri: &str) -> Result<Resource, ActionError>`
    Metadata for one application-owned resource; arbitrary filesystem paths are not URIs.
  - `fn resource_read( &self, _uri: &str, _offset: u64, _limit: usize, ) -> Result<Vec<u8>, ActionError>`
    Read a bounded resource slice. Offsets are bytes; unknown URIs must be rejected.

- **`struct Invocation<'a>`**
  Borrowed single invocation.
  - `action: &'a str` -- Stable action name.
  - `input: &'a Value` -- Structured parameters.
  - `expected_revision: Option<u64>` -- Expected revision for mutations.

- **`struct BatchCall`**
  Owned invocation for an ordered batch.
  - `action: String` -- Stable action name.
  - `input: Value` -- Structured parameters.
  - `expected_revision: Option<u64>` -- Revision at the moment this call should execute; atomic calls refer to the batch start.

- **`struct BatchResult`**
  Ordered batch outcome, retaining the outcome of each attempted call.
  - `results: Vec<Result<ActionResult, ActionError>>` -- Per-call results in original order.
  - `rolled_back: bool` -- True if a failed atomic batch was rolled back successfully.
  - `revision: u64` -- Current revision, including rollback.

**`impl BatchResult`**

- **`fn to_json(&self) -> Value`**
  Structured batch result.

- **`struct Page`**
  One deterministic page of action discovery.
  - `actions: Vec<Value>` -- Metadata of matching actions, without full schemas.
  - `next_cursor: Option<String>` -- Exclusive action-name cursor for the next page.
  - `revision: u64` -- Current state revision.

**`impl Page`**

- **`fn to_json(&self) -> Value`**
  Shared discovery response.

- **`struct Dispatcher`**
  Activation gate and deterministic dispatcher. Opening transports remains a host decision.

**`impl Dispatcher`**

- **`const fn new() -> Self`**
  Construct with all external access disabled.
- **`fn set_enabled(&mut self, enabled: bool)`**
  Explicitly enable/disable control; transports should also close when disabled.
- **`fn enabled(&self) -> bool`**
  Whether external access was explicitly enabled.
- **`fn check_enabled(&self) -> Result<(), ActionError>`**
  Reject access before consulting application state.
- **`fn execute( &self, host: &mut impl Host, call: Invocation<'_>, ) -> Result<ActionResult, ActionError>`**
  Execute an enabled external action after schema, capability and revision checks.
- **`fn execute_local( &self, host: &mut impl Host, name: &str, input: &Value, ) -> Result<ActionResult, ActionError>`**
  Invoke a trusted GUI/shortcut command through the same schema and handler.
- **`fn execute_command( &self, host: &mut impl Host, id: u32, input: &Value, ) -> Result<ActionResult, ActionError>`**
  Resolve a legacy numeric GUI command to its registered semantic handler.
- **`fn batch( &self, host: &mut impl Host, calls: &[BatchCall], atomic: bool, stop_on_error: bool, ) -> Result<BatchResult, ActionError>`**
  Run an ordered batch. Atomic support and every static precondition are checked first.
- **`fn discover( &self, host: &impl Host, query: &str, after: Option<&str>, limit: usize, ) -> Result<Page, ActionError>`**
  Find actions by name/description, with stable lexical ordering and name cursors.
- **`fn describe(&self, host: &impl Host, name: &str) -> Result<Value, ActionError>`**
  Return schemas, behavior metadata and a concrete availability reason for one action.

- **`fn validate_registry(actions: &[ActionSpec]) -> Result<(), ActionError>`**
  Validate stable identifiers and prevent ambiguous name/command registrations.

### `agent_core/src/json.rs`

Source: [`lib/agent_core/src/json.rs`](../lib/agent_core/src/json.rs)

```text
Strict, bounded UTF-8 JSON for local and browser control channels. New shared code.
```

- **`struct Number(String)`**
  Exact JSON number token; construction validates the JSON number grammar.

**`impl Number`**

- **`fn parse(text: &str) -> Option<Self>`**
  Parse one complete JSON number without rounding it through floating point.
- **`fn as_str(&self) -> &str`**
  Original validated JSON representation.
- **`fn as_i64(&self) -> Option<i64>`**
  Read an exactly integral JSON number without precision loss, including exponents.
- **`fn as_u64(&self) -> Option<u64>`**
  Read an exactly integral, nonnegative JSON number without precision loss.

- **`enum Value`**
  Owned JSON data. Objects retain member order and parsed objects have unique keys.
  - `Null` -- JSON null.
  - `Bool(bool)` -- JSON boolean.
  - `Number(Number)` -- Exact JSON number.
  - `String(String)` -- Unicode string.
  - `Array(Vec<Value>)` -- Ordered values.
  - `Object(Vec<(String, Value)>)` -- Ordered name/value members.

**`impl Value`**

- **`fn string(value: impl Into<String>) -> Self`**
  Make a JSON string.
- **`fn u64(value: u64) -> Self`**
  Make an unsigned number without converting to floating point.
- **`fn i64(value: i64) -> Self`**
  Make a signed number without converting to floating point.
- **`fn object<const N: usize>(fields: [(&str, Value); N]) -> Self`**
  Make an object, with later duplicate names replacing earlier values.
- **`fn insert(&mut self, key: &str, value: Value) -> bool`**
  Insert or replace an object member; false when this value is not an object.
- **`fn get(&self, key: &str) -> Option<&Value>`**
  Look up an object member.
- **`fn as_object(&self) -> Option<&[(String, Value)]>`**
  Borrow an object member list.
- **`fn as_array(&self) -> Option<&[Value]>`**
  Borrow an array.
- **`fn as_str(&self) -> Option<&str>`**
  Borrow a string.
- **`fn as_bool(&self) -> Option<bool>`**
  Read a boolean.
- **`fn as_u64(&self) -> Option<u64>`**
  Read an unsigned integer token.
- **`fn as_i64(&self) -> Option<i64>`**
  Read a signed integer token.

- **`struct Limits`**
  Bounds applied before and during decoding or encoding.
  - `bytes: usize` -- Maximum encoded byte count.
  - `depth: usize` -- Maximum nested arrays/objects; the root value starts at depth zero.
  - `values: usize` -- Maximum total values, including the root.
  - `string_bytes: usize` -- Maximum decoded bytes in any string, including member names.
  - `members: usize` -- Maximum members/elements in any container.

- **`struct Error`**
  Parse or serialization failure with the byte offset where it was found.
  - `offset: usize` -- Zero-based byte offset (zero for serialization limits).
  - `kind: &'static str` -- Stable machine-readable failure category.

- **`fn parse(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Value, Error>`**
  Parse a complete UTF-8 JSON value using default limits.

- **`fn parse_with_limits(bytes: &[u8], limits: Limits) -> Result<Value, Error>`**
  Parse a complete UTF-8 JSON value. Duplicate object keys and lone surrogates are errors.

- **`fn stringify(value: &Value) -> Result<String, Error>`**
  Encode JSON without literal newlines, enforcing the default bounds.

- **`fn stringify_with_limits(value: &Value, limits: Limits) -> Result<String, Error>`**
  Encode bounded JSON. Invalid duplicate keys in manually built objects are rejected.

### `agent_core/src/rpc.rs`

Source: [`lib/agent_core/src/rpc.rs`](../lib/agent_core/src/rpc.rs)

```text
Versioned newline JSON-RPC plus MCP adapters. No I/O: transports supply complete lines.
New shared implementation, kept independent from OS streams and browser MessagePorts.
```

```rust
/// Application protocol revision carried by JSON control responses.
JSON_VERSION: &str = "1"
/// Current MCP revision, selected per request through params._meta.
MCP_VERSION: &str = "2026-07-28"
/// Explicitly supported initialization-based MCP revision.
MCP_LEGACY_VERSION: &str = "2025-11-25"
```

- **`enum Protocol`**
  One transport chooses its protocol explicitly; there is no guessing from requests.
  - `Json` -- jfguiLib JSON-RPC control protocol version 1.
  - `Mcp` -- MCP with current per-request metadata or the supported legacy handshake.

- **`fn requested_protocol(line: &[u8]) -> Option<Protocol>`**
  Select a connection's protocol from its first valid JSON-RPC envelope. An explicit
  modern MCP version or legacy initialization selects MCP; other requests select JSON v1.
  Malformed envelopes return None so transports can report them without locking the session.

- **`struct RpcSession`**
  Per-connection protocol framing state; application state stays in the Host.

**`impl RpcSession`**

- **`fn requires_persistent_session(&self) -> bool`**
  Whether initialization-based MCP negotiation must survive between requests.
  Stateless JSON/current-MCP browser sessions may be evicted when an inactive cache fills.
- **`fn new(protocol: Protocol, server_name: &str, server_version: &str) -> Self`**
  Create a protocol adapter without activating application control.
- **`fn handle( &mut self, dispatcher: &Dispatcher, host: &mut impl Host, line: &[u8], ) -> Option<String>`**
  Handle one UTF-8 JSON line, returning a newline-terminated response or no notification reply.
  This method never logs or writes standard streams. Use one session per connection.

- **`fn control( dispatcher: &Dispatcher, host: &mut impl Host, method: &str, params: &Value, ) -> Result<Value, ActionError>`**
  Execute the version-independent methods used by JSON and the MCP tool catalog.

- **`fn base64(bytes: &[u8]) -> String`**
  Base64-encode a separately requested resource payload.

### `agent_core/src/schema.rs`

Source: [`lib/agent_core/src/schema.rs`](../lib/agent_core/src/schema.rs)

```text
Statically registered input/output schemas and runtime validation. New shared code.
```

- **`enum Schema`**
  Supported JSON Schema subset, represented without parsing schema text at runtime.
  - `Any` -- Any bounded JSON value.
  - `Null` -- Null only.
  - `Boolean` -- Boolean only.
  - `Number` -- Any syntactically valid JSON number.
  - `Integer { min: i64, max: i64, }` -- Signed integer with inclusive bounds.
  - `Unsigned { min: u64, max: u64, }` -- Unsigned integer with inclusive bounds, retaining all 64 bits.
  - `String { min: usize, max: usize, choices: &'static [&'static str], }` -- Unicode string, with lengths in Unicode scalar values and optional allowed values.
  - `Array { items: &'static Schema, min: usize, max: usize, }` -- Array whose items all follow one schema.
  - `Object { properties: &'static [Property], additional: bool, }` -- Object with named properties and an explicit policy for unknown names.
  - `OneOf(&'static [Schema])` -- Exactly one member schema must match.

- **`struct Property`**
  Named member of an object schema.
  - `name: &'static str` -- Stable JSON member name.
  - `schema: &'static Schema` -- Member validation contract.
  - `required: bool` -- Whether omission is an error.

- **`static EMPTY_OBJECT: Schema`**
  Empty object accepted by parameterless actions.

- **`static OBJECT: Schema`**
  General object, for extensible host results.

- **`static ANY: Schema`**
  Unconstrained bounded value.

- **`static BOOLEAN: Schema`**
  Boolean value.

- **`static U64: Schema`**
  A full-width unsigned integer.

- **`static STRING: Schema`**
  Typical short string.

- **`struct Violation`**
  Validation error with a JSON Pointer to the invalid field.
  - `path: String` -- JSON Pointer; empty means the root value.
  - `reason: &'static str` -- Stable error category suitable for automated correction.

**`impl Schema`**

- **`fn validate(&self, value: &Value) -> Result<(), Violation>`**
  Validate a value; unknown properties and missing required members are checked.
- **`fn to_json(&self) -> Value`**
  Emit a JSON Schema 2020-12 compatible representation for discovery.

### `agent_core/src/task.rs`

Source: [`lib/agent_core/src/task.rs`](../lib/agent_core/src/task.rs)

```text
Bounded cooperative tasks for long native/browser operations. New shared implementation.
```

- **`enum Status`**
  State of a cooperative host operation.
  - `Pending` -- Queued but not started.
  - `Running` -- Executing between interruption points.
  - `Cancelling` -- Cancellation was requested; a host checkpoint still has to acknowledge it.
  - `Complete` -- Finished successfully.
  - `Failed` -- Finished with an action error.
  - `Cancelled` -- Cancellation was acknowledged at an interruption point.

**`impl Status`**

- **`fn as_str(self) -> &'static str`**
  Stable status spelling.
- **`fn terminal(self) -> bool`**
  Whether no more computation should be performed.

- **`struct Task`**
  One task, isolated inside its app/connection owner.
  - `id: u64` -- Monotonically allocated identifier; never reused by a table.
  - `action: String` -- Semantic action that created the task.
  - `status: Status` -- Current lifecycle state.
  - `progress: Option<u16>` -- Progress in thousandths, when available.
  - `message: String` -- Short host-provided progress description.
  - `cancellable: bool` -- Whether cancellation is supported at host interruption points.
  - `result: Option<ActionResult>` -- Completed result, available only in the Complete state.
  - `error: Option<ActionError>` -- Terminal failure or cancellation diagnosis.

**`impl Task`**

- **`fn to_json(&self) -> Value`**
  Compact poll response. Binary resources remain links inside action results.

- **`struct TaskTable`**
  Bounded in-memory task owner; a host drives work by calling checkpoint/update/finish.

**`impl TaskTable`**

- **`fn new(capacity: usize) -> Self`**
  Create a table; capacity is clamped to 1..=1024 tasks.
- **`fn create(&mut self, action: &str, cancellable: bool) -> Result<u64, ActionError>`**
  Allocate a pending task. Completed tasks are retained until explicitly forgotten.
- **`fn get(&self, id: u64) -> Result<&Task, ActionError>`**
  Inspect one task, without allowing external callers to mutate its state directly.
- **`fn request_cancel(&mut self, id: u64) -> Result<&Task, ActionError>`**
  Request cancellation; completion is reported only when the host acknowledges it.
- **`fn checkpoint(&mut self, id: u64) -> Result<bool, ActionError>`**
  Enter a safe interruption point. False means cancelled/finished; do no more work.
- **`fn update( &mut self, id: u64, progress: Option<u16>, message: &str, ) -> Result<(), ActionError>`**
  Update progress; host work must still call checkpoint before each interruptible chunk.
- **`fn finish(&mut self, id: u64, result: ActionResult) -> Result<(), ActionError>`**
  Finish a task. A pending cancellation must first be resolved at a checkpoint.
- **`fn fail(&mut self, id: u64, error: ActionError) -> Result<(), ActionError>`**
  Fail a pending/running task with structured diagnostic details.
- **`fn forget(&mut self, id: u64) -> Result<(), ActionError>`**
  Remove only a terminal task, retaining monotonic IDs to prevent accidental reuse.
- **`fn list(&self, after: u64, limit: usize) -> Result<Value, ActionError>`**
  One ID-ordered page of task metadata.

```rust
LINE_LIMIT: usize = 1_048_576
```

**`impl Framer`**

- **`fn feed(&mut self, bytes: &[u8], receive: &mut impl FnMut(Record))`**

## gfx

*surfaces, the draw context, blend kernels, paths, strokes, masks, gradients* -- crate root [`lib/gfx/lib.rs`](../lib/gfx/lib.rs)

```text
gfx -- surfaces and the draw context (clip, origin, colour), fills, blits  [src/gfx/surface.s]

  Surface::new(w, h, fmt)            zeroed pixels, stride 64-byte aligned (sides 0..65536)
  Dc::new(w, h) / dc.begin(&sf) / dc.end()      the draw context of a target; begin saves
                                     clip, origin and opacity and resets them for another
                                     target, end restores them (gfx_begin / gfx_end)
  dc: set_color(argb), set_opacity(op), clip_push(x, y, w, h) / clip_pop(), origin(x, y),
      translate(dx, dy), fill / clear / hline / vline / frame / blit / blit_scaled (sf, ...)
  path (Path, 24.8 and float API), raster (Raster), stroke (Path::stroke), shapes (Shapes),
  blend (row kernels), straight (straight-alpha rows), blendmode (paint.net blend modes),
  tmask (tiled masks), flood (flood fill), gradient, pathdata (SVG path data), dash

Colours are straight 0xAARRGGBB; Dc keeps the premultiplied form for the kernels.  The
surface is passed to every drawing call: Dc holds no pointer to it.
```

### `gfx` crate root

Re-exports: `rt::fx::div255`

```rust
FMT_XRGB: u8 = 0  // 0x??RRGGBB, alpha byte ignored (window framebuffer)
FMT_PARGB: u8 = 1  // premultiplied 0xAARRGGBB (images, layers)
FMT_A8: u8 = 2  // 8-bit coverage / mask
FMT_ARGB: u8 = 3  // straight 0xAARRGGBB = bytes B, G, R, A
SFF_OWNED: u8 = 1
SFF_OPAQUE: u8 = 2  // hint: every alpha is 255
FX_ONE: i32 = 256  // 24.8 fixed point: 1 pixel
RULE_NONZERO: u32 = 0
RULE_EVENODD: u32 = 1
JOIN_MITER: u32 = 0  // stroke style = join | cap << 4
JOIN_ROUND: u32 = 1
JOIN_BEVEL: u32 = 2
CAP_BUTT: u32 = 0
CAP_ROUND: u32 = 1
CAP_SQUARE: u32 = 2
ERR_FAIL: i32 = -1
ERR_NOMEM: i32 = -8
ERR_LIMIT: i32 = -15
```

- **`struct Surface`**
  pixels of one image: rows of `stride` bytes (4 per pixel, A8: 1)
  - `px: Vec<u8>`
  - `w: i32`
  - `h: i32`
  - `stride: i32`
  - `fmt: u8`
  - `flags: u8`
  - `opac: u8` -- layer opacity 0..255
  - `blend: u8` -- layer blend mode

**`impl Surface`**

- **`fn new(w: i32, h: i32, fmt: u8) -> Surface`**
  zeroed pixels, stride 64-byte aligned; sides outside 0..65536 give an empty surface
  (surf_alloc fails). Byte lengths beyond the target's addressable allocation size
  also give an empty surface, before allocating pixels.
- **`fn new_a8_packed(w: i32, h: i32) -> Surface`**
  a tightly packed A8 surface (glyph masks: stride = w)
- **`fn resize(&mut self, w: i32, h: i32)`**
  new size, same format; the contents are NOT kept  [surf_resize]
- **`fn bpp(&self) -> usize`**

- **`struct Dc`**
  the draw context of a target: clip (x0, y0, x1, y1 exclusive, in target pixels), origin,
  colour (straight and premultiplied), opacity
  - `clip: [i32; 4]`
  - `ox: i32`
  - `oy: i32`
  - `w: i32`
  - `h: i32`
  - `color: u32`
  - `opac: u32`
  - `pcol: [u32; 4]` -- premultiplied B, G, R, A
  - `pcol32: u32`
  - `ia: u32` -- 255 - premultiplied alpha

**`impl Dc`**

- **`fn new(w: i32, h: i32) -> Dc`**
  a context for a w x h target: clip = the whole target, colour opaque black  [gfx_init]
- **`fn begin(&mut self, sf: &Surface)`**
  draw into another target: clip, origin and opacity of the current one are saved; the
  clip becomes the whole new target, the origin 0  [gfx_begin]
- **`fn end(&mut self)`**
  back to the previous target with its clip, origin and opacity  [gfx_end]
- **`fn set_color(&mut self, argb: u32)`**

- **`fn get_color(&self) -> u32`**
- **`fn set_opacity(&mut self, op: u32)`**
  global opacity 0..255 (multiplies every alpha)
- **`fn origin(&mut self, x: i32, y: i32)`**
- **`fn translate(&mut self, dx: i32, dy: i32)`**
- **`fn clip_push(&mut self, x: i32, y: i32, w: i32, h: i32)`**
  save the clip and intersect it with the rectangle (origin-relative)
- **`fn clip_pop(&mut self)`**
- **`fn clip_rect(&self, x: i32, y: i32, w: i32, h: i32) -> Option<[i32; 4]>`**
  origin-relative rectangle -> clipped target rectangle (x0, y0, x1, y1), or None
- **`fn fill(&self, sf: &mut Surface, x: i32, y: i32, w: i32, h: i32)`**

- **`fn clear(&self, sf: &mut Surface, argb: u32)`**

- **`fn hline(&self, sf: &mut Surface, x: i32, y: i32, w: i32)`**

### `gfx/blend.rs`

Source: [`lib/gfx/blend.rs`](../lib/gfx/blend.rs)

```text
blend -- pixel row kernels (premultiplied 0xAARRGGBB, bytes B G R A)   [src/gfx/blend.s, raster.s]

  fill_solid(row, colour)          store colour into every pixel
  fill_over(row, pcol, ia)         blend the premultiplied colour over every pixel
  mask_over(row, mask, pcol)       the colour through an 8-bit coverage row
  blit_over(dst, src, opacity)     premultiplied src over dst (x opacity 0..255)
  a8_over(dst, cov, alpha)         8-bit target: c = cov * alpha / 255, dst = c + dst * (255 - c) / 255

"over": dst = src + dst * (255 - src_alpha) / 255 with exact rounding (div255) and the
saturation of the SSE2 kernels (packuswb).  fill_over, mask_over and a8_over are SIMD
kernels (simd::Kernel; their fast paths are exact shortcuts, so SSE2 and AVX2 agree);
blit_over is the assembly's SSE2 loop on pixel pairs, because its pair skip (both
transparent: dst kept) is visible for invalid premultiplied input.
```

- **`fn fill_solid(row: &mut [u8], c: u32)`**
  k_fill_solid: store one colour into every pixel of the row

- **`fn fill_over(row: &mut [u8], pcol: &[u32; 4], ia: u32)`**
  k_fill_over: blend the premultiplied colour over every pixel

- **`fn mask_over(row: &mut [u8], mask: &[u8], pcol: &[u32; 4])`**
  k_mask_over: blend the colour through an 8-bit coverage row (mask.len() pixels)

- **`fn blit_over(dst: &mut [u8], src: &[u8], op: u32)`**
  k_blit_over: premultiplied src over dst, scaled by opacity (pixels = min of both lengths)

- **`fn a8_over(dst: &mut [u8], cov: &[u8], alpha: u32)`**
  k_a8_over: 8-bit coverage target, c = cov \* alpha / 255, dst = c + dst \* (255 - c) / 255

### `gfx/blendmode.rs`

Source: [`lib/gfx/blendmode.rs`](../lib/gfx/blendmode.rs)

```text
blendmode -- paint.net layer blend modes on straight-alpha BGRA rows (FMT_ARGB)  [src/gfx/blendmode.s]

  bm_row(dst, lhs, rhs, mode_op)              layer rhs over layer lhs
  bm_cov_row(dst, lhs, rhs, cov, mode_op)     same, rhs alpha x A8 coverage (None: full)
  bm_solid_row(dst, lhs, argb, cov, mode_op)  a solid colour through the coverage
  *_inplace: dst = lhs;   bm_px: one pixel

mode_op = mode | opacity << 8 (BM_*, unknown modes are BM_NORMAL).  lhs = bottom row,
rhs = top row.  The arithmetic is paint.net's UserBlendOps, all integers 0..255:

  INT_SCALE(a, b) = round(a*b / 255)
  rhsA = INT_SCALE(INT_SCALE(rhs.A, opacity), cov);  y = INT_SCALE(lhs.A, 255 - rhsA)
  x = INT_SCALE(lhs.A, rhsA);  z = rhsA - x;  totalA = y + rhsA (0 -> pixel 0)
  c = floor((lhs.c*y + rhs.c*z + F(lhs.c, rhs.c)*x) / totalA),  A = totalA

BM_OVERWRITE (tools): dst = round((lhs*(255 - m) + rhs*m) / 255) on all four channels,
m = INT_SCALE(cov, opacity).  Pixels with coverage 0 keep lhs.  bm_px is the scalar
reference; the row kernels are the assembly's SSE2 / AVX2 code (simd::Kernel), which
computes exactly this per pixel: its fast paths are exact shortcuts and its f32 divisions
are exact floor divisions in this range (numerators <= 65025, divisors 1..255).
```

```rust
BM_NORMAL: u32 = 0
BM_MULTIPLY: u32 = 1
BM_ADDITIVE: u32 = 2
BM_COLORBURN: u32 = 3
BM_COLORDODGE: u32 = 4
BM_REFLECT: u32 = 5
BM_GLOW: u32 = 6
BM_OVERLAY: u32 = 7
BM_DIFFERENCE: u32 = 8
BM_NEGATION: u32 = 9
BM_LIGHTEN: u32 = 10
BM_DARKEN: u32 = 11
BM_SCREEN: u32 = 12
BM_XOR: u32 = 13
BM_OVERWRITE: u32 = 14
BM_COUNT: u32 = 15
BM_OPAC_SHIFT: u32 = 8
```

- **`fn bm_px(l: u32, r: u32, cov: u32, mode_op: u32) -> u32`**
  one pixel: rhs over lhs with coverage cov (0..255) and mode_op = mode | opacity &lt;&lt; 8

- **`fn bm_row(dst: &mut [u8], lhs: &[u8], rhs: &[u8], mode_op: u32)`**
  k_bm_row: dst = rhs over lhs (pixels = shortest row)

- **`fn bm_row_inplace(lhs: &mut [u8], rhs: &[u8], mode_op: u32)`**

- **`fn bm_cov_row(dst: &mut [u8], lhs: &[u8], rhs: &[u8], cov: Option<&[u8]>, n: usize, mode_op: u32)`**
  k_bm_cov_row: rhs alpha scaled by the coverage row (None: full coverage); n pixels

- **`fn bm_cov_row_inplace(lhs: &mut [u8], rhs: &[u8], cov: Option<&[u8]>, n: usize, mode_op: u32)`**

- **`fn bm_solid_row(dst: &mut [u8], lhs: &[u8], argb: u32, cov: Option<&[u8]>, n: usize, mode_op: u32)`**
  k_bm_solid_row: the straight colour argb through the coverage row (None: full); n pixels

- **`fn bm_solid_row_inplace(lhs: &mut [u8], argb: u32, cov: Option<&[u8]>, n: usize, mode_op: u32)`**

### `gfx/dash.rs`

Source: [`lib/gfx/dash.rs`](../lib/gfx/dash.rs)

```text
dash -- dashed strokes with caps (flat / round / square / arrow / filled arrow) as a path:
the path's flattened centre lines are replaced by the outline of their stroke, closed
contours to fill with RULE_NONZERO                                          [src/gfx/dash.s]

  dash_pattern(style) -> &[f64]         DASH_* (DASH_SOLID / unknown: empty)
  dash_stroke(path, &DStyle) -> 0 / ERR_LIMIT

Geometry (f64): every open subpath (or dash piece) becomes ONE contour: the +n side
forward with joins, the end cap, the -n side backward, the start cap (n = (dy, -dx)).
Outer joins are mitred (limit miter, else bevelled), round or bevelled; inner joins pivot
through the vertex (the non-zero fill absorbs the overlap).  A closed subpath becomes two
contours (outer and inner offset loop, oriented so that the ring winds +1).  Every open
contour is oriented to a positive area, so overlapping pieces unite under non-zero.
Dashes: the pattern (entries x width) runs along each subpath from its start (phase
offset x width), a closed subpath's pattern runs once around; the ends of the pieces get
dcap except at the path's own start / end (cap0 / cap1).  A piece of length 0 is a dot.
Arrow caps (open subpaths): head length L = 3 w + 4, half width L / 2 at the end point,
pointing along the last segment; the open arrow is the polyline (base+, tip, base-)
stroked with the line's width and join; the filled arrow a triangle, and the line ends
L / 2 before the tip.  Round arcs use steps of 2 sqrt(0.1 / hw) rad (<= 45 degrees).
f64 operations in the order of the assembly, so the outline is bit-identical.
```

```rust
LCAP_FLAT: u32 = 0  // = CAP_BUTT
LCAP_ROUND: u32 = 1
LCAP_SQUARE: u32 = 2
LCAP_ARROW: u32 = 3  // open arrow head (two strokes of the line width)
LCAP_ARROW_FILLED: u32 = 4  // filled triangle
LCAP_COUNT: u32 = 5
DASH_SOLID: u32 = 0
DASH_DASH: u32 = 1  // 3 1
DASH_DOT: u32 = 2  // 1 1
DASH_DASHDOT: u32 = 3  // 3 1 1 1
DASH_DASHDOTDOT: u32 = 4  // 3 1 1 1 1 1
DASH_COUNT: u32 = 5
DASH_MAXPAT: usize = 32  // entries of a custom pattern
```

- **`struct DStyle<'a>`**
  a stroke style (the DSTYLE record)
  - `width: f64` -- line width (px, >= 0.05)
  - `pat: &'a [f64]` -- on / off lengths in units of the width (empty = solid; odd counts twice)
  - `join: u32` -- JOIN_\*
  - `cap0: u32` -- LCAP_\* at the start of every open subpath
  - `cap1: u32` -- LCAP_\* at its end
  - `dcap: u32` -- LCAP_FLAT / ROUND / SQUARE at the inner ends of dashes
  - `offset: f64` -- dash phase (units of the width)
  - `miter: f64` -- miter limit (ratio of miter length to width; &lt; 1 = 4)

- **`fn dash_pattern(style: u32) -> &'static [f64]`**
  the predefined pattern of style DASH_\* (units of the width)  [dash_pattern]

- **`fn dash_stroke(path: &mut Path, st: &DStyle) -> i32`**
  replace the path by the outline of its stroke (see the file header)  [dash_stroke]

### `gfx/flood.rs`

Source: [`lib/gfx/flood.rs`](../lib/gfx/flood.rs)

```text
flood -- flood fill (magic wand / paint bucket region) into a tiled mask   [src/gfx/flood.s]

  flood_fill_mask(src, x, y, tolerance_pct, flags, out) -> 0 / ERR_FAIL

src: 32-bit surface (FMT_ARGB straight; the colour test works on the raw B, G, R, A
bytes); out: a TMask at least as large as src.  out is cleared, then every pixel that
matches the seed colour (x, y) and -- without FF_GLOBAL -- is 4-connected to the seed
through matching pixels becomes 255.  ERR_FAIL: bad surface, mask too small, seed outside
src (out is cleared when it is usable).

Colour test = paint.net's CheckColor, a = seed colour, b = candidate:
  sum = sum over c in R, G, B of floor((1 + (a.c - b.c)^2) * a.A / 256) + (a.A - b.A)^2
  tol = round(tolerance_pct * 255 / 100) (pct clamped to 0..100), match: sum <= tol*tol*4
As in the assembly: a match bitmap M (1 bit per pixel, rows of ceil(w / 64) u64) computed
per row on demand by a SIMD kernel (FF_MATCH4, integer arithmetic, so SSE2 and AVX2 give
the same bits); the contiguous fill is a scanline fill on the bitmaps with an explicit stack
of run starts, filled runs move from M to the fill bitmap F (word operations); finally
64 x 64 bit blocks become Empty / Full slots or tiles.
```

```rust
FF_GLOBAL: u32 = 1  // every matching pixel, not only the connected ones
```

- **`fn flood_fill_mask(src: &Surface, x: i32, y: i32, tolerance_pct: i32, flags: u32, out: &mut TMask) -> i32`**
  fill the region of the seed (x, y) into out  [flood_fill_mask]

### `gfx/gradient.rs`

Source: [`lib/gfx/gradient.rs`](../lib/gfx/gradient.rs)

```text
gradient -- paint.net style gradients (7 shapes x 3 repeat modes)       [src/gfx/gradient.s]

  grad_span(dst, x, y, g)         dst.len() / 4 straight ARGB pixels of row y, columns x ..
                                  (pixel centres x + i + 0.5, y + 0.5); with GF_ALPHA_MODE the
                                  existing pixels keep their colour and their alpha is
                                  multiplied by the ramp A.a -> B.a: a' = round(a * ramp / 255)
  grad_alpha_span(dst, x, y, g)   dst.len() bytes: the ramp A.a -> B.a
  grad_t_span(dst, x, y, g)       positions T (0 .. GT_ONE)

With d = end - start, L2 = d.d, v = p - start, dot = vx dx + vy dy, cross = vy dx - vx dy,
r = sqrt(vx vx + vy vy):  linear dot / L2, linear reflected |dot| / L2, diamond (|dot| +
|cross|) / L2, radial r / L, conical |atan2(cross, dot)| / pi, spirals frac(r / L +-
atan2 / 2pi).  atan2 is the polynomial of the assembly (Abramowitz-Stegun 4.4.49).  Repeat:
none clamps to [0, 1], wrap frac(t), reflect the triangle wave of period 2.  T = floor(t *
16384 + 0.5); every channel c = (A (16384 - T) + B T + bias) >> 14, bias 8192 or the 4 x 4
Bayer threshold (GF_DITHER).  Degenerate L2 (0 or not finite): T = 16384.  f64 operations in
the order of the assembly (no FMA), lane by lane in SIMD registers, so the results are
bit-identical.
```

```rust
GT_LINEAR: u8 = 0
GT_LINEAR_REFLECTED: u8 = 1
GT_DIAMOND: u8 = 2
GT_RADIAL: u8 = 3
GT_CONICAL: u8 = 4
GT_SPIRAL_CW: u8 = 5
GT_SPIRAL_CCW: u8 = 6
GT_COUNT: u8 = 7
GR_NONE: u8 = 0
GR_WRAP: u8 = 1
GR_REFLECT: u8 = 2
GF_ALPHA_MODE: u16 = 1
GF_DITHER: u16 = 2
GT_ONE: i32 = 16384
```

- **`struct Grad`**
  a gradient (the GRAD record): type GT_\*, repeat GR_\*, flags GF_\*, colours A (t = 0) and
  B (t = 1), start and end point
  - `typ: u8`
  - `repeat: u8`
  - `flags: u16`
  - `col_a: u32`
  - `col_b: u32`
  - `sx: f64`
  - `sy: f64`
  - `ex: f64`
  - `ey: f64`

- **`fn grad_span(dst: &mut [u8], x: i32, y: i32, g: &Grad)`**
  straight ARGB pixels of gradient g (GF_ALPHA_MODE: multiply the existing alphas)  [grad_span]

- **`fn grad_alpha_span(dst: &mut [u8], x: i32, y: i32, g: &Grad)`**
  the alpha ramp A.a -> B.a (transparency mode mask)  [grad_alpha_span]

- **`fn grad_t_span(dst: &mut [i32], x: i32, y: i32, g: &Grad)`**
  the positions T (0 .. GT_ONE)  [grad_t_span]

### `gfx/path.rs`

Source: [`lib/gfx/path.rs`](../lib/gfx/path.rs)

```text
path -- vector paths: build, flatten (tolerance 0.2 px), fill        [src/gfx/path.s]

Integer API in 24.8 fixed point relative to the draw origin (move_fx, line_fx, quad_fx,
cubic_fx, rect_fx, rrect_fx, ellipse_fx), float API in absolute target pixels (move_f,
line_f, quad_f, add_pt; used by the text engine).  Curves are flattened at once; fill()
hands the closed subpaths to the rasterizer, stroke() (stroke.rs) their outlines.
The origin is taken from the Dc at begin() (set_origin changes it).
```

- **`struct Path`**

**`impl Path`**

- **`fn set_tolerance(&mut self, tolerance: f32) -> f32`**
  Set curve flattening tolerance in target pixels and return the old value.
  Invalid values preserve the setting; 0.2 is the legacy default.
- **`fn begin(&mut self, dc: &Dc)`**
  path_begin: an empty path; ox, oy = the draw origin used by the integer API
- **`fn reset(&mut self)`**
  an empty path, origin kept (path_begin for the float API)
- **`fn set_origin(&mut self, ox: i32, oy: i32)`**
- **`fn is_empty(&self) -> bool`**
- **`fn pts_mut(&mut self) -> &mut [(f32, f32)]`**
  the flattened points of every subpath (target pixels), e.g. to shear them before a
  fill (the asm's g_pts / g_npts)  // paint-tools: Paint's italic text
- **`fn add_pt(&mut self, p: (f32, f32))`**
  path_add_pt: append to the open subpath (a new one starts at the current point
  if none is open); a duplicate of the previous point is skipped
- **`fn move_fx(&mut self, x: i32, y: i32)`**
- **`fn move_f(&mut self, x: f32, y: f32)`**
- **`fn line_fx(&mut self, x: i32, y: i32)`**
- **`fn line_f(&mut self, x: f32, y: f32)`**
- **`fn close(&mut self)`**
- **`fn quad_fx(&mut self, cx: i32, cy: i32, x: i32, y: i32)`**
  path_quad (24.8 fixed, origin-relative)
- **`fn quad_f(&mut self, cx: f32, cy: f32, x: f32, y: f32)`**
  path_quad_f: quadratic Bezier from the current point (float pixels)
- **`fn cubic_fx(&mut self, c1x: i32, c1y: i32, c2x: i32, c2y: i32, x: i32, y: i32)`**
  path_cubic (24.8 fixed, origin-relative)
- **`fn cubic_f(&mut self, p1: (f32, f32), p2: (f32, f32), p3: (f32, f32))`**
  a cubic Bezier from the current point in float pixels (the flattening of path_cubic)
- **`fn rect_fx(&mut self, x: i32, y: i32, w: i32, h: i32)`**
- **`fn rrect_fx(&mut self, x: i32, y: i32, w: i32, h: i32, r: i32)`**
  path_rrect: closed rounded rectangle, r clamped to min(w, h) / 2
- **`fn ellipse_fx(&mut self, cx: i32, cy: i32, rx: i32, ry: i32)`**
  path_ellipse: closed ellipse from four cubic arcs
- **`fn fill_lines(&self, ras: &mut Raster)`**
  the line list of path_fill: every subpath of 2+ points closed implicitly
- **`fn fill(&self, ras: &mut Raster, dc: &Dc, sf: &mut Surface, rule: u32)`**
  path_fill: every subpath closed implicitly; rule 0 non-zero, 1 even-odd

```text
Fractional UI panels: compose fill and border coverage once, avoiding a dark
halo from drawing two antialiased shapes on top of the same boundary pixel.
```

**`impl Shapes<'_>`**

- **`fn panel_fx(&mut self, x: i32, y: i32, w: i32, h: i32, r: i32, t: i32, fill: u32, stroke: u32)`**
  Rounded panel in 24.8 pixels, with an inside border. Fill and stroke are

### `gfx/pathdata.rs`

Source: [`lib/gfx/pathdata.rs`](../lib/gfx/pathdata.rs)

```text
pathdata -- path data: a growable list of absolute path commands, the SVG / XAML path
mini-language and its output into a Path                                 [src/gfx/pathdata.s]

  PData::new() / clear()             empty (rule non-zero, error cleared)
  move_to(x, y), line_to(x, y)       (an implicit move to the current point opens a subpath)
  cubic(&[f64; 6]), quad(&[f64; 4])  (a quadratic Bezier is stored as the cubic)
  close()
  arc(&[f64; 9])                     SVG elliptical arc {x1, y1, rx, ry, rotation (deg),
                                     large-arc 0/1, sweep 0/1, x2, y2} as up to 4 cubics
  rrect(&[f64; 5])                   closed rounded rectangle {x0, y0, x1, y1, r}
  parse(text) -> (0 / PDE_* / ERR_LIMIT, byte offset)   M L H V C S Q T A Z (upper case
                                     absolute, lower case relative), implicit repetition,
                                     "F0" / "F1" (XAML even-odd / non-zero)
  emit(path, Option<&Matrix>) -> 0 / ERR_FAIL      the commands into the path (curves flattened
                                     to 0.05 px), the matrix applied first
  bounds(Option<&Matrix>) -> Option<[f64; 4]>      of the transformed, finely flattened outline
  bounds_box(Option<&Matrix>) -> (any, [f64; 4])   the same as the assembly leaves it

Every builder call checks its coordinates (finite, |v| <= COORD_MAX) and the size
(MAX_SLOTS); the first failure is kept in err and later calls do nothing.  The commands
are slots like the assembly's (op, then f64 arguments), so the limits are the same.
```

```rust
PDO_MOVE: u64 = 1
PDO_LINE: u64 = 2
PDO_CUBIC: u64 = 3
PDO_CLOSE: u64 = 4
MAX_SLOTS: usize = 0x40_0000  // 4 Mi slots per PData
MAX_TEXT: usize = 0x100_0000  // parse input limit (16 MB)
COORD_MAX: f64 = 10_000_000.0  // |coordinate| limit of the builder
PDE_SYNTAX: i32 = -40  // unexpected character / missing command
PDE_NUMBER: i32 = -41  // a number was expected
PDE_RANGE: i32 = -42  // a number / coordinate out of range (NaN, > COORD_MAX)
```

- **`struct PData`**
  path data (the PDATA record)
  - `rule: u32`
  - `err: i32`
  - `ncmd: u32`

**`impl PData`**

- **`fn new() -> PData`**
- **`fn clear(&mut self)`**
  no commands, no error  [pd_reset]
- **`fn slot_count(&self) -> usize`**
  the slots used (op words and f64 arguments)
- **`fn current(&self) -> (f64, f64)`**
  the current point
- **`fn move_to(&mut self, x: f64, y: f64)`**
  start a subpath  [pd_move]
- **`fn line_to(&mut self, x: f64, y: f64)`**
  straight segment  [pd_line]
- **`fn cubic(&mut self, p: &[f64; 6])`**
  cubic Bezier from the current point: c1, c2, end  [pd_cubic]
- **`fn quad(&mut self, q: &[f64; 4])`**
  quadratic Bezier (control, end) as the equivalent cubic  [pd_quad]
- **`fn close(&mut self)`**
  close the open subpath; the current point returns to its start  [pd_close]
- **`fn arc(&mut self, p: &[f64; 9])`**
  elliptical arc {x1, y1, rx, ry, phi (deg), large, sweep, x2, y2} (SVG 1.1 F.6.5 /
  F.6.6: radii scaled up when too small, 0 radius = line)  [pd_arc]
- **`fn rrect(&mut self, p: &[f64; 5])`**
  closed rounded rectangle {x0, y0, x1, y1, r} (corners in any order, r clamped to 0 ..
  half the shorter side), clockwise on screen from the top edge  [pd_rrect]
- **`fn parse(&mut self, text: &[u8]) -> (i32, usize)`**
  append SVG / XAML path data: (0 or the error, byte offset of the error or len)  [pd_parse]
- **`fn emit(&self, path: &mut Path, m: Option<&Matrix>) -> i32`**
  append the commands to the path (matrix applied first)  [pd_emit]
- **`fn bounds(&self, m: Option<&Matrix>) -> Option<[f64; 4]>`**
  {min x, min y, max x, max y} of the (transformed, finely flattened) outline  [pd_bounds]
- **`fn bounds_box(&self, m: Option<&Matrix>) -> (bool, [f64; 4])`**
  pd_bounds as the assembly leaves its output: (any point, box); without a point the box
  is {1e300, 1e300, -1e300, -1e300}

### `gfx/raster.rs`

Source: [`lib/gfx/raster.rs`](../lib/gfx/raster.rs)

```text
raster -- anti-aliased scanline rasterizer (signed-area accumulation)  [src/gfx/raster.s]

Every line deposits its signed area (integers in 1/65536 of a pixel) into an
accumulation buffer; the running sum along a row is the coverage.  Rows go in bands
of 64.  The float operations and their order are those of the assembly, so the
coverage bytes are identical (see raster.s for the clip-determinism argument).
```

- **`struct Raster`**

**`impl Raster`**

- **`fn lines_begin(&mut self)`**
- **`fn emit_line(&mut self, x0: f32, y0: f32, x1: f32, y1: f32)`**
- **`fn has_lines(&self) -> bool`**
- **`fn lbox(&self) -> [f32; 4]`**
  the bounding box of the line list (f32 minx, miny, maxx, maxy)
- **`fn fill(&mut self, dc: &Dc, sf: &mut Surface, rule: u32)`**
  ras_fill: rasterize the line list with the current colour into the target, clipped
- **`fn fill_rows(&mut self, clip: [i32; 4], rule: u32, emit: &mut dyn FnMut(i32, i32, &[u8]))`**
  rasterize the line list inside clip (x0, y0, x1, y1 exclusive): emit(y, x, coverage)
  for every touched row part, rows top to bottom (the coverage of a pixel does not
  depend on the clip)

```text
Integer pixels (UI): fill_rrect(x, y, w, h, r), stroke_rrect(x, y, w, h, r, t) -- only
```

### `gfx/straight.rs`

Source: [`lib/gfx/straight.rs`](../lib/gfx/straight.rs)

```text
straight -- row kernels on straight (not premultiplied) 0xAARRGGBB pixels  [src/gfx/straight.s]

  premul_row(dst, src)            straight -> premultiplied: c' = round(c * a / 255)
  unpremul_row(dst, src)          premultiplied -> straight, the rounding of png.s:
                                  c = min(255, (c * ceil(255 * 2^24 / a) + 2^23) >> 24)
  lerp_mask_row(dst, a, b, m)     dst = round((a * (255 - m) + b * m) / 255), 4 channels
  lerp_mask_into_a / _into_b      the same with dst = a / dst = b
  erase_row(dst, cov, strength)   alpha = round(alpha * (255 - e) / 255), e = round(cov *
                                  strength / 255); colour kept
  view_row(dst, src, x0, y0, cfg)     straight src over a checkerboard -> XRGB row
  view_row_pm(dst, src, x0, y0, cfg)  premultiplied src over a checkerboard -> XRGB row
  *_px: the same for one pixel (u32), for callers whose rows alias

Rows hold 4 bytes per pixel (B, G, R, A); a kernel works on as many pixels as its
shortest row has.  The assembly's fast paths (opaque / transparent groups) are exact
shortcuts of these per-pixel formulas, so the results are the same bytes.
```

- **`struct ViewCfg`**
  the checkerboard of view_row: cell size (>= 1), colour of the even cells (cx + cy) and
  of the odd ones (alpha ignored)
  - `cell: i32`
  - `c0: u32`
  - `c1: u32`

- **`fn premul_px(p: u32) -> u32`**

- **`fn unpremul_px(p: u32) -> u32`**

- **`fn lerp_px(a: u32, b: u32, m: u32) -> u32`**

- **`fn erase_px(p: u32, cov: u32, strength: u32) -> u32`**

- **`fn view_bg(x: i32, y: i32, cfg: &ViewCfg) -> u32`**
  the opaque checkerboard colour of pixel (x, y)

- **`fn view_mix(s: u32, bg: u32) -> u32`**
  straight s over bg: round((s \* a + bg \* (255 - a)) / 255), alpha 255

- **`fn view_mix_pm(s: u32, bg: u32) -> u32`**
  premultiplied s over bg: s + round(bg \* (255 - a) / 255) (saturated), alpha 255

- **`fn premul_row(dst: &mut [u8], src: &[u8])`**
  k_premul_row

- **`fn premul_inplace(buf: &mut [u8])`**

- **`fn unpremul_row(dst: &mut [u8], src: &[u8])`**
  k_unpremul_row

- **`fn unpremul_inplace(buf: &mut [u8])`**

- **`fn lerp_mask_row(dst: &mut [u8], a: &[u8], b: &[u8], m: &[u8])`**
  k_lerp_mask_row: pixels = m.len() (bounded by the rows)

- **`fn lerp_mask_into_a(a: &mut [u8], b: &[u8], m: &[u8])`**
  k_lerp_mask_row with dst = a

- **`fn lerp_mask_into_b(a: &[u8], b: &mut [u8], m: &[u8])`**
  k_lerp_mask_row with dst = b

- **`fn erase_row(dst: &mut [u8], cov: &[u8], strength: u32)`**
  k_erase_row: pixels = cov.len() (bounded by the row)

- **`fn view_row(dst: &mut [u8], src: &[u8], x0: i32, y0: i32, cfg: &ViewCfg)`**
  k_view_row: straight src over the checkerboard; (x0, y0) = checkerboard coordinates of
  the first pixel

- **`fn view_row_pm(dst: &mut [u8], src: &[u8], x0: i32, y0: i32, cfg: &ViewCfg)`**
  k_view_row_pm: premultiplied src over the checkerboard

### `gfx/stroke.rs`

Source: [`lib/gfx/stroke.rs`](../lib/gfx/stroke.rs)

```text
stroke -- stroke outlines of a path                                     [src/gfx/stroke.s]

  path.stroke(ras, dc, sf, width, style)   width 24.8 fixed pixels; style = join | cap << 4
                                          (JOIN_MITER (limit 4, else bevel) / ROUND / BEVEL,
                                          CAP_BUTT / ROUND / SQUARE)
  path.stroke_lines(ras, width, style)     only the outline line list (for other targets)

Every flattened subpath becomes closed contours (outer side forward, inner side
backward, with joins and caps) that are filled with the non-zero rule.  Inner joins
pivot through the vertex; the non-zero fill absorbs the overlap.  Offsets use
n = (dy, -dx) * half_width, so with y pointing down a positive cross product din x dout
means the +n side is the outer side of the turn.  The float operations follow the
assembly one by one (0 - x instead of -x, comparisons with its NaN behaviour).
```

**`impl Path`**

- **`fn stroke_lines(&self, ras: &mut Raster, width: i32, style: u32)`**
  the outline lines of path_stroke (ras.lines_begin + the contours); the caller fills
  them with the non-zero rule
- **`fn stroke(&self, ras: &mut Raster, dc: &Dc, sf: &mut Surface, width: i32, style: u32)`**
  path_stroke: the outline filled (non-zero) with the current colour

### `gfx/tmask.rs`

Source: [`lib/gfx/tmask.rs`](../lib/gfx/tmask.rs)

```text
tmask -- sparse tiled A8 masks                                          [src/gfx/tmask.s]

  TMask::new(w, h) -> Option          1 <= w, h <= 65535, every pixel 0
  clear(value)                        every pixel = value (0 / 255 need no memory)
  get(x, y) -> u8                     coverage of one pixel (0 outside the mask)
  span(x, y, maxn) -> (Span, n)       run of n (1..maxn) pixels from (x, y): Empty (all 0),
                                      Full (all 255) or Bytes inside one tile (n <= 64);
                                      sentinel runs span tiles; outside -> Empty
  row(x, y, n, buf) -> (&[u8], bool)  the n bytes of (x .. x+n-1, y): inside a tile when
                                      possible, else buf is filled (true = buf returned)
  row_w(x, y) -> Option<&mut [u8]>    writable bytes from (x, y) to the end of its tile row
                                      (at most w - x); materialises the tile
  tile_w(tx, ty) -> Option<&mut [u8]> writable 64 x 64 tile;   tile_set(tx, ty, full)
  fill_rect(x0, y0, x1, y1, value)    (x1, y1 exclusive, clipped)
  combine(src, op) / combine_block(tx, ty, Block, op) / copy(src) / clone()
  invert(), threshold(level), bbox() -> Option<[i32; 4]>, optimize(), count_tiles()
  load_a8(src, stride), store_a8(dst, stride)
  raster_path(path, ras, rule, op), raster_polygon(path, ras, pts, rule, op)
  trace(cb) -> i32                    outlines of the pixels >= 128 as closed polylines in
                                      pixel-edge coordinates, cb(points) -> 0 = go on

Tiles are 64 x 64 bytes (row stride 64).  A slot holds Empty, Full or a tile; tiles come
from a pool (count_tiles reports the ones in use, as the assembly does).  Pixels of edge
tiles outside w x h are ignored everywhere.  Ops return 0 / ERR_* like the assembly.
```

```rust
TM_TILE: usize = 64
TM_MAX_SIDE: u32 = 65535
TM_REPLACE: u32 = 0  // src
TM_ADD: u32 = 1  // max(dst, src)
TM_SUB: u32 = 2  // round(dst * (255 - src) / 255)
TM_INTERSECT: u32 = 3  // round(dst * src / 255)
TM_XOR: u32 = 4  // |dst - src|
TM_OP_COUNT: u32 = 5
TM_TRACE_LEVEL: u8 = 128
```

- **`enum Span<'a>`**
  a run of tm_span
  - `Empty`
  - `Full`
  - `Bytes(&'a [u8])`

- **`enum Block<'a>`**
  a 64 x 64 source block of combine_block
  - `Empty`
  - `Full`
  - `Data(&'a [u8], usize)` -- bytes, row stride (64 readable bytes in each of 64 rows)

- **`struct TMask`**
  - `w: u32`
  - `h: u32`

**`impl TMask`**

- **`fn new(w: u32, h: u32) -> Option<TMask>`**
  a mask of w x h pixels, every pixel 0 (None: bad size)  [tm_alloc]
- **`fn tiles_x(&self) -> u32`**
- **`fn tiles_y(&self) -> u32`**
- **`fn clear(&mut self, value: u8) -> i32`**
  every pixel = value  [tm_clear]
- **`fn get(&self, x: i32, y: i32) -> u8`**
  coverage of one pixel (0 outside)  [tm_get]
- **`fn span(&self, x: i32, y: i32, maxn: i32) -> (Span<'_>, i32)`**
  a run of n (1..maxn) pixels from (x, y)  [tm_span]
- **`fn row<'a>(&'a self, x: i32, y: i32, n: i32, buf: &'a mut [u8]) -> (&'a [u8], bool)`**
  the n bytes of (x .. x+n-1, y): (bytes, true when they are buf)  [tm_row]
- **`fn row_w(&mut self, x: i32, y: i32) -> Option<&mut [u8]>`**
  writable bytes from (x, y) to the end of its tile row (at most w - x)  [tm_row_w]
- **`fn tile_w(&mut self, tx: u32, ty: u32) -> Option<&mut [u8]>`**
  writable 64 x 64 tile (materialised with its content)  [tm_tile_w]
- **`fn tile_set(&mut self, tx: u32, ty: u32, full: bool)`**
  tile (tx, ty) := all 0 / all 255  [tm_tile_set]
- **`fn fill_rect(&mut self, x0: i32, y0: i32, x1: i32, y1: i32, value: u8) -> i32`**
  set the pixels of the rectangle (x1, y1 exclusive, clipped) to value; whole tiles of
  0 / 255 become sentinels  [tm_fill_rect]
- **`fn combine_block(&mut self, tx: u32, ty: u32, src: Block, op: u32) -> i32`**
  combine a 64 x 64 block into tile (tx, ty)  [tm_combine_block]
- **`fn combine(&mut self, src: &TMask, op: u32) -> i32`**
  dst = dst OP src per pixel (same size)  [tm_combine]
- **`fn combine_self(&mut self, op: u32) -> i32`**
  dst = dst OP dst (tm_combine with the same mask as both operands)
- **`fn copy(&mut self, src: &TMask) -> i32`**
  dst = src (same size)  [tm_copy]
- **`fn invert(&mut self)`**
  v = 255 - v  [tm_invert]
- **`fn threshold(&mut self, level: i32)`**
  v = v >= level ? 255 : 0 (level clamped to 1..255)  [tm_threshold]
- **`fn optimize(&mut self)`**
  every uniform tile becomes a sentinel  [tm_optimize]
- **`fn count_tiles(&self) -> (u32, u32)`**
  (tiles held in memory, slots that are all 255)  [tm_count_tiles]
- **`fn bbox(&self) -> Option<[i32; 4]>`**
  x0, y0, x1, y1 (exclusive) of the pixels != 0, None when the mask is empty  [tm_bbox]
- **`fn load_a8(&mut self, src: &[u8], stride: usize) -> i32`**
  the mask becomes a copy of the dense w x h A8 image at src (row stride `stride`)  [tm_load_a8]
- **`fn store_a8(&self, dst: &mut [u8], stride: usize)`**
  write the mask as a dense w x h A8 image (row stride `stride`)  [tm_store_a8]
- **`fn raster_path(&mut self, path: &Path, ras: &mut Raster, rule: u32, op: u32) -> i32`**
  rasterize the path (subpaths closed implicitly, rule RULE_\*) with exact anti-aliased
  coverage and combine it into the mask with op (TM_\*)  [tm_raster_path]
- **`fn raster_polygon(&mut self, path: &mut Path, ras: &mut Raster, pts: &[(f32, f32)], rule: u32, op: u32) -> i32`**
  raster_path of one closed polygon given as points in mask pixels (replaces the path)
- **`fn trace(&self, cb: &mut dyn FnMut(&[(i32, i32)]) -> i32) -> i32`**
  outlines of the pixels with coverage >= 128 along pixel edges: cb(points) for every
  closed contour (corners in pixel-edge coordinates, implicitly closed, inside on the
  right); returns 0 or the first nonzero cb result  [tm_trace]

### `gfx/xform.rs`

Source: [`lib/gfx/xform.rs`](../lib/gfx/xform.rs)

```text
xform -- affine matrices and the sine / cosine of the transform code     [src/img/xform.s]

  Matrix {a, b, c, d, e, f}: (x, y) -> (a x + b y + c, d x + e y + f)
  Matrix::IDENTITY, m.apply(x, y)
  sincos(angle) -> (sin, cos)    polynomial with a two-part pi/2 reduction (xf_sincos)

Used by pathdata (arcs, transformed output) and dash (round joins); img_xform (R6) builds
on the same functions.  f64 operations in the order of the assembly.
```

- **`struct Matrix`**
  an affine matrix (XM_\*)
  - `a: f64`
  - `b: f64`
  - `c: f64`
  - `d: f64`
  - `e: f64`
  - `f: f64`

**`impl Matrix`**

- **`const IDENTITY: Matrix = Matrix { a: 1.0, b: 0.0, c: 0.0, d: 0.0, e: 1.0, f: 0.0 }`**
- **`fn apply(&self, x: f64, y: f64) -> (f64, f64)`**
  xy = m \* xy  [xf_apply]

- **`fn sincos(angle: f64) -> (f64, f64)`**
  (sin angle, cos angle)  [xf_sincos]

## text

*fonts (SGF1, TrueType, CFF, COLR), glyph cache, text runs* -- crate root [`lib/text/lib.rs`](../lib/text/lib.rs)

```text
text -- fonts (embedded SGF1 / TrueType, runtime OpenType / collections, synthetic styles,
colour glyphs), the glyph cache and text runs                        [src/text/*.s]

  Text::new(fonts, names)          register the style's four embedded fonts
  set_font(font, size_fx)          select a font id and the pixel size (24.8)
  draw(dc, sf, x, y, s) -> px      y = baseline (origin-relative); stops at NUL / newline
  draw_n(dc, sf, x, y, s, len)     at most len bytes (a UTF-8 sequence cut by len ends it)
  measure(s) -> 24.8   width(s) -> px (rounded up)   ascent() / descent() / line_h() -> px
  hit(s, len, x) -> byte index     caret_x(s, index) -> px
  decor(dc, sf, x, y, w, TD_*)     underline / strikeout rectangles
  icon_draw(dc, sf, cp, x, y, size_fx)   FONT_ICON with its em box at x, y
  gc_get / gc_get_bitmap / gc_box / gc_flush / gc_scale   the glyph cache (glyphcache.rs)
  png_decode / img_scale     CBDT hooks (default: img_png::decode, img_core::ops::scale)
  font registry: registry.rs; system font database: fontdb.rs

Pen positions are 24.8 fixed point; glyphs are placed on quarter pixels and their
coverage masks are cached per (font, glyph, size, phase).  Colour fonts: COLR glyphs are
drawn layer by layer (palette 0; the foreground entry uses the current colour, palette
alphas are multiplied by the current colour's alpha), CBDT glyphs as PARGB bitmaps (not
drawn into A8 targets).  Glyphs too large for the glyph cache are filled directly.
```

### `text` crate root

```rust
FONT_REG: u32 = 0
FONT_SEMI: u32 = 1
FONT_BOLD: u32 = 2
FONT_ICON: u32 = 3
```

- **`struct Text`**
  - `ol: Outline` -- the outline buffer of runtime / derived fonts
  - `gp: Gp` -- the glyph transform used by glyph_path
  - `path: Path`
  - `ras: Raster`
  - `png_decode: Option<colr::PngDecode>` -- PNG decoder and bilinear scaler for CBDT colour bitmaps (set by the app / ui)
  - `img_scale: Option<colr::ImgScale>`

**`impl Text`**

- **`fn new(fonts: [&'static [u8]; 4], names: [[&'static str; 5]; 4]) -> Text`**
  font_init: register FONT_REG, FONT_SEMI, FONT_BOLD, FONT_ICON (the style's embedded
  fonts, SGF1 or TrueType/OpenType) with their names (family, style, full,
  typographic family / style). Each face starts with the legacy raster policy.
- **`fn set_font(&mut self, font: u32, size_fx: i32)`**
  text_font(font, size_fx)
- **`fn get_font(&self) -> (u32, i32)`**
  text_get_font() -> (font, size_fx)
- **`fn ascent(&self) -> i32`**
  ceil(ascender px)
- **`fn descent(&self) -> i32`**
  ceil(descender px), positive
- **`fn line_h(&self) -> i32`**
  round((asc - desc + gap) px)
- **`fn measure(&mut self, s: &str) -> i32`**
  text_measure: advance of the run (24.8), up to NUL / newline
- **`fn measure_n(&mut self, s: &[u8], len: usize) -> i32`**
  text_measure_n(s, len)
- **`fn width(&mut self, s: &str) -> i32`**
  text_width: px, rounded up
- **`fn width_n(&mut self, s: &[u8], len: usize) -> i32`**
- **`fn draw(&mut self, dc: &mut Dc, sf: &mut Surface, x: i32, y: i32, s: &[u8]) -> i32`**
  text_draw(x, y = baseline, s) -> width px
- **`fn draw_n(&mut self, dc: &mut Dc, sf: &mut Surface, x: i32, y: i32, s: &[u8], len: usize) -> i32`**
  text_draw_n(x, y, s, len) -> width px
- **`fn caret_x(&mut self, s: &[u8], index: usize) -> i32`**
  text_caret_x(s, index) -> x px (rounded) of the caret before byte index
- **`fn hit(&mut self, s: &[u8], len: Option<usize>, x: i32) -> usize`**
  text_hit(s, len, x) -> byte index whose caret position is closest to x (len None:
  up to NUL)
- **`fn decor(&self, dc: &Dc, sf: &mut Surface, x: i32, y: i32, w: i32, flags: u32)`**
  text_decor(x, y, w, flags): underline / strikeout rectangles of the current font and
  size (filled from x, w px long; y = baseline)
- **`fn icon_draw(&mut self, dc: &mut Dc, sf: &mut Surface, cp: u32, x: i32, y: i32, size_fx: i32)`**
  icon_draw(cp, x, y, size_fx): the icon's em box has its top-left corner at x, y

### `text/cff.rs`

Source: [`lib/text/cff.rs`](../lib/text/cff.rs)

```text
cff -- CFF ('CFF ' table of OpenType fonts): Type 2 charstrings -> cubic outlines
                                                                       [src/text/cff.s]

  Cff::open(table, upem) -> context / ERR_*   the header, Top DICT, CharStrings, global and
       local subrs, CID-keyed FDArray / FDSelect (formats 0 and 3), FontMatrix
  outline(table, glyph, ol) -> false on error   run the charstring, points into the outline
       buffer (OLT_ON / OLT_CUBIC, font units)

Type 2 operators: all path operators (r/h/v moveto, lineto, rrcurveto, hh/vv/hv/vh curveto,
rcurveline, rlinecurve, flex, hflex, hflex1, flex1), hint operators (stems are counted for
hintmask / cntrmask and skipped), callsubr / callgsubr (bias 107 / 1131 / 32768, nesting
<= 10), return, endchar (a seac accent is ignored), the arithmetic / storage operators
(abs add sub div neg eq drop put get ifelse random mul sqrt dup exch index roll and or
not).  Argument stack 48, transient array 32, at most 200000 operators per glyph.  The
width argument is dropped (advances come from hmtx).  CFF2 is not supported.  Like the
assembly, the transient array and the random generator are process-wide.
```

- **`struct Cff`**

**`impl Cff`**

- **`fn open(t: Tb, upem: u32) -> Result<Cff, i32>`**
  cff_open: ERR_HDR (malformed) / ERR_UNSUPPORTED (not CFF 1 / not Type 2 charstrings)
- **`fn outline(&self, t: Tb, glyph: u32, ol: &mut Outline) -> bool`**
  run the charstring of glyph into the outline buffer: false = malformed  [cff_outline]

### `text/colr.rs`

Source: [`lib/text/colr.rs`](../lib/text/colr.rs)

```text
colr -- colour glyphs: COLR v0 / CPAL layers and CBDT / CBLC PNG bitmaps  [src/text/colr.s]

  open(ctx)                         validate COLR + CPAL (palette 0) and CBDT + CBLC; sets
                                    TFF_COLR / TFF_CBDT
  layers(ctx, glyph, cap) -> {glyph | LAYER_FG, argb (straight)}   (empty = none)
  bitmap(ctx, glyph, size_fx, png, scale) -> Bitmap   the strike's PNG decoded and scaled
       to size_fx (area average when shrinking, bilinear when enlarging)

COLR v1 paint graphs are not supported: v1 fonts that keep v0 base glyph records use
those, otherwise the monochrome glyph is drawn.  Palette 0 only; palette index 0xFFFF =
the current text colour.  CBDT: index subtable formats 1..5, glyph image formats 17, 18,
19 (PNG); strike = the smallest ppem >= the requested size, else the largest.  The PNG
decoder and the enlarging scaler come from the image crates through hooks (Text).
```

```rust
LAYER_FG: u32 = 0x8000_0000
```

- **`type PngDecode = fn(&[u8]) -> Option<Surface>`**
  decode a PNG into a PARGB surface

- **`type ImgScale = fn(&Surface, i32, i32) -> Option<Surface>`**
  bilinear resize of a PARGB surface to w x h (img_scale mode 1)

- **`struct Bitmap`**
  a colour glyph bitmap: placement relative to pen x / baseline, PARGB pixels (stride 4 w)
  - `left: i32`
  - `top: i32`
  - `w: i32`
  - `h: i32`
  - `px: Vec<u8>`

- **`fn open(f: &mut TtFont)`**
  validate COLR + CPAL and CBDT + CBLC  [colr_open]

- **`fn layers(f: &TtFont, glyph: u32, cap: usize, out: &mut Vec<(u32, u32)>)`**
  layers of a COLR base glyph (empty = none), at most cap  [colr_layers]

- **`fn bitmap(f: &TtFont, glyph: u32, size_fx: i32, png: Option<PngDecode>, scale: Option<ImgScale>, metrics: &mut [u8; 5]) -> Option<Bitmap>`**
  the colour bitmap of glyph at size_fx (24.8 px)  [cbdt_bitmap]

```text
font -- embedded SGF1 fonts (tools/ttf2bin.py, ASMLIB/docs/FONTS.md), the font header and
FONTMETRICS records shared by every font kind                       [src/text/font.s]

  Font::parse(data)         the 64-byte header, the fast codepoint table (None: not SGF1)
  glyph(cp) -> id           fallback glyph when unmapped      lookup(cp) -> Option<id>
```

```rust
FAST_CP: u32 = 384
SGF1_MAGIC: u32 = 0x3146_4753
// SGF1 header flags (SG_FLAGS)
SGH_ICON: u16 = 1
SGH_KERN: u16 = 2
SGH_RUNTIME: u16 = 0x100
SGH_COLR: u16 = 0x200  // COLR layers (text runs draw them layer by layer)
SGH_CBDT: u16 = 0x400  // CBDT bitmaps (PARGB glyph cache entries)
// font_style flags
FS_SYN_BOLD: u32 = 1
FS_SYN_ITALIC: u32 = 2
FS_SHARP: u32 = 4
/// Classic screen typography: round kerning and advances to whole device pixels.
FS_CLASSIC: u32 = 8
FS_MASK: u32 = 15
// text_decor flags
TD_UNDERLINE: u32 = 1
TD_STRIKEOUT: u32 = 2
// font_name ids (OpenType name IDs)

FN_STYLE: u32 = 2
FN_FULL: u32 = 4
FN_TYPO_FAMILY: u32 = 16
FN_TYPO_STYLE: u32 = 17
// FONTMETRICS byte offsets (font_metric)
FM_UPEM: u32 = 0
FM_ASC: u32 = 4
FM_DESC: u32 = 8
FM_GAP: u32 = 12
FM_TYPO_ASC: u32 = 16
FM_TYPO_DESC: u32 = 20
FM_TYPO_GAP: u32 = 24

FM_WIN_DESC: u32 = 32

FM_UL_POS: u32 = 44
FM_UL_THICK: u32 = 48
FM_ST_POS: u32 = 52
FM_ST_THICK: u32 = 56
FM_WEIGHT: u32 = 60
FM_FLAGS: u32 = 64
FM_NGLYPHS: u32 = 68
FM_ADVMAX: u32 = 72
FM_WIDTH: u32 = 76
FM_SIZE: u32 = 80
// FONTMETRICS flags
FMF_ITALIC: i32 = 1
FMF_BOLD: i32 = 2
FMF_OBLIQUE: i32 = 4
FMF_COLOR: i32 = 8
FMF_CFF: i32 = 16
```
