### `plat/win_test.rs` (Windows)

Source: [`lib/plat/win_test.rs`](../lib/plat/win_test.rs)

```text
win_test -- test aids of the Windows platform layer (plattest --test window): they look at
and poke our own windows only (no input is injected into the system)

  win_test(op, w, a) -> i64   op:
    WT_PIXEL      0x00RRGGBB of client pixel a[0], a[1] of window w (GetPixel on its DC; -1
                  outside / no window)
    WT_REDRAW     invalidate the whole window and paint it now (WM_PAINT repaints from the
                  kept copy) -> 1
    WT_MOVE       move / size the window like the user would (SetWindowPos of the frame, not
                  win_set_rect): client origin a[0], a[1], client size a[2], a[3] -> 1
    WT_DPI        a WM_DPICHANGED of dpi a[0] with the suggested rect scaled around the
                  client origin (as Windows sends it when the window changes monitors) -> 1
    WT_CURSOR_IS  the current cursor is the one of shape a[0] (CUR_* / custom) -> 1 / 0
    WT_VISIBLE    window w is visible -> 1 / 0
    WT_POPUP_RECT popup slot w: its window rect x, y, w, h packed as 4 x i16 (-1: not shown)
    WT_POPUP_PIXEL popup slot w: 0x00RRGGBB at a[0], a[1]
    WT_ENABLED    window w takes input -> 1 / 0
    WT_CARET      the system caret's position x | y << 32 (GetCaretPos)
    WT_ACTIVE     window w is the active window -> 1 / 0
    WT_CLIP_ACCESS  0 when the clipboard can be opened, else the error (5: access denied,
                  e.g. in a sandbox that keeps processes off the clipboard)
    WT_POPUP_SEND popup slot w: SendMessageW(msg a[0], wParam a[1], lParam = point a[2], a[3])
  file_dialog_autoclose(ms, name)   the next file_dialog is closed after ms by a timer of the
                  UI thread: name None = Cancel, Some(name) = the name typed + OK
```

```rust
WT_PIXEL: u32 = 1
WT_REDRAW: u32 = 2
WT_MOVE: u32 = 3
WT_DPI: u32 = 4
WT_CURSOR_IS: u32 = 5
WT_VISIBLE: u32 = 6
WT_POPUP_RECT: u32 = 7
WT_POPUP_PIXEL: u32 = 8
WT_ENABLED: u32 = 9
WT_CARET: u32 = 10
WT_ACTIVE: u32 = 11
WT_CLIP_ACCESS: u32 = 12
WT_POPUP_SEND: u32 = 13
```

- **`fn win_test(op: u32, w: i32, a: [i32; 4]) -> i64`**
  see the file header

- **`fn file_dialog_autoclose(ms: u32, name: Option<&str>)`**
  (test aid) close the next file_dialog after ms: Cancel (name None) or name + OK

### `plat/win_inst.rs` (Windows)

Source: [`lib/plat/win_inst.rs`](../lib/plat/win_inst.rs)

```text
win_inst -- the Windows calls of the setup (apps/installer): the own exe path, known
folders, .lnk shortcuts through COM IShellLinkW, the HKCU registry key of the "installed
apps" entry, starting a command line             (r12-inst)  [apps/installer/src/shell.s]

  exe_path() -> Some(path)        GetModuleFileNameW of the running exe as UTF-8 (None when
                                  it does not fit PATH_CAP wide chars)  [self_path]
  known_folder(which) -> Some(p)  0 = desktop, 1 = start menu "Programs" (SHGetKnownFolderPath)
  shell_link(lnk, target, dir, desc) -> bool   a shortcut file lnk -> target (working
                                  folder dir, icon 0 of the target, comment desc)  [sh_link]
  reg_create(subkey) -> Some(RegKey)   HKCU\subkey opened for writing (created when missing);
                                  RegKey::set_sz / set_dword, closed when dropped
  reg_delete(subkey) -> bool      RegDeleteKeyW(HKCU, subkey)
  run_cmdline(cmdline, dir, hidden) -> bool    CreateProcessW (dir None = the current one,
                                  hidden = CREATE_NO_WINDOW)  [sh_run]

ole32 / shell32 / advapi32 are loaded with LoadLibraryA on the first use (inst_load, as the
asm's sh_load): only kernel32 / user32 / gdi32 are linked.  Paths are converted with the
asm's capacities (utf8_to_w with PATH_CAP wide chars, w_to_utf8 with PATH_CAP bytes).  The
test mode (ASMINST_SHELL_DIR) is the app's business: these functions always touch the real
shell / registry.
```

```rust
PATH_CAP: usize = 520
```

```text
win_sys -- Windows files, directories, environment, well-known paths, the date
                                        [src/plat/win/win_sys.s, win_files.s, plat_win.s]

Every function converts its paths into its own buffers, so all of them may run on any
thread.  File::read_at / write_at use explicit offsets (OVERLAPPED positions): several
threads may use one File at the same time.
```

  the same as env (ASMLIB's plat_getenv)

- **`fn setenv(name: &str, value: Option<&str>) -> bool`**
  set (Some) or remove (None) an environment variable of this process  [plat_setenv]

- **`struct File(isize)`**
  an open file (closed when dropped)

- **`struct FileLock`**
  Nonblocking exclusive sidecar lock. Drop releases the OS handle; keep the file in place.
  All cooperating writers must lock the same path before checking and replacing data.

**`impl File`**

- **`fn read_at(&self, off: u64, buf: &mut [u8]) -> Option<usize>`**
  bytes read at off (fewer at the end of the file), None on an error before any byte
- **`fn write_at(&self, off: u64, buf: &[u8]) -> Option<usize>`**
  bytes written at off (the file grows as needed), None on an error before any byte
- **`fn size(&self) -> Option<u64>`**
- **`fn sync(&self) -> bool`**
  write cached data to the disk
- **`fn set_len(&self, len: u64) -> bool`**
  cut or extend the file to `len` bytes

  wall-clock time: nanoseconds since 1970-01-01 00:00 UTC

### `plat/win_thread.rs` (Windows)

Source: [`lib/plat/win_thread.rs`](../lib/plat/win_thread.rs)

```text
win_thread -- Windows threads, semaphores, the wake-up of the UI thread, system information
                                                              [src/plat/win/win_thread.s]

wake(): a flag coalesces bursts.  With a window the wake-up is a posted WM_APP_WAKE (the
window turns it into E_WAKE, also inside modal loops); without one (headless, before the
window exists) an auto-reset event that wait_events waits for.  Everything here is
thread safe.
```

- **`struct Thread(isize)`**
  a thread started by thread_spawn; join it to wait for its end

- **`fn thread_spawn(stack: usize, f: Start) -> Option<Thread>`**
  run f on a new thread with stack bytes of stack (0 = 1 MB), committed up front  [plat_thread_create]

**`impl Thread`**

- **`fn join(self)`**
  wait until the thread has ended  [plat_thread_join]

- **`fn thread_yield()`**
  give the rest of the time slice away  [plat_thread_yield]

- **`struct Sem(isize)`**
  a counting semaphore

**`impl Sem`**

- **`fn new(initial: u32) -> Option<Sem>`**
- **`fn post(&self, n: u32)`**
  add n units (0: nothing)
- **`fn wait(&self, ms: i32) -> bool`**
  take one unit: true taken, false timeout (ms -1 = forever, 0 = only try)

- **`fn wake()`**
  any thread: make the UI thread's wait return with E_WAKE (coalesced)  [plat_wake]

- **`fn wait_events(deadline_us: u64, on_event: &mut dyn FnMut(&Event)) -> u32`**
  headless wait (no window): until a wake-up (E_WAKE to on_event, returns 1) or the
  deadline in time_us() (0 = only poll, u64::MAX = forever; returns 0)  [plat_wait]

- **`fn cpu_count() -> u32`**
  logical processors this process may use (>= 1)  [plat_cpu_count]

- **`fn mem_total() -> u64`**
  physical memory in bytes (0 unknown)  [plat_mem_total]

### `plat/win_print.rs` (Windows)

Source: [`lib/plat/win_print.rs`](../lib/plat/win_print.rs)

```text
win_print -- printing on Windows: PrintDlgExW (comdlg32, loaded at run time) and GDI
                                                     [src/plat/win/win_print.s; docs/modules/PRINT.md]

  print(job) -> 0 printed / ERR_CANCEL / ERR_UNSUPPORTED / ERR_IO / ERR_NOMEM / ERR_DIMS.
       The system print dialog (printer, paper, orientation, copies; the job's paper and
       PJF_LANDSCAPE preselected through a DEVMODE) returns a printer DC; the page geometry
       comes from GetDeviceCaps (PHYSICALWIDTH / -OFFSET, HORZRES, LOGPIXELS), layout()
       places the image, which goes out with StretchDIBits (HALFTONE) in bands of at most
       BAND_BYTES of 24-bit rows composited over white (row_white), clipped to the
       printable area (IntersectClipRect).
  Test variant (never a dialog, never paper):
       jfgui_PRINT_TO=FILE          output goes to FILE (DOCINFO.lpszOutput) on the printer
       jfgui_PRINT_PRINTER=NAME     (default "Microsoft Print to PDF"), opened with
                                     CreateDCW and a DEVMODE for paper / orientation
       Without a main window (headless) and without jfgui_PRINT_TO: ERR_UNSUPPORTED.
The gdi32 functions are imported directly (the assembly took ten of them with
GetProcAddress only to keep its import library small); PrintDlgExW (comdlg32),
GetDefaultPrinterW (winspool.drv) and CoInitializeEx (ole32) are loaded at run time.
```

### `plat/win_lib.rs` (Windows)

Source: [`lib/plat/win_lib.rs`](../lib/plat/win_lib.rs)

```text
win_lib -- plugin libraries and the crash guard: the Windows part
                                        [src/plat/win/win_lib.s; docs/modules/PLUGIN_LOADER.md]

  lib_open(path) -> Lib / Err(LIBE_*)   LoadLibraryExW(DONT_RESOLVE_DLL_REFERENCES): the
       image is mapped and relocated, but DllMain / TLS callbacks never run and no import
       is bound -- a plugin with an import, delay-import or TLS directory is rejected
       (LIBE_DEPS / LIBE_INIT); only x86-64 DLLs (LIBE_FORMAT).  Hard-error dialogs are
       suppressed (SetThreadErrorMode).  Segments: headers + every section.
  lib_sym(h, name) -> address | 0       own export-directory lookup (bounds checked against
       SizeOfImage, every table / name inside a readable section -- a section without
       MEM_READ is PAGE_NOACCESS; forwarders -> 0, so no other DLL is ever loaded)
  (crate) lib_unload(h)                 FreeLibrary (lib_close / drop)
  (crate) plat_tid() -> u64             thread id (TEB ClientId, gs:[0x48])
  (crate) guard_thread()                nothing on Windows
  (crate) guard_os_install()            AddVectoredExceptionHandler(first, guard_veh)

guard_veh handles access violations, in-page errors, illegal / privileged instructions,
integer and FP traps, misalignment, breakpoints and stack overflow -- only while the
faulting thread has an active guard (plib.rs); everything else goes on to the next
handler (win_crash.rs).
```

- **`fn lib_open(path: &str) -> Result<Lib, u32>`**
  open a plugin DLL (see the file header)  [plat_lib_open]

- **`fn lib_sym(h: &Lib, name: &str) -> usize`**
  the address of the export name, 0 when missing / forwarded  [plat_lib_sym]

### `plat/linux_disp.rs` (Linux)

Source: [`lib/plat/linux_disp.rs`](../lib/plat/linux_disp.rs)

```text
linux_disp -- display backend dispatch on Linux: Wayland (linux_wl*.rs) or X11 (linux_x11*.rs)
                                                       [src/plat/linux/disp.s, lx_popup.s]

The display-dependent functions go through the backend object (trait Display).  The
first win_create (the main window, owner PW_NOOWNER) picks the backend:
  jfgui_BACKEND=wayland | x11     forces one (only the first letter counts, like disp.s)
  WAYLAND_DISPLAY set              Wayland first, then X11 if DISPLAY is set
  only DISPLAY set                 X11 first, then Wayland (default socket)
  neither                          Wayland (default socket), else an error
When no backend can open its main window the process ends with exit code 1 and a
message (as disp.s).  Before that the minimum size of window 0 and the refresh callback
are remembered and handed to the chosen backend.  wait() without a backend (headless
runs) sleeps until its deadline or a wake() (lx_wait_nodisp).

The trait is the contract between the two backend agents and with Windows (win_api.rs has
the same free functions): change it only together and say so in the report.

plat-x11 (R4): the backend choice follows disp.s exactly (first letter of
jfgui_BACKEND, the fallback order, exit 1 when every backend fails, the Wayland
backend's "quiet" flag: disp_wl_quiet()); Display::pen_devices (default -1) was added
for plat_pen_devices; cursor_dpi without a display answers the dpi (pre_cdpi); the
headless wait is lx_wait_nodisp (wake fd, E_WAKE).
```

```rust
BK_NONE: u32 = 0
BK_WAYLAND: u32 = 1
BK_X11: u32 = 2
```

- **`trait Display: Send`**
  a display backend (window-indexed; see the free functions below for the semantics)
  - `fn ime_pos(&mut self,_x:i32,_y:i32,_h:i32)`
  - `fn win_create(&mut self, title: &str, w_dip: i32, h_dip: i32, flags: u32, owner: i32, x: i32, y: i32) -> i32`
  - `fn win_destroy(&mut self, w: i32)`
  - `fn win_show(&mut self, w: i32, mode: u32)`
  - `fn win_activate(&mut self, w: i32)`
  - `fn win_enable(&mut self, w: i32, on: bool)`
  - `fn win_set_rect(&mut self, w: i32, x: i32, y: i32, cw: i32, ch: i32)`
  - `fn win_get_rect(&mut self, w: i32) -> Option<[i32; 4]>`
  - `fn win_work_area(&mut self, w: i32) -> Option<[i32; 4]>`
  - `fn win_set_title(&mut self, w: i32, title: &str)`
  - `fn win_state(&mut self, w: i32) -> u32`
  - `fn win_cmd(&mut self, w: i32, cmd: u32)`
  - `fn win_begin_move(&mut self, w: i32)`
  - `fn win_begin_resize(&mut self, w: i32, edges: u32)`
  - `fn win_size(&mut self, w: i32) -> (i32, i32)`
  - `fn win_dpi(&mut self, w: i32) -> i32`
  - `fn win_set_min_size(&mut self, w: i32, w_dip: i32, h_dip: i32)`
  - `fn win_present(&mut self, w: i32, f: &Frame, rects: Option<&[[i32; 4]]>)`
  - `fn win_set_cursor(&mut self, w: i32, shape: u32)`
  - `fn set_refresh(&mut self, f: Option<fn()>)`
  - `fn wait(&mut self, deadline_us: u64) -> u32`
  - `fn popup(&mut self, slot: u32, owner: i32, rect: Option<[i32; 4]>, f: Option<&Frame>)`
  - `fn cursor_create(&mut self, px: &[u8], w: i32, h: i32, hot_x: i32, hot_y: i32, dpi: i32) -> i32`
  - `fn cursor_destroy(&mut self, id: i32)`
  - `fn cursor_dpi(&mut self, dpi: i32) -> i32`
  - `fn clip_set(&mut self, fmt: u32, data: &[u8]) -> bool`
  - `fn clip_set_many(&mut self, entries: &[(u32, &[u8])]) -> bool`
  - `fn clip_get(&mut self, fmt: u32) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`
  - `fn clip_has(&mut self, fmt: u32) -> bool`
  - `fn pen_devices(&mut self) -> i32`
    pens / pen tablets the backend sees, -1 = unknown (plat_pen_devices: wl_pen_devices /
    x11_pen_devices)  -- plat-x11: added with a default, the Wayland backend overrides it

- **`fn disp_backend() -> u32`**
  0 none, 1 Wayland, 2 X11

- **`fn disp_wl_quiet() -> bool`**
  (disp.s g_wl_quiet) the Wayland backend is being tried without jfgui_BACKEND=wayland:

### `plat/linux_wl.rs` (Linux)

Source: [`lib/plat/linux_wl.rs`](../lib/plat/linux_wl.rs)

```text
linux_wl -- the Wayland display backend behind linux_disp::Display
                                    [src/plat/linux/wl_window.s, wl_wire.s, wl_present.s, ...]

  wl_connect() -> the backend, None when no compositor answers ($WAYLAND_DISPLAY in
       $XDG_RUNTIME_DIR, /run/user/UID, /mnt/wslg/runtime-dir)
  test hooks for linuxtest (synthetic events, no compositor needed): wl_test_event,
       wl_test_setup, wl_input_service, wl_cursor_image, wl_cursor_render, wl_test_protoerr
  xkb_* (linux_xkb.rs): the keymap of the Wayland keyboard, also used by X11

The modules follow the assembly files:
  linux_wl_wire.rs     connection, object table, marshalling, fd passing, dispatch,
                       roundtrips, fatal errors, the jfgui_WLDEBUG trace   [wl_wire.s]
  linux_wl_proto.rs    interface descriptors (event tables) and requests    [wl_proto.s]
  linux_wl_window.rs   globals, xdg toplevels, configure, scale, commands, the wait loop
                                                                            [wl_window.s]
  linux_wl_present.rs  wl_shm buffers, damage, frame callbacks            [wl_present.s]
  linux_wl_input.rs    wl_seat pointer / keyboard, key repeat               [wl_input.s]
  linux_wl_tablet.rs   zwp_tablet_v2 pens                                  [wl_tablet.s]
  linux_wl_popup.rs    xdg_popup windows                                    [wl_popup.s]
  linux_wl_clip.rs     wl_data_device: clipboard both ways, file drops       [wl_clip.s]
  linux_wl_cursor.rs   cursor-shape protocol, own cursor images, custom cursors [wl_cursor.s]

State: the assembly's globals are one record (Wl) owned by this module, created on the
first use (the synthetic tests use it without a connection).  Every entry point (the
Display methods, the hooks) borrows it once for its whole call and passes `&mut Wl` down
to the event handlers; a nested borrow (re-entry) panics instead of aliasing.  Only the
UI thread calls in; wake() from other threads goes through the eventfd of linux_thread.
```

- **`fn wl_connect() -> Option<Box<dyn Display>>`**
  connect to the compositor -> the backend, None when there is no Wayland display (quiet
  unless jfgui_BACKEND=wayland forces it)  [wl_connect]

- **`fn wl_test_event(iface: &str, event: &str, args: &[u64]) -> bool`**
  call the handler of event `event` ("enter", "key", ...) of interface `iface`
  ("wl_pointer", "wl_keyboard", ...) with decoded arguments (object id 1, udata 0), as
  linuxtest --test synth does -> false when there is no such event  [hcall]

- **`fn wl_test_event_u(iface: &str, event: &str, args: &[u64], udata: u64) -> bool`**
  (main agent) wl_test_event with the handler's udata (a tablet tool's slot ...), as
  plattest --test pen does  [tcall in apps/plattest/src/pen.s]

- **`fn wl_test_scale(s120: i32)`**
  (main agent) the pointer scale while there is no window (g_wl_s120, 120ths) alone

- **`fn wl_test_setup(s120: i32, seat_ver: u32)`**
  the pointer scale while there is no window (g_wl_s120, 120ths) and the seat version
  the handlers assume (g_wl_seat_ver)

- **`fn wl_input_service(now: u64) -> u64`**
  key repeat at `now` (time_us) -> the next repeat deadline, u64::MAX = none
  [wl_input_service]

- **`fn wl_cursor_image(shape: u32) -> Option<(u32, u32, u32, u32, &'static [u8])>`**
  the built-in cursor image of a CUR_\* shape: (width, height, hot x, hot y, rows of 'X'
  black / 'o' white / '.' transparent)  [wl_cursor_image]

- **`fn wl_cursor_render(dst: &mut [u32], shape: u32, s: u32)`**
  expand a cursor image into ARGB pixels (S x S blocks, rows of width \* S pixels)
  [wl_cursor_render]

- **`fn wl_test_protoerr()`**
  attach a buffer id that does not exist to the main window's surface: the compositor
  answers with wl_display.error, which ends the process (linuxtest --test protoerr)

### `plat/linux_xkb.rs` (Linux)

Source: [`lib/plat/linux_xkb.rs`](../lib/plat/linux_xkb.rs)

```text
linux_xkb -- minimal XKB keymap parser and key translation (Wayland keyboard; the X11
backend fills the same table from the core keyboard mapping)        [src/plat/linux/xkb.s]

  xkb_parse(text) -> 0 ok / -1           parse an xkbcommon text keymap (format 1)
  xkb_use_fallback()                     built-in evdev -> US English table
  xkb_set_mods(depressed, latched, locked, group) -> MOD_* bits (also wl_mods())
  xkb_key(evdev) -> (VK, text codepoint)  for the current modifiers (text 0 = none)
  xkb_is_modifier(evdev) -> bool         the key is a modifier (never auto-repeats)
  xkb_mod_bit(evdev) -> MOD_* bit the key holds (Shift / Ctrl / Alt / Super / AltGr) or 0
  xkb_dump()                             print the parsed table to stdout (tests)
  wl_mods() / wl_mods_set(m)             the MOD_* bits of the events (g_wl_mods)
  X11 backend: xkb_begin(), xkb_set_key(evdev, levels, nlev), xkb_set_roles(alt, num,
  super, level3)

Parsed: xkb_keycodes (`<NAME> = code;`, `alias <A> = <B>;`, names <= 4 chars, codes
< 264), xkb_symbols (first level list of group 1 per key, `type=`, `modifier_map`).
xkb_types / xkb_compatibility are skipped; key types are classified by name (ONE_LEVEL,
CTRL+ALT, PC_* -> one level; *KEYPAD* -> keypad; *ALPHABETIC* -> Caps Lock applies) or,
without an explicit type, like xkbcomp does (1 symbol -> one level, KP_* -> keypad, a/A
pair -> alphabetic).

Level: ONE -> 1; KEYPAD -> (Shift ^ NumLock) ? 2 : 1;
       else s = Shift ^ (ALPHA && Caps && !Level3), level = (Level3 ? 3 : 1) + s.
Text is produced for codepoints >= 0x20 (not 0x7F) unless Ctrl / Alt / Super is held
without Level3 (AltGr may arrive as Ctrl + Mod5 from Windows hosts).  Dead keys produce
their spacing character at once (no compose).  VK codes: layout-following for a-z / 0-9
(level-1 symbol, like Win32) and for + , - . (VK_OEM_PLUS/COMMA/MINUS/PERIOD); otherwise
by key position; keypad keys give navigation VKs while Num Lock is off.

The table is process state behind a lock (both display backends and the tests use it).
```

```rust
// symbol values: codepoint | flags
KV_CP: u32 = 0x1FFFFF
KV_KP: u32 = 0x2000_0000
KV_DEAD: u32 = 0x4000_0000
KV_MODSHIFT: u32 = 24
```

- **`fn wl_mods() -> u32`**
  the MOD_\* bits the events carry (g_wl_mods)

- **`fn wl_mods_set(m: u32)`**
  set the MOD_\* bits of the events (a modifier key's own event, wl_input)

- **`fn xkb_parse(text: &[u8]) -> i32`**
  parse an xkbcommon text keymap -> 0 ok / -1 (fewer than 20 keys found)  [xkb_parse]

- **`fn xkb_use_fallback()`**
  evdev -> US English symbols (used when no keymap parses)  [xkb_use_fallback]

- **`fn xkb_begin()`**
  (X11 backend) empty key table and modifier roles, before xkb_set_key  [xkb_begin]

- **`fn xkb_set_key(evdev: u32, levels: &[u32; 4], nlev: u32)`**
  (X11 backend) define one key: 4 symbol values (as the keymap parser makes them), nlev =
  number of levels listed  [xkb_set_key]

- **`fn xkb_set_roles(alt: u32, num: u32, sup: u32, level3: u32)`**
  (X11 backend) real-modifier masks of the modifier roles (0 = the Wayland default)
  [xkb_set_roles]

- **`fn xkb_set_mods(depressed: u32, latched: u32, locked: u32, _group: u32) -> u32`**
  the real modifier state -> MOD_\* bits (also the events' modifiers)  [xkb_set_mods]

- **`fn xkb_is_modifier(evdev: u32) -> bool`**
  the key's level-1 symbol is a modifier  [xkb_is_modifier]

- **`fn xkb_mod_bit(evdev: u32) -> u32`**
  MOD_\* bit of a held modifier key (0 for other keys and locks)  [xkb_mod_bit]

- **`fn xkb_key(evdev: u32) -> (u32, u32)`**
  evdev code -> (VK code, text codepoint (0 = none))  [xkb_key]

- **`fn xkb_dump()`**
  print every defined key: evdev, VK, flags, modifier id, 4 levels  [xkb_dump]

### `plat/linux_x11.rs` (Linux)

Source: [`lib/plat/linux_x11.rs`](../lib/plat/linux_x11.rs)

```text
linux_x11 -- the X11 display backend: our own X11 protocol client over a Unix socket (no
Xlib / xcb) behind linux_disp::Display
                    [src/plat/linux/x11.inc, x11_wire.s, x11_window.s, x11_present.s,
                     x11_input.s, x11_xi.s, x11_clip.s, x11_dnd.s, x11_popup.s,
                     x11_cursor.s, x11_monitor.s; docs/modules/WINDOWS_PLATFORM.md Ã‚Â§4, Ã‚Â§9, Ã‚Â§10]

  x11_connect() -> backend        $DISPLAY reachable: the backend (the main window is made
                                  by its win_create), None (no message) otherwise
  x11t::*                         the self-test entry points of linuxtest (x11synth,
                                  x11err): events without a server, keysyms, the core
                                  keymap, raw requests, the last X error

The state of the assembly's globals lives in one X11 record (module state, one UI thread):
the backend object is empty and every Display call borrows the record for its duration.
Nothing inside calls back into the plat API that would borrow it again (the refresh
callback is only stored, as on X11 in the assembly), so a re-entrant borrow would be a
bug and panics.  Files:
  linux_x11_wire.rs     connection, authentication, setup, requests, replies, errors
  linux_x11_window.rs   window records, hints, WM messages, DPI, state, events, wait
  linux_x11_present.rs  MIT-SHM (memfd / SysV) or PutImage, per window
  linux_x11_input.rs    core keyboard / pointer input, keysyms, the core keymap (built into
                        the key table of linux_xkb.rs: xkb_begin / xkb_set_key / xkb_key)
  linux_x11_xi.rs       XInput 2 pens
  linux_x11_clip.rs     CLIPBOARD (INCR both ways, images) and XDND drops
  linux_x11_misc.rs     popups (override-redirect), cursors (font / RENDER), monitors (RandR)

Deviation from the assembly (the Rust API hands the frame over only for the call):
presented pixels are copied into a per-window shadow image (the damaged rectangles), and
popups keep a copy of their pixels; repaints (Expose, after a map, the PutImage fallback)
read these copies where the assembly re-read the caller's framebuffer.
```

- **`fn x11_connect() -> Option<Box<dyn Display>>`**
  connect to $DISPLAY -> the backend, None when there is no X server  [x11_plat_win_create]

**`mod x11t`**

the X11 internals linuxtest --test x11synth / x11err drives (x11_event, x11_ks_value,
x11_kb_\*, g_x11_xkb_on, g_x11_mods, x11_req, x11_sync, g_x11_err_last; the key table is
plat::xkb_\*)

- **`fn event(pkt: &[u8; 32])`**
  one X event packet (32 bytes) through the event handler (x11_event)

- **`fn event_bytes(pkt: &[u8])`**
  one X event packet of any length (a GenericEvent: 32 bytes + its extra length)
  through the event handler (x11_event)

- **`fn xi_setup(op: u32, atoms: [u32; 3])`**
  XInput 2 without a server: its major opcode and the valuator label atoms ("Abs
  Pressure", "Abs Tilt X", "Abs Tilt Y"); op 0 turns it off again (g_xi_op, g_xi_atoms)

- **`fn xi_parse_devices(reply: &[u8]) -> u32`**
  the pen devices of an XIQueryDevice reply -> pens found (x11_xi_parse_devices)

- **`fn pen_devices() -> i32`**
  pens found, -1 = no XInput 2 (x11_pen_devices)

- **`fn ks_value(ks: u32) -> u32`**
  a keysym as an xkb.s symbol value (x11_ks_value)

- **`fn kb_set_keysyms(ks: &[u32], first: u32, count: u32, per: u32)`**
  the core keyboard mapping: `per` keysyms per keycode from `first` (x11_kb_set_keysyms)

- **`fn kb_set_modmap(kc: &[u8], per: u32)`**
  the modifier mapping: 8 modifiers x per keycodes (x11_kb_set_modmap)

- **`fn kb_build(group: u32)`**
  build the key records of a group (x11_kb_build)

- **`fn set_xkb_on(on: bool)`**
  XKB on (event state bits 13-14 select the group)  (g_x11_xkb_on)

- **`fn mods() -> u32`**
  the current MOD_\* bits (g_x11_mods)

- **`fn req(major: u32, body: &[u8]) -> u32`**
  a raw request: major opcode, body after the 4-byte header -> its sequence number
  (x11_req; nothing without a connection)

- **`fn sync()`**
  round trip: every earlier request has been processed (x11_sync)

- **`fn err_last() -> u32`**
  the sequence number of the last X error (g_x11_err_last)

**`mod x11s`**

the X11 state other plat modules read (plat-shared)

- **`fn main_xid() -> u32`**
  the main window's XID (0 = none)  (g_x11_win)

- **`fn root_property(name: &[u8], atom: &mut u32) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`**
  the root window property `name` (AnyPropertyType, X11_PROP_CHUNK longs) -> the
  GetProperty reply (see get_property), None without a connection / on an error.  `atom`
  caches the atom between calls: 0 not asked yet (InternAtom only-if-exists is sent),
  u32::MAX the name does not exist  (lx_icc.s g_icc_atom)

### `plat/linux_portal.rs` (Linux)

Source: [`lib/plat/linux_portal.rs`](../lib/plat/linux_portal.rs)

```text
linux_portal -- xdg-desktop-portal over a minimal D-Bus client (raw socket, SASL EXTERNAL,
little-endian marshalling): the file dialog, OpenURI / OpenDirectory, Print
                                                           [src/plat/linux/portal.s]

  file_dialog(flags, path, exts) -> (1 chosen / 0 cancelled / -1 no portal, index of the
       chosen extension entry).  Same contract as on Windows (win_filedlg.rs): flags FD_SAVE
       | index << FD_EXT_SHIFT, path in / out (UTF-8, '/'; at most PATH_CAP - 1 bytes out),
       exts ["png=PNG-Bild", "jpg;jpeg=JPEG-Bild"] (an empty entry ends the list).
  open_url(url) -> 0 / -1         a web page in the default application: OpenURI.OpenURI;
                                  jfgui_OPEN_DRYRUN=1 only prints it
  open_folder(path) -> 0 / -1     show a folder / a file in the file manager:
       OpenURI.OpenDirectory with an O_PATH descriptor (SCM_RIGHTS), fallback
       OpenURI.OpenURI("file://...").  The Response is awaited for OF_WAIT ms (the window
       keeps being serviced): code 0 -> 0, 1 / 2 -> -1; without an answer by then the
       request stays alive on its connection until the next call and 0 is returned.  No
       bus / no portal / no such path -> -1.  jfgui_OPEN_DRYRUN=1: printed, nothing sent.
  (crate) portal_print_prepare / portal_print_send / portal_print_end  org.freedesktop.portal
       .Print for linux_print.rs (PreparePrint with the job's paper / orientation, the page
       setup of the answer; Print with the PDF as a descriptor)

The dialogs belong to the portal's process.  While one is open the display keeps being
serviced (wait); input events of that time are dropped, window events (resize, close,
focus, dpi, paint) are kept and queued again afterwards.

State: the assembly's globals (receive buffer, body / message builders, token, request
path, ...) are one Bus record (module state, UI thread only).  Messages are byte-identical
to the assembly's (serials, field order, padding, token "jfgui<n>").
```

```rust
/// file_dialog: the path capacity (FD_PATH of ui_filedlg.s)
PATH_CAP: usize = 1040
```

- **`fn file_dialog(flags: u32, path: &mut String, exts: &[&str]) -> (i32, u32)`**
  the portal's FileChooser (see the file header)  [plat_file_dialog]

- **`fn open_folder(path: &str) -> i32`**
  show a folder / a file in the file manager (OpenURI.OpenDirectory) -> 0 / -1  [plat_open_folder]

- **`fn open_url(url: &str) -> i32`**
  a web page in the default application (OpenURI.OpenURI) -> 0 / -1  [plat_open_url]

### `plat/linux_sys.rs` (Linux)

Source: [`lib/plat/linux_sys.rs`](../lib/plat/linux_sys.rs)

```text
linux_sys -- Linux files, directories, well-known paths   [src/plat/linux/lx_sys.s, plat_linux.s]

No static buffers: every function may run on any thread.  File::read_at / write_at are
pread / pwrite, so several threads may use one File at the same time.
```

- **`fn dev_ino(path: &str) -> Option<(u64, u64)>`**
  (st_dev, st_ino) of a path (links followed), None when it cannot be read: the identity
  fontdb uses to visit a directory once  [fd_visit_once]

- **`fn config_dir() -> Option<String>`**
  $XDG_CONFIG_HOME (absolute), else $HOME/.config; no trailing slash

- **`fn temp_dir() -> Option<String>`**
  $TMPDIR (absolute), else /tmp

- **`fn cwd() -> String`**
  the current directory ("" on failure)  [plat_cwd]

- **`struct File(i32)`**
  an open file (closed when dropped)

- **`struct FileLock`**
  Nonblocking advisory sidecar lock. Drop closes the descriptor and releases the lock.
  All cooperating writers must lock the same path; never delete an active sidecar.

**`impl File`**

- **`fn read_at(&self, off: u64, buf: &mut [u8]) -> Option<usize>`**
  bytes read at off (fewer at the end of the file), None on an error before any byte
- **`fn write_at(&self, off: u64, buf: &[u8]) -> Option<usize>`**
  bytes written at off (the file grows as needed), None on an error before any byte
- **`fn size(&self) -> Option<u64>`**
- **`fn sync(&self) -> bool`**
  write cached data to the disk (fsync)
- **`fn set_len(&self, len: u64) -> bool`**
  cut or extend the file to `len` bytes (ftruncate)

### `plat/linux_thread.rs` (Linux)

Source: [`lib/plat/linux_thread.rs`](../lib/plat/linux_thread.rs)

```text
linux_thread -- Linux threads (clone + futex), semaphores, the wake-up of the UI thread,
system information, the date                       [src/plat/linux/lx_thread.s, plat_linux.s]

A thread's stack is an mmap with a PROT_NONE guard page below it; the thread record sits
at its top.  The kernel clears the record's tid word and wakes it (a shared futex wake)
when the thread ends, which is what join waits for.  Semaphores are process-private
futex counters.  The wake-up is an eventfd: the display backends poll it next to their
socket, wait_events polls it alone (headless).
```

- **`struct Thread(*mut ThreadRec)`**
  a thread started by thread_spawn; join it to wait for its end (and free its stack)

- **`fn thread_spawn(stack: usize, f: Start) -> Option<Thread>`**
  run f on a new thread with stack bytes of stack (0 = 1 MB)  [plat_thread_create]

**`impl Thread`**

- **`fn join(self)`**
  wait until the thread has ended, then free its stack  [plat_thread_join]

- **`fn thread_yield()`**
  give the rest of the time slice away  [plat_thread_yield]

- **`struct Sem(Box<SemInner>)`**
  a counting semaphore

**`impl Sem`**

- **`fn new(initial: u32) -> Option<Sem>`**
- **`fn post(&self, n: u32)`**
  add n units, wake sleepers (0: nothing)
- **`fn wait(&self, ms: i32) -> bool`**
  take one unit: true taken, false timeout (ms -1 = forever, 0 = only try)

- **`fn wake()`**
  any thread: make the UI thread's wait return with E_WAKE (coalesced)  [plat_wake]

- **`fn wait_events(deadline_us: u64, on_event: &mut dyn FnMut(&Event)) -> u32`**
  headless wait (no display): until a wake-up (E_WAKE to on_event, returns 1) or the
  deadline in time_us() (0 = only poll, u64::MAX = forever; returns 0)  [lx_wait_nodisp]

- **`fn cpu_count() -> u32`**
  CPUs this process may run on (sched_getaffinity), >= 1  [plat_cpu_count]

- **`fn mem_total() -> u64`**
  physical memory in bytes (sysinfo), 0 unknown  [plat_mem_total]

- **`fn date() -> (Date, bool)`**
  the date and time in UTC (no time zone database); the flag is true (UTC)  [plat_date]

### `plat/linux_print.rs` (Linux)

Source: [`lib/plat/linux_print.rs`](../lib/plat/linux_print.rs)

```text
linux_print -- printing on Linux: xdg-desktop-portal Print with a PDF of the job
                                                   [src/plat/linux/lx_print.s; docs/modules/PRINT.md]

  print(job) -> 0 printed / ERR_CANCEL / ERR_UNSUPPORTED / ERR_IO / ERR_NOMEM / ERR_DIMS.
       PreparePrint (the portal's dialog: printer, paper, copies; our paper and orientation
       suggested), then the job laid out on the chosen page as a one-page PDF (print_pdf)
       in a memfd handed to Print.
  Test / headless variants (never a dialog):
       jfgui_PRINT_TO=FILE.pdf    the PDF is written to FILE (paper from job.paper)
       jfgui_PRINT_SMASK=1        (with jfgui_PRINT_TO) alpha as /SMask instead of white
       jfgui_PRINT_PORTAL=1       allow the portal without a window (fake bus tests);
       otherwise a run without a display (headless) returns ERR_UNSUPPORTED.
```

- **`fn print(job: &mut PrintJob) -> i32`**
  print one image on one page (see the file header)  [plat_print]

### `plat/linux_elfload.rs` (Linux)

Source: [`lib/plat/linux_elfload.rs`](../lib/plat/linux_elfload.rs)

```text
linux_elfload -- plugin libraries on Linux: a small ELF loader without libc
                                       [src/plat/linux/elfload.s; docs/modules/PLUGIN_LOADER.md]

  lib_open(path) -> Lib / Err(LIBE_*)
  lib_sym(h, name) -> address | 0       .dynsym lookup (count from DT_HASH nchain or the
                                        DT_GNU_HASH chains)
  (crate) lib_unload(h)                 munmap (lib_close / drop)

Accepted: ELFCLASS64, little endian, ET_DYN, EM_X86_64, program headers of 56 bytes
(1..64), at most LH_SEGMAX PT_LOAD segments in ascending, page-disjoint order, never
writable AND executable, one PT_DYNAMIC; relocations R_X86_64_NONE / RELATIVE and
R_X86_64_64 / GLOB_DAT / JUMP_SLOT against symbols the object defines itself (RELA only).
Rejected: DT_NEEDED or undefined symbols (LIBE_DEPS), PT_INTERP / PT_TLS / DT_INIT /
DT_INIT_ARRAY / DT_PREINIT_ARRAY (LIBE_INIT: nothing of the object runs while loading),
DT_REL and other relocation types (LIBE_RELOC), everything malformed (LIBE_FORMAT).
The file is untrusted input: every header value, offset, size and address is checked in
64-bit arithmetic against the file size or the mapped span (LIB_SPAN_MAX) before use.
Mapping: one anonymous read-write reservation of the whole span, the segments copied in,
relocations applied, then mprotect per segment (gaps PROT_NONE, PT_GNU_RELRO read-only).
While loading, the mapping is accessed as a slice (every access bounds-checked on top of
the assembly's checks); lib_sym reads the protected image like the assembly.
```

- **`fn lib_open(path: &str) -> Result<Lib, u32>`**
  open a plugin ELF object (see the file header)  [plat_lib_open]

- **`fn lib_sym(h: &Lib, name: &str) -> usize`**
  the address of the defined dynamic symbol name, or 0  [plat_lib_sym]

### `plat/web.rs`

Source: [`lib/plat/web.rs`](../lib/plat/web.rs)

```text
Shared browser/Android host adapter. The host exchanges bounded buffers and queued events;
app state stays in the same Rust UI/runtime used by native applications.
```

```rust
/// Persist a virtual file to browser storage; payload: u32 path byte count, path, file bytes.
REQUEST_PERSIST: u32 = 1
/// Export/download a file; same payload framing as persistence.
REQUEST_DOWNLOAD: u32 = 2
/// Open a browser file picker; payload is a UTF-8 extension filter.
REQUEST_OPEN: u32 = 3
/// Read browser clipboard text asynchronously.
REQUEST_CLIP_READ: u32 = 4
/// Write browser clipboard text; payload is UTF-8.
REQUEST_CLIP_WRITE: u32 = 5
/// Update the DOM composition element's caret rectangle (three little-endian i32 values).
REQUEST_IME: u32 = 6
/// Update the browser pointer shape (one little-endian u32 CUR value).
REQUEST_CURSOR: u32 = 7
/// Update the document title (UTF-8).
REQUEST_TITLE: u32 = 8
/// Open an explicitly requested HTTP(S)/mailto URL; payload is UTF-8.
REQUEST_URL: u32 = 9
/// Mirror explicit in-app control activation/revocation to the host (one boolean byte).
REQUEST_AGENT: u32 = 10
```

- **`enum RequestStatus`**
  Completion state of one browser operation; durable and exported are distinct outcomes.
  - `Pending` -- The host has not completed the operation.
  - `Durable` -- Browser storage confirmed this exact file revision was committed.
  - `Exported` -- The host completed a download/export, not an origin-storage commit.
  - `Completed` -- A transient operation such as clipboard access completed.
  - `Denied` -- The user or browser denied access.
  - `Quota` -- Browser storage quota was exhausted.
  - `Unsupported` -- This browser lacks the requested service.
  - `Cancelled` -- User cancellation.
  - `Failed` -- Other host I/O failure.

- **`trait WebApplication`**
  An owned app runtime registered once when the browser entrypoint returns.
  - `fn frame_delay(&self) -> u32`
    Milliseconds until a timer or animation needs a host tick; input wakes immediately.
  - `fn input(&mut self, time_ms: u32)`
    Drain queued input without presenting intermediate frames; default runtimes may tick.
  - `fn tick(&mut self, time_ms: u32)`
    Process queued input, advance timers and render one frame.
  - `fn control(&mut self, line: &[u8]) -> Option<String>`
    Process one explicitly enabled agent request, returning its UTF-8 response.
  - `fn set_control_enabled(&mut self, _enabled: bool)`
    Synchronize runtime visibility and command state when the host changes activation.

- **`fn install(app: Box<dyn WebApplication>) -> bool`**
  Install the owned runtime. Returns false if an app is already registered.

- **`fn persist(path: &str) -> Option<u32>`**
  Request durable origin storage for the current virtual-file revision.

- **`fn download(path: &str) -> Option<u32>`**
  Request a browser download; success means export, not durable origin storage.

- **`fn pick_file(extensions: &str) -> Option<u32>`**
  Request file selection. The host imports selected files before completing the request.

- **`fn request_status(id: u32) -> Option<RequestStatus>`**
  Query an operation. Old terminal results expire when the 256-entry history fills.

- **`fn forget_request(id: u32) -> bool`**
  Remove a completed operation once its result has been consumed.

- **`fn capabilities() -> Capabilities`**
  Services supplied by this browser session, including dynamically granted host services.

- **`fn args() -> Vec<String>`**
  App start arguments copied from the host, with a synthetic executable name first.

- **`fn time_us() -> u64`**
  Monotonic microseconds from the host's performance clock.

- **`fn sleep_ms(_ms: u32)`**
  No blocking sleep occurs in the browser; callers must return to their step loop.

- **`fn env(name: &str) -> Option<String>`**
  Read a per-instance virtual environment setting.

- **`fn getenv(name: &str) -> Option<String>`**
  Alias for the shared environment API.

- **`fn setenv(name: &str, value: Option<&str>) -> bool`**
  Set a per-instance environment value without changing the browser or OS environment.

- **`fn date() -> (Date, bool)`**
  Host-supplied UTC calendar, updated independently from the monotonic clock.

- **`fn unix_time_ns() -> i64`**
  Wall-clock time from the host calendar: nanoseconds since 1970-01-01 00:00 UTC.

- **`fn theme() -> i32`**
  Theme requested by the host: 0 light, 1 dark, or -1 unspecified.

- **`fn cpu_count() -> u32`**
  Available compute workers in a shared-memory bundle, otherwise one app worker.

- **`fn mem_total() -> u64`**
  Current WASM linear-memory allocation; zero when the embedded host does not report RAM.

- **`fn thread_yield()`**
  Cooperative yield is achieved by returning from the current runtime step.

- **`struct Thread`**
  Browser thread handle placeholder; spawning native threads is unsupported.

**`impl Thread`**

- **`fn join(self)`**
  No thread was started.

- **`fn thread_spawn(_stack: usize, _f: Box<dyn FnOnce() + Send>) -> Option<Thread>`**
  Reject native thread creation. Use Job for cooperative background work.

- **`struct Sem(AtomicU32)`**
  Nonblocking counting semaphore used only by portable try-wait paths.

**`impl Sem`**

- **`fn new(initial: u32) -> Option<Self>`**
  Create a logical semaphore inside this worker.
- **`fn post(&self, count: u32)`**
  Add available units.
- **`fn wait(&self, _timeout: i32) -> bool`**
  Try to take a unit; browser execution never blocks on another thread.

- **`fn wake()`**
  Coalesce job/repaint notifications without occupying the bounded input ring.

- **`fn wait(_deadline_us: u64) -> u32`**
  Poll available events; the host schedules future ticks.

- **`fn wait_events(_deadline_us: u64, on_event: &mut dyn FnMut(&Event)) -> u32`**
  Dispatch already queued events without blocking the browser worker.

- **`fn read_stdin(_bytes: &mut [u8]) -> Result<usize, u32>`**
  Browser MessagePort replaces process stdin.

- **`fn file_dialog(_flags: u32, _path: &mut String, _extensions: &[&str]) -> (i32, u32)`**
  The browser uses the shared virtual file dialog; its native synchronous picker is unavailable.

- **`fn print(_job: &mut crate::PrintJob<'_>) -> i32`**
  Native printing is disabled until a browser print adapter has been connected.

- **`fn open_folder(_path: &str) -> i32`**
  Operating-system folder launch is unavailable.

- **`fn open_url(url: &str) -> i32`**
  Request an explicitly selected web URL; the host still enforces user-gesture restrictions.

- **`fn spawn_self(_args: &[&str]) -> i32`**
  Process spawning is unavailable in the browser.

- **`fn pen_devices() -> i32`**
  Number of pen devices is not exposed by browser pointer events.

- **`fn ime_pos(x: i32, y: i32, height: i32)`**
  Update the DOM IME/composition caret position in physical pixels.

- **`fn clip_set_many(entries: &[(u32, &[u8])]) -> bool`**
  Set the local multi-format clipboard; browser system text writes are asynchronous requests.

- **`fn clip_set(format: u32, bytes: &[u8]) -> bool`**
  Set one instance-local clipboard representation.

- **`fn clip_get(format: u32) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`**
  Read an available clipboard representation; the host refreshes text before paste input.

- **`fn clip_has(format: u32) -> bool`**
  Whether an instance-local clipboard representation is present.

- **`extern "C" fn jfgui_buffer(len: u32) -> usize`**
  Reserve the reusable host input buffer (at most 256 MiB); zero means allocation failure.

- **`extern "C" fn jfgui_start(width: i32, height: i32, dpi: i32, args_len: u32) -> i32`**
  Start once. Inbox contains newline-separated arguments, excluding the synthetic program name.

- **`extern "C" fn jfgui_tick(time_ms: u32)`**
  Advance the owned runtime and cooperative jobs, then submit the composed framebuffer.

- **`extern "C" fn jfgui_input(time_ms: u32)`**
  Drain input in order, deferring expensive rasterization until the batch's next tick.

- **`extern "C" fn jfgui_frame_delay() -> u32`**
  Idle host scheduling hint. The host must still wake immediately for new input.

- **`extern "C" fn jfgui_event( typ: u32, button: u32, mods: u32, window: u32, x: i32, y: i32, data: u32, ) -> u32`**
  Enqueue a bounded platform event; pointer/resize events update virtual window routing.

- **`extern "C" fn jfgui_text(len: u32) -> u32`**
  Enqueue committed UTF-8 from the host composition element; uncommitted IME text stays in the DOM.

- **`extern "C" fn jfgui_control(len: u32) -> u32`**
  Process one MessagePort/JavaScript agent request only after explicit activation.

- **`extern "C" fn jfgui_response() -> usize`**
  Address of the current response, valid until the next control request.

- **`fn set_agent_enabled(enabled: bool)`**
  Disable browser control immediately; reactivation must be explicit in application UI/startup.

- **`extern "C" fn jfgui_agent(enabled: u32) -> u32`**
  Explicitly enable or revoke browser automation after app startup.

- **`extern "C" fn jfgui_capabilities(bits: u32)`**
  Grant only browser-supported dynamic services; native plugins/IPC/threads cannot be granted.

- **`extern "C" fn jfgui_date( year: u32, month: u32, day: u32, hour: u32, minute: u32, second: u32, millis: u32, weekday: u32, ) -> u32`**
  Refresh UTC calendar fields, validated before replacing the last host value.

- **`extern "C" fn jfgui_import(path_len: u32, total_len: u32, dropped: u32, durable: u32) -> u32`**
  Import path + file bytes from the inbox. Selected files can be announced through E_DROP.

- **`extern "C" fn jfgui_clipboard(format: u32, len: u32) -> u32`**
  Update a browser clipboard representation before delivering the corresponding paste key.

- **`extern "C" fn jfgui_complete(id: u32, status: i32) -> u32`**
  Confirm a host operation: 0 durable, 1 exported, 2 completed; negatives are explicit errors.

### `plat/web_files.rs`

Source: [`lib/plat/web_files.rs`](../lib/plat/web_files.rs)

```text
Worker-local virtual files. File codecs use their existing byte and streaming APIs;
durable storage is a separate asynchronous request confirmed by the browser host.
```

- **`fn cwd() -> String`**
  Current browser virtual directory; it is unrelated to the host operating system.

- **`fn config_dir() -> Option<String>`**
  Configuration namespace in virtual storage.

- **`fn temp_dir() -> Option<String>`**
  Temporary virtual namespace.

- **`fn home() -> Option<String>`**
  Home namespace for user documents imported or created in this app instance.

- **`fn drives() -> Vec<String>`**
  Browser virtual roots; no native drives are exposed.

- **`fn dev_ino(_path: &str) -> Option<(u64, u64)>`**
  Stable filesystem device/inode identity is not available in browser storage.

- **`struct File`**
  An open virtual file handle; rename or replacement does not invalidate an existing handle.

**`impl File`**

- **`fn read_at(&self, offset: u64, out: &mut [u8]) -> Option<usize>`**
  Read available bytes at an offset, returning zero at EOF.
- **`fn write_at(&self, offset: u64, bytes: &[u8]) -> Option<usize>`**
  Write bytes at an offset, growing with zeroes; reject overflow or read-only handles.
- **`fn size(&self) -> Option<u64>`**
  Current virtual length.
- **`fn sync(&self) -> bool`**
  True only when the current revision has a successful persistence completion.

- **`struct FileLock`**
  Nonblocking exclusive lock inside one browser instance's virtual namespace.

### `plat/web_windows.rs`

Source: [`lib/plat/web_windows.rs`](../lib/plat/web_windows.rs)

```text
Library windows and popups composited into one browser canvas. Every surface is drawn
by the existing software renderer; the browser host receives pixels, not another UI.
```

- **`fn win_create( title: &str, width: i32, height: i32, _flags: u32, _owner: i32, x: i32, y: i32, ) -> i32`**
  Create a window within the app canvas; window zero uses the host viewport.

- **`fn win_destroy(id: i32)`**
  Destroy a library surface and return focus to the main window.

- **`fn win_show(id: i32, mode: u32)`**
  Show or hide a library surface.

- **`fn win_activate(id: i32)`**
  Set keyboard focus to an enabled library window.

- **`fn win_enable(id: i32, enabled: bool)`**
  Enable or disable window input, including modality established by the UI runtime.

- **`fn win_set_rect(id: i32, x: i32, y: i32, width: i32, height: i32)`**
  Change a library surface rectangle, preserving fields equal to PW_KEEP.

- **`fn win_get_rect(id: i32) -> Option<[i32; 4]>`**
  Current canvas-relative client rectangle.

- **`fn win_work_area(_id: i32) -> Option<[i32; 4]>`**
  The browser canvas is the available work area.

- **`fn win_set_title(id: i32, title: &str)`**
  Update a library title and the browser document title for window zero.

- **`fn win_state(id: i32) -> u32`**
  Current library state flags; browser surfaces use library-owned chrome.

- **`fn win_cmd(id: i32, command: u32)`**
  Apply library-window commands; close is delivered to the normal app close workflow.

- **`fn win_begin_move(id: i32)`**
  Begin a pointer-driven move of a non-main library window.

- **`fn win_begin_resize(id: i32, edges: u32)`**
  Begin resizing a library surface inside the browser canvas.

- **`fn win_size(id: i32) -> (i32, i32)`**
  Physical client dimensions.

- **`fn win_dpi(id: i32) -> i32`**
  Current host DPI of a library surface.

- **`fn win_set_min_size(id: i32, width: i32, height: i32)`**
  Minimum drag-resize dimensions in DIP.

- **`fn win_present(id: i32, frame: &Frame<'_>, rects: Option<&[[i32; 4]]>)`**
  Copy an existing software-renderer frame into its composited browser surface.

- **`fn win_set_cursor(_id: i32, shape: u32)`**
  Request one of the shared browser pointer shapes.

- **`fn win_hit_hook(_hook: Option<fn(i32, i32, i32) -> u32>)`**
  Hit testing remains inside the shared UI; no OS nonclient callback is installed.

- **`fn set_refresh(_callback: Option<fn()>)`**
  Native modal resize callbacks are unnecessary for host-stepped browser rendering.

- **`fn cursor_create( _pixels: &[u8], _width: i32, _height: i32, _x: i32, _y: i32, _dpi: i32, ) -> i32`**
  Custom native cursor handles are unavailable; use the shared system-shape cursor mapping.

- **`fn cursor_destroy(_id: i32)`**
  No native custom cursor handle exists.

- **`fn cursor_dpi(dpi: i32) -> i32`**
  Cursor scale follows the canvas DPI.

- **`fn popup(slot: u32, owner: i32, rect: Option<[i32; 4]>, frame: Option<&Frame<'_>>)`**
  Paint an existing library popup into the canvas overlay stack.

### `plat/android_ime.rs`

Source: [`lib/plat/android_ime.rs`](../lib/plat/android_ime.rs)

```text
Android editor snapshots and edits. UTF-8 byte offsets cross JNI; Java owns UTF-16.
This adapter shares the host's single UI thread and never invokes Android under a lock.
```

- **`fn android_ime_publish(id: u32, text: &[u8], anchor: usize, caret: usize, flags: u32)`**
  Publish the focused editor's actual text, flags and UTF-8 selection to Android's IME.

- **`fn android_ime_clear()`**
  Close the Android input session when library focus leaves an editor.

- **`fn android_ime_take(id: u32) -> Option<(Vec<u8>, usize, usize)>`**
  Consume one host edit for the focused widget, including the requested UTF-8 selection.

- **`extern "C" fn jfgui_editor_snapshot() -> u32`**
  Build a JNI snapshot: id, flags, anchor, caret (little-endian u32), then UTF-8 text.

- **`extern "C" fn jfgui_editor_data() -> usize`**
  Snapshot address, valid until the next snapshot call on this same host thread.

- **`extern "C" fn jfgui_editor_replace(id: u32, len: u32, anchor: u32, caret: u32) -> u32`**
  Queue a bounded, valid editor replacement from the shared JNI input buffer.

### `plat/web_threads.rs`

Source: [`lib/plat/web_threads.rs`](../lib/plat/web_threads.rs)

```text
OS primitives for the existing pool.rs scheduler. Each Worker instantiates the
same module and shared memory, with its own __stack_pointer. Only the app worker
enters the UI. LLD's atomic memory initializer runs once across all instances.
```

- **`struct Thread`**
  A browser worker with an exclusively owned stack and a join completion word.

**`impl Thread`**

- **`fn join(self)`**
  Wait until JavaScript has returned from WASM before releasing its stack.

- **`fn spawn(stack: usize, f: Box<dyn FnOnce() + Send>) -> Option<Thread>`**
  Start a closure on a compute worker; return None if the host cannot start it.

- **`struct Sem(AtomicU32)`**
  Counting semaphore backed by WebAssembly atomics and worker-only Atomics.wait.

**`impl Sem`**

- **`fn new(initial: u32) -> Option<Self>`**
  Construct a semaphore with the specified number of available units.
- **`fn post(&self, count: u32)`**
  Publish work and wake up to count sleeping workers.
- **`fn wait(&self, timeout: i32) -> bool`**
  Take a unit; negative timeout waits indefinitely, zero only tries once.

### `plat/clipdrop.rs`

Source: [`lib/plat/clipdrop.rs`](../lib/plat/clipdrop.rs)

```text
clipdrop -- the shared helpers of the clipboard image formats and file drops (every OS)
                                    [src/plat/clipdrop.s; docs/modules/CLIPBOARD_DND.md]

  drop_reset()                 start a new pending drop list (the old one is dropped)
  drop_add(path) -> bool       one path (empty, too long, NUL inside or too many: false)
  drop_uri_list(data) -> n     text/uri-list: every file:// URI (host empty or "localhost"),
                               %XX decoded, '#' lines skipped
  drop_take() -> paths         hand the pending list over (E_DROP data = its count)
  clip_dib_to_bmp(dib) -> BMP file   a clipboard DIB (BITMAPCOREHEADER / INFO / V4 / V5
       header, masks, colour table, pixels) as a BMP file, None when the header is
       invalid.  32-bit BI_RGB / BI_BITFIELDS / BI_ALPHABITFIELDS DIBs are rewritten with
       a V5 header (BI_BITFIELDS, the source masks) and paint.net's alpha rule: the alpha
       channel (the 4th byte of BI_RGB, else the header's alpha mask) is used when any
       pixel has a non-zero alpha, else the image is opaque (alpha mask 0).  Other depths
       are copied with a BITMAPFILEHEADER in front.
  clip_bmp_fix(bmp) -> BMP file   the same rule for a BMP file (image/bmp of other programs)

Every length is checked: clipboard / drop data come from other programs.  The pending
list keeps raw bytes (a decoded URI need not be UTF-8); drop_take converts lossily.
```

- **`fn drop_reset()`**
  forget the pending list

- **`fn drop_add(path: &str) -> bool`**
  one path (copied) -> false when it is empty, too long, has a NUL or the list is full

- **`fn drop_uri_list(data: &[u8]) -> u32`**
  text/uri-list -> paths added

- **`fn drop_take() -> Vec<String>`**
  hand the pending list over (it starts empty again)

- **`fn clip_dib_to_bmp(dib: &[u8]) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`**
  a clipboard DIB as a BMP file (None: invalid header)

- **`fn clip_bmp_fix(bmp: &[u8]) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`**
  a BMP file of another program with the same alpha rule

- **`fn clipimg_pack(png: &[u8], bmp: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8>`**
  the CLIPIMG bytes of a PNG file and a BMP file (either may be empty)

- **`fn clipimg_split(data: &[u8]) -> Option<(&[u8], &[u8])>`**
  (PNG, BMP) of CLIPIMG bytes (an absent part is empty; None: malformed)

### `plat/cliprich.rs`

Source: [`lib/plat/cliprich.rs`](../lib/plat/cliprich.rs)

```text
cliprich -- bounded rich clipboard records and Windows CF_HTML framing.
New opt-in API by clipboard_platform; no assembly counterpart.
CF_HTML: learn.microsoft.com/windows/win32/dataxchg/html-clipboard-format
```

```rust
/// Maximum combined payload of an opt-in multi-format clipboard write (64 MiB).
CLIP_RICH_MAX: usize = 64 << 20
```

- **`fn clip_many_valid(entries: &[(u32, &[u8])]) -> bool`**
  Validate a multi-format text clipboard before replacing the previous contents.
  Formats must be distinct TEXT/HTML/RTF/DOCX; text and HTML are NUL-free UTF-8.

- **`fn clip_html_encode(fragment: &[u8]) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`**
  Wrap a UTF-8 HTML fragment in Windows CF_HTML, with byte offsets and final NUL.

- **`fn clip_html_decode(data: &[u8]) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`**
  Extract a UTF-8 fragment from CF_HTML. Reject invalid/overflowing offsets and UTF-8.

### `plat/consts.rs`

Source: [`lib/plat/consts.rs`](../lib/plat/consts.rs)

```text
consts -- the platform constants of jfgui.inc / ui.inc shared by every OS layer and the UI
                                                       [ASMLIB/inc/jfgui.inc, ui.inc]
```

*events (Event::typ)*

```rust
E_NONE: u8 = 0
E_MOVE: u8 = 1
E_DOWN: u8 = 2  // btn
E_UP: u8 = 3  // btn
E_WHEEL: u8 = 4  // data = (i16 dy) | (i16 dx << 16), 120 per notch, up / right positive
E_KEY_DOWN: u8 = 5  // data = VK | (repeat << 31)
E_KEY_UP: u8 = 6  // data = VK
E_TEXT: u8 = 7  // data = Unicode codepoint
E_RESIZE: u8 = 8  // x = width, y = height (physical pixels)
E_DPI: u8 = 9  // data = dpi (96 = 100 %)
E_FOCUS_IN: u8 = 10
E_FOCUS_OUT: u8 = 11
E_LEAVE: u8 = 12  // pointer left the window
E_CLOSE: u8 = 13  // user asked to close the window
E_WAKE: u8 = 14  // generic wake-up (timer, repaint request)
E_PAINT: u8 = 15  // the OS needs the window content again
E_WINPOS: u8 = 16  // window moved: x, y = client origin in screen px
E_DROP: u8 = 17  // files dropped: x, y = position, data = count (drop_take)
E_PREEDIT: u8 = 18  // ime_preedit(win), uncommitted text
E_IME_COMMIT: u8 = 19  // data = bounded ime_commit_take token
EVQ_CAP: usize = 256  // event ring capacity (power of two)
```

*pointer types, buttons, modifiers*

```rust
PTR_MOUSE: u32 = 0
PTR_PEN: u32 = 1  // pen tip
PTR_ERASER: u32 = 2  // eraser end of a pen
PTR_TOUCH: u32 = 3
BTN_LEFT: u8 = 1
BTN_RIGHT: u8 = 2
```

### `plat/evq.rs`

Source: [`lib/plat/evq.rs`](../lib/plat/evq.rs)

```text
evq -- the platform event ring (shared by every platform and the script runner)
                                                                       [src/plat/evq.s]

  evq_push(ev) -> false when the ring is full (event dropped)
  evq_pop() -> Some(event)        evq_count()        evq_clear()
  evq_pen_data(pressure, type, tilt_x, tilt_y) -> u32   (PN1) Event::data of a pointer
       event: pressure -1 = the device has none (-> 0), else clamped to 1..PRESSURE_ONE;
       type PTR_*; tilts in degrees, clamped to -63..63.  0 for PTR_MOUSE and while pen
       input is off.
  pen_enable(on)    pen input on (default) / off: off = pens report plain mouse events
  pen_enabled() -> bool

The OS layer pushes from its message handlers, the UI core pops between frames (one
thread; the lock only makes the static safe).
```

```rust
PD_PRESSURE: u32 = 0xFFFF  // bits 0..15 pressure 1..PRESSURE_ONE, 0 = none (full)
PD_TYPE_SHIFT: u32 = 16  // bits 16..17 PTR_*
PD_TILTX_SHIFT: u32 = 18  // bits 18..24 i7 tilt x in degrees (-63..63, + = right)
PD_TILTY_SHIFT: u32 = 25  // bits 25..31 i7 tilt y in degrees (+ = towards the user)
PRESSURE_ONE: i32 = 65535
```

- **`fn evq_push(e: Event) -> bool`**
  append an event; false when the ring is full (the event is dropped)

- **`fn evq_pop() -> Option<Event>`**
  the oldest queued event

- **`fn evq_count() -> u32`**

- **`fn evq_clear()`**

- **`fn evq_pen_data(pressure: i32, typ: u32, tilt_x: i32, tilt_y: i32) -> u32`**
  Event::data of a pen pointer event (0 for a mouse or while pen input is off)

- **`fn pen_enable(on: bool)`**
  pen input on (default) / off (pens become plain mouse events)

- **`fn pen_enabled() -> bool`**

### `plat/icc.rs`

Source: [`lib/plat/icc.rs`](../lib/plat/icc.rs)

```text
icc -- the colour profile of the monitor a window is on
         [src/plat/win/win_icc.s, src/plat/linux/lx_icc.s; docs/modules/COLOR_MANAGEMENT.md]

  monitor_icc(w) -> profile bytes / None
       Windows: the ICC profile of the monitor that shows window w (0 = the main window;
       other windows use the main window's monitor; ICC_MON_PRIMARY (-1) = the primary
       monitor, no window needed): GetMonitorInfoW -> CreateDCW(device) -> GetICMProfileW
       -> the file.  No window (headless) -> None.
       Linux X11: the root window property _ICC_PROFILE (ICC Profiles in X Specification;
       set by colord / xiccd / dispwin for screen 0); profiles above 1 MB are ignored.
       Wayland: None -- the core protocol has no colour management (the compositor shows
       sRGB), so callers assume sRGB.  No display (headless): None.
       Every profile is validated (icc_check: header, 'acsp', version 2..5, tag table).
  monitor_key(w) -> u64   Windows: the HMONITOR of that monitor (0 = none) -- cheap; poll
       it every frame and query the profile again when it changes (window moved to another
       monitor).  Linux: 0 (the X11 property is per screen, not per monitor).

icc_check is a private copy of img_core::icc::icc_check (plat does not depend on the image
crates).  Linux: the X11 requests go through linux_x11.rs (x11s::root_property, marked
plat-shared there).
```

```rust
/// monitor_icc / monitor_key: the primary monitor instead of a window's
ICC_MON_PRIMARY: i32 = -1
```

- **`fn monitor_icc(w: i32) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`**
  the ICC profile of the monitor showing window w (validated), None = assume sRGB
  [plat_monitor_icc]

- **`fn monitor_key(w: i32) -> u64`**
  a cheap key of the monitor showing window w (poll it every frame; 0 = unknown)
  [plat_monitor_key]

```text
pwin0 -- the single-window platform API on top of the window-indexed win_* functions
(window 0 = the main window), and the "current" window of the calls a UI pass makes
                                                                     [src/plat/pwin0.s]

  win_use(w)                       the window set_cursor / window_state / window_cmd /
                                   begin_move / begin_resize refer to (-1 = none)
  win_current() -> i32             that window
  window_create(title, w_dip, h_dip, af_flags) -> bool   window 0 (AF_* flags), shown
  set_min_size(w_dip, h_dip)       window 0 (before or after create)
  window_size() -> (w, h)          window 0, client pixels
  dpi() -> i32   set_title(utf8)   present(frame, rects)     window 0
  set_cursor(shape)  window_state() -> WST_*  window_cmd(cmd)  begin_move()
  begin_resize(edges)              the current window

The OS layers (win_api.rs; linux_disp.rs -> Wayland / X11) implement the win_* calls.
```

- **`fn win_use(w: i32)`**
  the window the single-window per-window calls refer to (-1 = none)

- **`fn win_current() -> i32`**
  the window set by win_use (0 by default)

- **`fn window_create(title: &str, w_dip: i32, h_dip: i32, af: u32) -> bool`**
  the main window (AF_CUSTOM_TITLEBAR: no system caption; AF_RESIZABLE), shown at once

- **`fn set_title(title: &str)`**

- **`fn present(f: &Frame, rects: Option<&[[i32; 4]]>)`**

- **`fn window_cmd(cmd: u32)`**
