### `plat/plib.rs`

Source: [`lib/plat/plib.rs`](../lib/plat/plib.rs)

```text
plib -- plugin libraries and the crash guard: the portable part
                                       [src/plat/plib.s; docs/modules/PLUGIN_LOADER.md]

  lib_open(path) -> Lib / Err(LIBE_*)    Windows: LoadLibraryExW without DllMain / imports
                                         (win_lib.rs); Linux: own ELF ET_DYN loader
                                         (linux_elfload.rs)
  lib_sym(h, name) -> address | 0        exported symbol (own export / .dynsym lookup)
  lib_close(h)                           unload (also when a Lib is dropped)
  lib_seg(h, addr) -> (end of the image segment holding addr (0 = outside), LIBA_*)
                                         -- check every pointer a plugin hands over (data:
                                         LIBA_R, code: LIBA_X) before dereferencing it
  guard_init()                           install the OS fault handler once (VEH on
                                         Windows, SIGSEGV / SIGBUS / SIGILL / SIGFPE /
                                         SIGTRAP on Linux; linux_guard.rs)
  guard_call(f, a1, a2, a3, a4, info) -> (rax, rdx)
       calls the code at address f with the Win64 ABI (on Linux too: the plugin ABI) as
       f(a1, a2, a3, a4); a fault inside it on this thread (also in nested host code)
       resumes here with rax = rdx = ERR_FAULT and info (GuardInfo) filled; otherwise
       info.kind = 0 and rax / rdx are those of f.  Nested calls are allowed.  ERR_LIMIT
       when the thread table is full (GT_MAX threads).
  guard_tag() -> u64                     info.tag of this thread's innermost active guard
                                         (0 = none)

guard_call pushes a guard record (rsp / rbp of its trampoline, xmm6-15, MXCSR, x87
control word, tag) and links it as the innermost guard of the calling thread in a table
{OS thread id -> record} (thread id = TEB ClientId on Windows, gettid on Linux).  The
handlers find the thread's entry by its id, unlink the innermost record (a second fault
goes to the enclosing guard), store the fault into it and resume the thread at
jfgui_guard_fault_ip with rsp / rbp of the trampoline's body.  That code restores x87,
MXCSR, DF and xmm6-15 and returns through the epilogue, which restores the caller's
callee-saved registers.  NOT a sandbox: the guard catches faults, nothing else (see the
docs: corrupt state stays corrupt, hangs are not detected).

Safety contract: guard_call runs untrusted machine code at an arbitrary address; it is a
safe function only in the sense the assembly API is: the caller hands it addresses of
loaded plugin code (lib_sym) or of its own `extern "win64"` functions.  Pointers a plugin
returns are checked with lib_seg before the host reads through them.
```

```rust
ERR_FAULT: i32 = -64  // the guarded call faulted (plugin disabled)
LIBA_R: u32 = 1  // lib_seg flags
LIBA_W: u32 = 2
LIBA_X: u32 = 4
LIBE_IO: u32 = 1  // file missing / unreadable / larger than LIB_FILE_MAX
LIBE_FORMAT: u32 = 2  // not an x86-64 ELF shared object / PE DLL, or damaged
LIBE_DEPS: u32 = 3  // needs other libraries / undefined symbols
LIBE_RELOC: u32 = 4  // unsupported relocation
LIBE_NOMEM: u32 = 5
LIBE_INIT: u32 = 6  // TLS, initialisers or an interpreter (not supported)
LIBE_MAP: u32 = 7  // mmap / mprotect failed
LIB_FILE_MAX: u64 = 64 << 20
LH_SEGMAX: usize = 16
GK_NONE: u32 = 0
GK_ACCESS: u32 = 1  // access violation / SIGSEGV
GK_ILLEGAL: u32 = 2  // illegal / privileged instruction / SIGILL
GK_ARITH: u32 = 3  // division by zero, overflow, FP trap / SIGFPE
GK_BUS: u32 = 4  // misalignment / SIGBUS
GK_BREAK: u32 = 5  // breakpoint (int3) / SIGTRAP
GK_STACK: u32 = 6  // stack overflow (Windows)
GT_MAX: usize = 128
```

- **`struct LibSeg`**
  one segment of a loaded image: [start, end), LIBA_\*
  - `start: usize`
  - `end: usize`
  - `flags: u32`

- **`struct Lib`**
  a loaded plugin library (LIBH)

**`impl Lib`**

- **`fn base(&self) -> usize`**
  the image base
- **`fn span(&self) -> usize`**
  bytes mapped from the base
- **`fn segs(&self) -> &[LibSeg]`**
  the segments (Windows: the headers, then every section)

- **`fn lib_close(h: Lib)`**
  unload a library (dropping the Lib does the same)  [plat_lib_close]

- **`fn lib_seg(h: &Lib, addr: usize) -> (usize, u32)`**
  the end of the segment of h holding addr (0 = none) and its LIBA_\* flags  [plat_lib_seg]

- **`struct GuardInfo`**
  GUARDINFO: what a guarded call hit; tag is an input (guard_tag)
  - `kind: u32` -- GK_\* (0 = no fault)
  - `code: u32` -- NTSTATUS (Windows) / signal number (Linux)
  - `pc: u64` -- faulting instruction
  - `addr: u64` -- faulting data address (access faults) or 0
  - `tag: u64` -- in: tag of this guard (guard_tag)

- **`fn guard_init() -> i32`**
  install the fault handler (idempotent, thread safe)  [plat_guard_init]

- **`fn guard_tag() -> u64`**
  info.tag of the innermost active guard of this thread (0 = none)  [plat_guard_tag]

- **`fn guard_call(f: usize, a1: u64, a2: u64, a3: u64, a4: u64, info: Option<&mut GuardInfo>) -> (u64, u64)`**
  f(a1, a2, a3, a4) under the crash guard (see the file header)  [plat_guard_call]

- **`fn guard_run<R, F: FnOnce() -> R>(f: F, info: Option<&mut GuardInfo>) -> Result<R, i32>`**
  run the Rust closure f under the crash guard -> Ok(result), Err(ERR_FAULT) when it faulted
  (info filled), Err(ERR_LIMIT) when the thread table is full (f did not run).  A fault
  abandons f's frames without running destructors (what they own leaks): meant for host
  code that may touch plugin memory (the loader fuzz runs lib_open / lib_sym under it).

- **`fn guard_read(addr: usize, len: usize) -> Option<alloc::vec::Vec<u8>>`**
  copy len bytes at any address under the crash guard -> None when addr is 0, the copy
  faulted or the memory for the copy is missing  (pn-sys)

- **`fn guard_write(addr: usize, data: &[u8]) -> bool`**
  write data at any address under the crash guard -> false when addr is 0 or it faulted
  (pn-sys)

- **`fn lib_read(h: &Lib, addr: usize, len: usize) -> Option<alloc::vec::Vec<u8>>`**
  copy len bytes at addr of the image of h -- only when they lie in one readable segment
  (lib_seg), else None (pointers a plugin hands over)

- **`fn guard_test_code(k: u32) -> usize`**
  (test aid) the address of guard test function k (see above), 0 for an unknown k

- **`fn guard_test_state() -> (u32, u32, bool)`**
  (test aid) this thread's MXCSR, x87 control word and direction flag

### `plat/print.rs`

Source: [`lib/plat/print.rs`](../lib/plat/print.rs)

```text
print -- printing, the OS-independent part: paper sizes, page layout, rows over white, the
job as a one-page PDF  [src/plat/print.s, src/img/pdfwrite.s; docs/modules/PRINT.md]

  print(job) -> 0 printed / ERR_CANCEL / ERR_UNSUPPORTED / ERR_IO / ERR_NOMEM / ERR_DIMS
       the OS part: win_print.rs (PrintDlgExW + GDI), linux_print.rs (portal Print + PDF)
  page_init(paper, flags) -> PPage         the paper PAPER_* in pt (PJF_LANDSCAPE swaps
                                           width / height), no unprintable edge
  layout(job, page) -> Place               the image rectangle and the printable area on
                                           the page (pt, from the top-left)
  row_white(dst, src, n, mode)             n pixels (B, G, R, A) -> n * 3 bytes composited
                                           over white (PRW_* mode bits)
  print_pdf(job, page) -> PDF bytes / ERR_*  the job laid out on page as a one-page PDF
                                           (job.page_w / page_h set)
  print_dpi(unit, dpu) -> f64              pixels per inch from a resolution in unit (0 none
                                           -> 96, 1 inch, 2 cm)
  pdf_write_image(src, opt) -> PDF bytes / ERR_*   one image on one PDF page (pdfwrite.s)
  set_print_deflate(f)                     the zlib compressor of the PDF streams

Deviation (plat-shared): plat must not depend on the image crates, so pdfwrite.s is ported
here and the one thing it took from img -- zlib_deflate -- comes through a hook.  An app
sets it to img_core::deflate::zlib_deflate (then the PDF is byte-identical to the
assembly's); without a hook the streams are zlib "stored" blocks (a valid, uncompressed
PDF).  The source is a PrintSrc (rows of B, G, R, A bytes; the gfx::Surface fields)
instead of a SURFACE pointer.
```

*results (the ERR_\* of jfgui.inc / plugin.inc that plat returns)*

```rust
ERR_NOMEM: i32 = -8
ERR_UNSUPPORTED: i32 = -9
ERR_DIMS: i32 = -12
ERR_IO: i32 = -13
ERR_CANCEL: i32 = -14
ERR_LIMIT: i32 = -15
```

*the source formats (FMT_\* of jfgui.inc)*

```rust
PRINT_XRGB: u8 = 0  // alpha byte ignored
PRINT_PARGB: u8 = 1  // premultiplied
PRINT_ARGB: u8 = 3  // straight
```

*PrintJob::flags*

```rust
PJF_LANDSCAPE: u32 = 1  // Querformat
PJF_CENTER: u32 = 2  // centre in the printable area (else its top-left corner)
PJF_SMASK: u32 = 4  // PDF only: straight colours + alpha as /SMask (no white)
```

*PrintJob::scale*

```rust
PJS_FIT: u32 = 0  // as large as the printable area allows (aspect kept)
PJS_DPI: u32 = 1  // original size: pixels / DPI inches
PJS_PERCENT: u32 = 2  // PrintJob::percent of the original size
```

*paper*

```rust
PAPER_A4: u32 = 0
PAPER_LETTER: u32 = 1
PAPER_A5: u32 = 2
PAPER_LEGAL: u32 = 3
PAPER_A3: u32 = 4
PAPER_N: u32 = 5
```

*row_white modes*

```rust
PRW_BGR: u32 = 1  // B, G, R byte order (GDI DIB) instead of R, G, B (PDF)
PRW_PREMUL: u32 = 2  // source is premultiplied
PRW_COPY: u32 = 4  // straight colours unchanged (no white; PDF with SMask)
PRW_OPAQUE: u32 = 8  // alpha ignored (XRGB)
```

- **`struct PrintSrc<'a>`**
  the pixels of a print job: rows of 32-bit B, G, R, A (the fields of gfx::Surface)
  - `px: &'a [u8]`
  - `w: i32`
  - `h: i32`
  - `stride: i32`
  - `fmt: u8` -- PRINT_ARGB (straight), PRINT_PARGB or PRINT_XRGB

- **`struct PrintJob<'a>`**
  one print job (PRINTJOB)
  - `src: Option<PrintSrc<'a>>`
  - `title: &'a str` -- document name (spooler job, PDF /Title); "" = "Bild" (no PDF /Title)
  - `dpi_x: f64` -- image pixels per inch (&lt;= 0: 96)
  - `dpi_y: f64` -- (&lt;= 0: dpi_x)
  - `flags: u32` -- PJF_\*
  - `scale: u32` -- PJS_\*
  - `percent: u32` -- PJS_PERCENT: 1..10000 % of the size by DPI
  - `margin: u32` -- on every side, 1/100 mm (the printer's unprintable edge wins when larger)
  - `paper: u32` -- PAPER_\* suggested to the dialog / used without one
  - `copies: u32` -- copies (informative, 0 = unknown)
  - `page_w: f64` -- page size in pt the job was laid out on
  - `page_h: f64`

**`impl<'a> PrintJob<'a>`**

- **`fn new(src: Option<PrintSrc<'a>>, title: &'a str) -> PrintJob<'a>`**

- **`struct PPage`**
  the page (PPAGE, pt = 1/72 inch)
  - `w: f64` -- page size (orientation applied)
  - `h: f64`
  - `l: f64` -- unprintable edge left / top / right / bottom
  - `t: f64`
  - `r: f64`
  - `b: f64`
  - `token: u32` -- print system cookie (portal token)
  - `flags: u32` -- PJF_LANDSCAPE as chosen in the dialog

- **`struct Place`**
  where the image goes (PLACE, pt from the page's top-left)
  - `x: f64` -- image rectangle
  - `y: f64`
  - `w: f64`
  - `h: f64`
  - `ax: f64` -- printable area (margins applied); the image is clipped to it
  - `ay: f64`
  - `aw: f64`
  - `ah: f64`

- **`struct PdfOpt<'a>`**
  PDFOPT of pdf_write_image
  - `page_w: f64` -- MediaBox in pt
  - `page_h: f64`
  - `x: f64` -- image rectangle, pt from the page's top-left
  - `y: f64`
  - `w: f64`
  - `h: f64`
  - `cx: f64` -- clip rectangle (cw &lt;= 0: no clip)
  - `cy: f64`
  - `cw: f64`
  - `ch: f64`
  - `title: &'a str` -- /Title ("" = none)
  - `flags: u32` -- PJF_SMASK
  - `level: i32` -- deflate level (&lt;= 0: 6)

- **`fn page_init(paper: u32, flags: u32) -> PPage`**
  the paper in pt, PJF_LANDSCAPE swaps; no unprintable edge  [print_page_init]

- **`fn print_dpi(unit: u32, dpu: f64) -> f64`**
  pixels per inch from a document resolution: unit 0 none -> 96, 1 inch, 2 cm (\* 2.54);
  dpu &lt;= 0 -> 96  [print_dpi]

- **`fn layout(job: &PrintJob, page: &PPage) -> Place`**
  the image rectangle and the printable area on the page  [print_layout]

- **`fn row_white(dst: &mut [u8], src: &[u8], n: usize, mode: u32)`**
  n pixels of src (B, G, R, A) -> n \* 3 bytes of dst (R, G, B; PRW_BGR: B, G, R).  Straight:
  c \* a / 255 + 255 - a (rounded); PRW_PREMUL: c + 255 - a; PRW_COPY / PRW_OPAQUE: c
  [print_row_white]

- **`fn print_pdf(job: &mut PrintJob, page: &PPage) -> Result<Vec<u8>, i32>`**
  the job laid out on page as a one-page PDF (job.page_w / page_h set)  [print_pdf]

- **`type DeflateFn = fn(&[u8], i32) -> Vec<u8>`**
  a zlib compressor: (data, level) -> zlib stream (img_core::deflate::zlib_deflate)

- **`fn set_print_deflate(f: Option<DeflateFn>)`**
  the compressor of the PDF streams (None: zlib stored blocks, no compression)

- **`fn pdf_write_image(src: &PrintSrc, opt: &PdfOpt) -> Result<Vec<u8>, i32>`**
  one image on one PDF 1.4 page: objects 1 Catalog, 2 Pages, 3 Page, 4 Contents, 5 Image
  (/DeviceRGB 8 bit /FlateDecode, composited over white -- with PJF_SMASK and a straight
  source: straight colours), 6 Info, [7 /SMask alpha]; classic xref  [pdf_write_image]

### `plat/modhost.rs`

Source: [`lib/plat/modhost.rs`](../lib/plat/modhost.rs)

```text
modhost -- loadable modules, the host side (jxl-module): the JPEG XL writer module jxlenc
                                                                      [jxl-module: no asm]

  jxlenc_set_path(Some(path) | None)   the module file: None = <exe folder>/jxlenc.dll
                             (Linux jxlenc.so); "off" = none.  Before the first use.
  jxlenc_ready() -> bool     the module is loaded (loads it on the first call and keeps it;
                             a missing / refused file stays missing for the process)
  jxlenc_file() -> path      the file the module was (or would be) loaded from
  jxlenc_encode(req) -> Some(file / ERR_*) | None (no module)
  module_at(addr) -> Some((file name, image base))   the loaded module holding addr (crash
                             reports: offsets into its .map; allocation-free)

The module (modules/jxlenc) is a no_std cdylib without imports, loaded with the plugin
loader (lib_open / lib_sym: win_lib.rs, linux_elfload.rs -- the same checks), its one export
jxlenc_module(&HostTable) -> &ModTable (modabi.rs: the records, the memory and thread rules).
It is our own code, called without a guard: a fault inside it is a crash with the normal
report, a panic comes back through HostTable::panic into rt::crash::report.  The JPEG XL
specifics (what JxlReq carries) are img_jxl::writer's; this file only moves the bytes.
jfgui_JXLENC_TEST=panic | fault (tests) makes the module panic / fault in its encode.
```

```rust
/// the module's file name next to the exe
JXLENC_FILE: &str = "jxlenc.dll"
/// the module's file name next to the exe
JXLENC_FILE: &str = "jxlenc.so"
```

- **`fn module_at(addr: u64) -> Option<(&'static str, u64)>`**
  the loaded module holding addr: (its file name, its image base) -- for crash reports (no
  lock, no allocation)

- **`fn jxlenc_set_path(path: Option<String>)`**
  the module file to load (None = the default next to the exe, "off" = none); resets an
  earlier failed attempt, never a loaded module

- **`fn current_exe() -> Option<String>`**
  Absolute path of the running native executable, independent of argv[0].

- **`fn jxlenc_file() -> Option<String>`**
  the file the module is (or would be) loaded from; None = "off" / no exe folder

- **`fn jxlenc_ready() -> bool`**
  the module is loaded (the first call loads it)

- **`struct JxlEncReq<'a>`**
  a JPEG XL encode for the module (img_jxl::writer): the samples (owned: they move to the
  module) and the writer's options
  - `w: u32`
  - `h: u32`
  - `gray: bool`
  - `alpha: bool`
  - `rgba: Vec<u8>`
  - `effort: u32`
  - `quality: u32`
  - `flags: u32`
  - `icc: Option<&'a [u8]>`
  - `exif: Option<&'a [u8]>`
  - `xmp: Option<&'a [u8]>`
  - `cancel: Option<&'a AtomicU32>`

- **`fn jxlenc_encode(req: JxlEncReq) -> Option<Result<Vec<u8>, i32>>`**
  the module's writer on req: Some(file / ERR_\*), None without a module

### `plat/modabi.rs`

Source: [`lib/plat/modabi.rs`](../lib/plat/modabi.rs)

```text
modabi -- the ABI of loadable modules (jxl-module): the records the host (modhost.rs) and a
module (the module runtime modules/modrt, which includes this file) exchange
                                                                      [jxl-module: no asm]

A module is a no_std cdylib linked against nothing (no imports; built and checked like a
Rust PaintCraft plugin, build_rs.py section jxl-module) that exports exactly one function

    <name>_module(host: *const HostTable) -> *const ModTable          extern "win64"

(`jxlenc_module` for the JPEG XL writer).  The host keeps the module loaded for the rest of
the process; the module keeps the HostTable pointer.  Null = the module refuses the host
(another MOD_VERSION).  Every function pointer is extern "win64" on both systems (as the
plugin ABI), so the records are the same on Windows and Linux.

Memory: the module's global allocator is the host's (HostTable::alloc / free = the host's
GlobalAlloc with the same Layout), so blocks move between both sides as Vec<u8> raw parts
(JxlReq::rgba to the module, JxlRes to the host) without a copy.
Threads: parallel_for runs item(ctx, index, job) on the host's worker pool (and the calling
thread); thr_count / thr_self are the pool's, so per-thread arrays of the module work as in
the host.  A panic of the module ends in HostTable::panic: the host's normal crash report
(stderr "panic: jxlenc: ...", the crash hook, exit code 3); a fault inside the module code
goes to the host's crash handlers like any other.

Version 1.  Layouts are asserted below.
```

```rust
/// ModTable::magic ("MODM" little endian)
MOD_MAGIC: u32 = 0x4D44_4F4D
/// the ABI version of this file
MOD_VERSION: u32 = 1
/// ModTable::kind of the JPEG XL writer
MOD_JXLENC: u32 = 1
/// JxlReq::blobs: which of the blobs are present (None vs Some)
JXB_ICC: u32 = 1
JXB_EXIF: u32 = 2
JXB_XMP: u32 = 4
/// JxlReq::test: crash tests of the module (plat::jxlenc_*: jfgui_JXLENC_TEST=panic / fault)
JXT_PANIC: u32 = 1
JXT_FAULT: u32 = 2
```

- **`type ItemFn = extern "win64" fn(ctx: *const c_void, index: usize, job: *const c_void)`**
  one item of a parallel loop: item(ctx, index, job)

- **`struct HostTable`**
  the host's functions (the module keeps the pointer)
  - `size: u32` -- size_of::&lt;HostTable>()
  - `version: u32` -- MOD_VERSION
  - `cpu: u32` -- the host's rt::cpu::features() (--simd sse2: 0, the module then uses SSE2 too)
  - `threads: u32` -- workers of the host's pool
  - `alloc: extern "win64" fn(size: usize, align: usize, zeroed: u32) -> *mut u8` -- a block of `size` bytes aligned to `align` from the host's allocator (zeroed != 0:
    cleared), null when out of memory
  - `free: extern "win64" fn(p: *mut u8, size: usize, align: usize)` -- free a block of alloc with its size and alignment
  - `parallel_for: extern "win64" fn(count: usize, ctx: *const c_void, item: ItemFn) -> u32` -- item(ctx, i, job) for i in 0..count on the pool; 0 = done, 1 = cancelled
  - `job_cancel: extern "win64" fn(job: *const c_void)` -- job.cancel() of the job an item got
  - `job_cancelled: extern "win64" fn(job: *const c_void) -> u32` -- job.cancelled()
  - `thr_count: extern "win64" fn() -> u32` -- the pool's thr_count() / thr_self()
  - `thr_self: extern "win64" fn() -> usize`
  - `yield_now: extern "win64" fn()` -- give the time slice away (spin locks)
  - `log: extern "win64" fn(msg: *const u8, len: usize)` -- one line on stderr ("&lt;module>: msg")
  - `panic: extern "win64" fn(msg: *const u8, len: usize) -> !` -- a panic of the module (UTF-8 message): the host's crash report, exit code 3

- **`struct ModTable`**
  what a module offers
  - `size: u32` -- size_of::&lt;ModTable>()
  - `version: u32` -- MOD_VERSION
  - `magic: u32` -- MOD_MAGIC
  - `kind: u32` -- MOD_JXLENC
  - `jxl_encode: extern "win64" fn(req: *const JxlReq, res: *mut JxlRes) -> i32` -- the JPEG XL writer: 0 and res filled, or ERR_\* (res empty)

- **`struct JxlReq`**
  a JPEG XL encode: the samples of img_jxl::writer::image_of and the writer's options
  - `size: u32` -- size_of::&lt;JxlReq>()
  - `w: u32`
  - `h: u32`
  - `gray: u32` -- Px::gray
  - `alpha: u32` -- Px::alpha
  - `effort: u32`
  - `quality: u32`
  - `flags: u32` -- IO_FLAGS
  - `blobs: u32` -- JXB_\*: which blobs are present
  - `test: u32` -- JXT_\* (0)
  - `rgba: *mut u8` -- Px::rgba (R, G, B, A per pixel) as Vec raw parts: the module owns it from now on
  - `rgba_len: usize`
  - `rgba_cap: usize`
  - `icc: *const u8`
  - `icc_len: usize`
  - `exif: *const u8`
  - `exif_len: usize`
  - `xmp: *const u8`
  - `xmp_len: usize`
  - `cancel: *const u32` -- the cancel flag (an AtomicU32), null = none

- **`struct JxlRes`**
  the file the writer made, as Vec raw parts (the host owns it from now on)
  - `data: *mut u8`
  - `len: usize`
  - `cap: usize`

## base

*UTF-8, command line, settings files, numbers and text* -- crate root [`lib/base/lib.rs`](../lib/base/lib.rs)

```text
base -- portable basics above the runtime and the platform     [src/core/str.s, args.s, ini.s]

  utf8_decode(s, i) -> (codepoint, next)    invalid bytes decode as themselves
  Args: value(name) / flag(name) / int(name, default) over the command line
  num:  f64_parse / f64_format (bit-identical to ASMLIB), parse_i64
  ini:  Ini -- settings files (load / parse / save / get* / set* / remove)

Strings and formatting are Rust's own (str, String, format!); the thread pool, cpu
features and files are in plat (they need unsafe).
```

### `base` crate root

- **`fn utf8_decode(s: &[u8], i: usize) -> (u32, usize)`**
  UTF-8 decoding: (codepoint, index after it); an invalid / overlong / surrogate / too large
  sequence decodes as U+FFFD and advances one byte  [src/core/str.s utf8_decode]

- **`struct Args(pub Vec<String>)`**
  the command line (argument 0 is the program)

**`impl Args`**

- **`fn value(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&str>`**
  the argument after `name` (`--shot FILE`); argument 0 (the program) is skipped
- **`fn flag(&self, name: &str) -> bool`**
- **`fn int(&self, name: &str, def: i32) -> i32`**
  a decimal value, `def` when missing or invalid

### `base/ini.rs`

Source: [`lib/base/ini.rs`](../lib/base/ini.rs)

```text
ini -- settings files: UTF-8 "[section]" / "key=value" text              [src/core/ini.s]

  Ini::new() / Ini::load(path) / Ini::parse(bytes)    a missing or unreadable file is empty
  ini.save(path) -> bool       plat::write_file, creates missing directories
  ini.to_bytes()               the text save writes
  ini.get(sec, key) -> Option<&str>   (get_bytes for values that are not UTF-8)
  ini.get_int(sec, key, def)   decimal (+/-), 0x hex, true/false/yes/no/on/off; else def
  ini.get_f64(sec, key, def)   '.' decimal point only; else def
  ini.set(sec, key, value) / set_int / set_f64 -> bool   add or replace (false: bad name)
  ini.remove(sec, key) -> bool
  ini.section(sec) -> Vec<(&str, &str)>   the keys of a section and their values, in order

Format: one entry per line; sec "" = the keys before the first [section].  Section and
key names compare ASCII case-insensitively; values are stored as written (blanks around
names and values trimmed; no quoting, no inline comments, so values may contain ';' and
'#').  Lines starting with ';' or '#', blank lines and malformed lines (no '=', "[" without
"]", NUL bytes, longer than LINE_MAX) are kept verbatim and written back unchanged, as are
all lines not touched by set (the original spacing survives).  New keys go after the
last key of their section, new sections to the end.  A UTF-8 BOM is skipped.  Files are
read up to FILE_MAX bytes; after LINES_MAX lines the rest is kept as one verbatim block.
Line ends: LF, CRLF (any number of CRs before the LF are dropped) when reading; written as
CRLF on Windows, LF on Linux.  Key names must not contain '=', CR or LF nor start with
';' '#' '['; section names must not contain ']', CR or LF; neither may start or end with
a blank (set -> false).  CR / LF inside a value are written as blanks.
```

- **`struct Ini`**
  a settings object (one thread at a time)

**`impl Ini`**

- **`fn new() -> Ini`**
- **`fn load(path: &str) -> Ini`**
  the parsed file; empty when it is missing or unreadable  [ini_load]
- **`fn parse(data: &[u8]) -> Ini`**
  parse settings text  [ini_parse]
- **`fn to_bytes(&self) -> Vec<u8>`**
  the text save writes
- **`fn save(&self, path: &str) -> bool`**
  Write through plat::write_file; missing directories are created. [ini_save]
- **`fn section(&self, sec: &str) -> Vec<(&str, &str)>`**
  every key of a section with its value, in file order (entries that are not UTF-8 are
  left out; sec "" = the keys before the first header)
- **`fn get(&self, sec: &str, key: &str) -> Option<&str>`**
  the value, or None when missing or not UTF-8  [ini_get]
- **`fn get_bytes(&self, sec: &str, key: &str) -> Option<&[u8]>`**
- **`fn get_int(&self, sec: &str, key: &str, def: i64) -> i64`**
  [ini_get_int]
- **`fn get_f64(&self, sec: &str, key: &str, def: f64) -> f64`**
  the whole value must be the number  [ini_get_f64]
- **`fn set(&mut self, sec: &str, key: &str, value: &str) -> bool`**
  add or replace (false: a bad name)  [ini_set]
- **`fn set_int(&mut self, sec: &str, key: &str, value: i64) -> bool`**
  [ini_set_int]
- **`fn set_f64(&mut self, sec: &str, key: &str, value: f64) -> bool`**
  the shortest text that reads back to the same double  [ini_set_f64]
- **`fn remove(&mut self, sec: &str, key: &str) -> bool`**
  false: not there  [ini_remove]

### `base/xml.rs`

Source: [`lib/base/xml.rs`](../lib/base/xml.rs)

```text
Bounded, namespace-aware XML subset for interchange. No DTD, entities, I/O or XInclude.
New native functionality; deliberately rejects constructs it cannot represent losslessly.
```

- **`struct Name`**
  Expanded XML name (prefixes are not identities).
  - `ns: String`
  - `local: String`

- **`struct Node`**
  Element-only or text-only node; mixed content is rejected by this subset reader.
  - `name: Name`
  - `attrs: Vec<(Name, String)>`
  - `text: String`
  - `children: Vec<Node>`

**`impl Node`**

- **`fn is(&self, ns: &str, local: &str) -> bool`**
  Test an expanded name.
- **`fn attr(&self, ns: &str, local: &str) -> Option<&str>`**
  Attribute lookup; the default namespace never applies to unprefixed attributes.

- **`enum Error`**
  Syntax error, resource limit, or an intentionally unsupported XML construct.
  - `Syntax`
  - `Limit`
  - `Unsupported`

- **`fn valid_char(c: char) -> bool`**
  XML 1.0 character validity (also used before emitting document text).

- **`fn escape(s: &str) -> Result<String, Error>`**
  Escape text/attribute content, including CR so it survives XML newline normalization.

- **`fn parse(s: &str) -> Result<Node, Error>`**
  Parse a bounded XML subset. DTDs, processing instructions, comments, CDATA and mixed
  content are explicitly rejected. No resources are ever resolved or fetched.

### `base/num.rs`

Source: [`lib/base/num.rs`](../lib/base/num.rs)

```text
num -- numbers <-> text                                                  [src/core/ini.s]

  f64_parse(s) -> (value, used)   leading blanks, sign, digits, '.', exponent, inf /
                                  infinity / nan; used = 0 when there is no number
  f64_format(v) -> String         the shortest text that reads back to the same double,
                                  '.' separator, "1e-07" style below 1e-5 and from 1e17,
                                  "inf" / "-inf" / "nan"
  parse_i64(s) -> Option<i64>     decimal (+/-), 0x hex, true/false/yes/no/on/off (the whole
                                  string; overflow -> None)

The scaling steps run on the x87 unit like in ASMLIB (rt::x87), so both directions are
bit-identical to the assembly -- the text of a saved setting included.
```

- **`fn f64_parse(s: &[u8]) -> (f64, usize)`**

- **`fn f64_format(v: f64) -> String`**
  the shortest decimal text that reads back to v  [f64_format]

- **`fn parse_i64(s: &[u8]) -> Option<i64>`**
  an integer setting: the whole string must match  [parse_i64]

## datetime

*timestamps, civil dates, RFC 3339, IANA time zones with DST, cron schedules* -- crate root [`lib/datetime/lib.rs`](../lib/datetime/lib.rs)

```text
datetime -- calendar time: timestamps, civil dates, RFC 3339, time zones, cron schedules

  Timestamp { secs, nanos }          seconds since 1970-01-01 00:00 UTC
  Civil { year .. second, nanos }    a calendar date and wall time (no zone)
  days_from_civil / civil_from_days / days_in_month / is_leap / weekday (0 = Monday)
  rfc3339(ts) / rfc3339_offset(ts, offset) / parse_rfc3339(s)
  format_date_time(ts, &zone, en) / format_date / format_time / format_duration(secs, en)
  tz::Zone                           IANA zones with their current DST rule (zones.rs from
                                     tools/gen_tz.py), Windows zone keys, POSIX TZ strings;
                                     offset_at / to_local / from_local (Single, Ambiguous,
                                     Gap)
  cron::Cron                         5-field cron expressions: next_after in a zone (a time
                                     in a DST gap runs right after the gap, a repeated time
                                     runs once), next_n, describe (de / en)

No clock here: plat::unix_time_ns gives "now"; datetime only computes.
```

### `datetime` crate root

- **`struct Timestamp`**
  a point in time: seconds since 1970-01-01 00:00:00 UTC and nanoseconds

  - `nanos: u32`

**`impl Timestamp`**

- **`const fn from_secs(secs: i64) -> Timestamp`**

  from nanoseconds since the epoch (plat::unix_time_ns)
- **`const fn from_millis(ms: i64) -> Timestamp`**
- **`const fn as_millis(&self) -> i64`**
- **`const fn add_secs(&self, s: i64) -> Timestamp`**

- **`struct Civil`**
  a calendar date and wall time without a zone
  - `year: i32`
  - `month: u8`
  - `day: u8`
  - `hour: u8`
  - `minute: u8`
  - `second: u8`
  - `nanos: u32`

- **`const fn date(year: i32, month: u8, day: u8) -> Civil`**
- **`fn from_unix(secs: i64) -> Civil`**
  the civil time of seconds since the epoch read as UTC
- **`fn to_unix(&self) -> i64`**
  seconds since the epoch of this wall time read as UTC
- **`fn weekday(&self) -> u8`**
  0 = Monday .. 6 = Sunday
- **`fn is_valid(&self) -> bool`**
- **`fn add_days(&self, days: i64) -> Civil`**

  days since 1970-01-01 of a date (proleptic Gregorian; Howard Hinnant's algorithm)

  (year, month, day) of days since 1970-01-01

- **`fn weekday(y: i32, m: u8, d: u8) -> u8`**

- **`fn rfc3339(ts: Timestamp) -> String`**

  the timestamp as local time with its UTC offset: "2026-10-12T21:58:00+02:00"

- **`fn parse_rfc3339(s: &str) -> Option<Timestamp>`**
  parse "YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS[.fff…](Z|±HH:MM)" (also with a space or lower-case t / z)

- **`fn format_date_time(ts: Timestamp, zone: &Zone, en: bool) -> String`**
  "12.10.2026 19:58" (de) / "2026-10-12 19:58" (en) in a zone

- **`fn format_date(ts: Timestamp, zone: &Zone, en: bool) -> String`**

- **`fn format_time(ts: Timestamp, zone: &Zone) -> String`**
  "19:58"

- **`fn format_duration(secs: i64, en: bool) -> String`**
  a duration for people: "42 s", "3 min 05 s", "1 h 12 min", "2 d 3 h"

- **`fn format_relative(now: i64, then: i64, en: bool) -> String`**
  a relative time for people: "vor 5 min" / "5 min ago", "in 2 h" (now and then in seconds)

### `datetime/cron.rs`

Source: [`lib/datetime/cron.rs`](../lib/datetime/cron.rs)

```text
cron -- 5-field cron schedules evaluated in a time zone

  Cron::parse("30 2 * * 1-5")   minute hour day-of-month month day-of-week; fields: *, n,
                                a-b, a-b/n, */n, lists a,b; month names jan..dec, weekday
                                names sun..sat (0 and 7 = Sunday); @hourly @daily @midnight
                                @weekly @monthly @yearly @annually.  Day of month and day of
                                week restricted both: either matches (Vixie cron).
  cron.next_after(ts, &zone)    the first run after ts: a wall time in a DST gap runs at the
                                jump (right after the gap), a repeated wall time runs once
                                (its first occurrence); None when nothing matches within
                                5 years
  cron.next_n(ts, &zone, n)     the next n runs
  cron.describe(en)             "Täglich um 02:00", "Werktags um 02:00", "Alle 15 Minuten" …
  Schedule helpers: daily(h, m), weekly(mask, h, m), monthly(day, h, m), hourly(m) build
  the expressions of the usual choices (mask bit 0 = Monday)
```

- **`struct Cron`**
  a parsed cron expression

- **`struct CronError`**
  a parse error: the field (0 minute .. 4 day of week, 5 = the whole expression) and why
  - `field: u8`
  - `msg: &'static str`

**`impl Cron`**

- **`fn parse(s: &str) -> Result<Cron, CronError>`**
  parse an expression (see the module comment)
- **`fn as_str(&self) -> &str`**
  the expression as written
- **`fn matches_date(&self, y: i32, m: u8, d: u8) -> bool`**
  whether a date matches the month, day-of-month and day-of-week fields
- **`fn next_after(&self, after: Timestamp, zone: &Zone) -> Option<Timestamp>`**
  the first run strictly after `after` in `zone`
- **`fn next_n(&self, after: Timestamp, zone: &Zone, n: usize) -> Vec<Timestamp>`**
  the next n runs after `after`
- **`fn describe(&self, en: bool) -> String`**
  the schedule in words (German, or English with en)

- **`fn daily(h: u8, m: u8) -> String`**
  "M H \* \* \*"

- **`fn hourly(m: u8) -> String`**
  "M \* \* \* \*"

- **`fn weekly(mask: u8, h: u8, m: u8) -> String`**
  "M H \* \* days" with mask bit 0 = Monday .. bit 6 = Sunday (empty mask: every day)

- **`fn monthly(day: u8, h: u8, m: u8) -> String`**
  "M H D \* \*"

- **`fn classify(c: &Cron) -> (u8, u8, u8, u8, u8)`**
  the (kind, hour, minute, weekday mask, day of month) of an expression built by the helpers
  above: kind 1 hourly, 2 daily, 3 weekly, 4 monthly, 0 anything else (custom)

### `datetime/tz.rs`

Source: [`lib/datetime/tz.rs`](../lib/datetime/tz.rs)

```text
tz -- time zones from POSIX TZ rules ("CET-1CEST,M3.5.0,M10.5.0/3")

  Zone::utc() / Zone::by_name("Europe/Berlin") / Zone::from_windows("W. Europe Standard Time")
  Zone::from_posix(name, rule) / Zone::fixed(name, offset_secs)
  Zone::names() -> every IANA name of the table (sorted)
  zone.offset_at(secs) -> UTC offset (seconds east), zone.abbrev_at(secs), zone.is_dst(secs)
  zone.to_local(ts) -> Civil;  zone.from_local(&civil) -> LocalResult:
       Single(ts) | Ambiguous(first, second) (the repeated hour) | Gap { after } (the skipped
       hour: `after` is the instant the clocks jumped, the first valid time after the gap)

A rule describes the zone from its last change on (the TZif footer of tools/gen_tz.py), so
times before that change use today's rule.
```

- **`struct Zone`**
  a time zone: its name, standard offset and an optional daylight saving rule

- **`enum LocalResult`**
  the instants a local wall time stands for
  - `Single(Timestamp)`
  - `Ambiguous(Timestamp, Timestamp)` -- the wall time occurs twice (the clocks went back): earlier and later instant
  - `Gap { after: Timestamp }` -- the wall time does not exist (the clocks jumped forward): the jump's instant

**`impl LocalResult`**

- **`fn earliest(&self) -> Timestamp`**
  the earliest instant that stands for the wall time (a gap: the jump)

**`impl Zone`**

- **`fn utc() -> Zone`**
  UTC
- **`fn fixed(name: &str, offset_secs: i32) -> Zone`**
  a fixed offset (seconds east of UTC)
- **`fn from_posix(name: &str, rule: &str) -> Option<Zone>`**
  a zone from a POSIX TZ rule
- **`fn by_name(name: &str) -> Option<Zone>`**
  an IANA zone of the table ("Europe/Berlin"; "UTC", "Etc/UTC", "Z" also work)
- **`fn from_windows(key: &str) -> Option<Zone>`**
  the IANA zone of a Windows time zone key ("W. Europe Standard Time")
- **`fn windows_to_iana(key: &str) -> Option<&'static str>`**
  the IANA name of a Windows time zone key
- **`fn names() -> impl Iterator<Item = &'static str>`**
  every IANA zone name of the table, sorted
- **`fn from_tzif(name: &str, data: &[u8]) -> Option<Zone>`**
  a zone from a TZif file (its footer rule; /etc/localtime), named `name`
- **`fn name(&self) -> &str`**
- **`fn std_offset(&self) -> i32`**
  the standard offset (seconds east of UTC)
- **`fn is_dst(&self, secs: i64) -> bool`**
  whether daylight saving time is in effect at an instant
- **`fn offset_at(&self, secs: i64) -> i32`**
  the UTC offset (seconds east) in effect at an instant
- **`fn abbrev_at(&self, secs: i64) -> &str`**
  the abbreviation in effect at an instant ("CET" / "CEST")
- **`fn to_local(&self, ts: Timestamp) -> Civil`**
  the wall time of an instant
- **`fn from_local(&self, c: &Civil) -> LocalResult`**
  the instants of a wall time (seconds; nanos kept)

## crypto

*hashes, MACs, KDFs, Argon2id, AEAD, X25519, Ed25519, sealed boxes (no randomness inside)* -- crate root [`lib/crypto/lib.rs`](../lib/crypto/lib.rs)

```text
crypto -- the primitives BackupCraft (and anything else) builds on, in plain Rust

  sha2      Sha256 / Sha384 / Sha512, sha256 / sha384 / sha512(data)         (FIPS 180-4)
  hmac      Hmac<H>, hmac_sha256 / hmac_sha512, hkdf_sha256 / hkdf_sha512,
            pbkdf2_sha256 (import of foreign key files only)                (RFC 2104/5869/8018)
  blake2b   Blake2b (keyed, 1..64 byte digests)                             (RFC 7693)
  blake3    Blake3 (hash, keyed_hash, derive_key, any output length)
  argon2    argon2id(password, salt, params, out), PHC strings hash / verify (RFC 9106)
  chacha    ChaCha20 (IETF), HChaCha20, XChaCha20                           (RFC 8439)
  poly1305  Poly1305 one-time authenticator                                 (RFC 8439)
  aead      ChaCha20-Poly1305 and XChaCha20-Poly1305 seal / open            (RFC 8439)
  x25519    Diffie-Hellman over Curve25519                                  (RFC 7748)
  ed25519   signatures, strict verification                                 (RFC 8032)
  sealbox   anonymous public-key encryption: X25519 + HKDF-SHA256 + XChaCha20-Poly1305
  aes, gcm  AES-128/256 (bitsliced, constant time), AES-GCM seal / open         (FIPS 197,
            SP 800-38D)
  bn        big integers modulo odd numbers (public values only: variable time)
  rsa       RSA PKCS #1 v1.5 and PSS verification                            (RFC 8017)
  ecdsa     ECDSA verification on P-256 / P-384                              (FIPS 186-4)
  p256      Diffie-Hellman on P-256 in constant time (complete formulas)      (SEC 1)
  b64       Base64 (standard and URL alphabet, with or without padding)
  ct_eq / zeroize / hex

No randomness here: callers pass random bytes in (plat::fill_random), so everything is
deterministic and testable.  Secret-dependent work avoids branches and table lookups
(field arithmetic with 51-bit limbs, masked conditional swaps and selects).
```

### `crypto` crate root

- **`trait Hash: Clone`**
  a hash function for Hmac / HKDF / PBKDF2
  - `const BLOCK: usize`
    block size in bytes (at most 128)
  - `const OUT: usize`
    digest size in bytes (at most 64)
  - `fn new() -> Self`
  - `fn update(&mut self, data: &[u8])`
  - `fn finish_into(self, out: &mut [u8])`
    the digest into out[..OUT]

- **`enum HashAlg`**
  the hash functions of signatures
  - `Sha256`
  - `Sha384`
  - `Sha512`

**`impl HashAlg`**

- **`fn len(self) -> usize`**
- **`fn digest(self, data: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8>`**

- **`fn ct_eq(a: &[u8], b: &[u8]) -> bool`**
  equality of two byte strings in time that depends only on their lengths

- **`fn zeroize(buf: &mut [u8])`**
  overwrite a secret (the write is kept even though nothing reads the buffer afterwards)

- **`fn hex(data: &[u8]) -> String`**
  lower-case hex

- **`fn unhex(s: &str) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`**
  the bytes of a hex string (upper or lower case; None on odd length or other characters)

### `crypto/aead.rs`

Source: [`lib/crypto/aead.rs`](../lib/crypto/aead.rs)

```text
aead -- ChaCha20-Poly1305 (RFC 8439) and XChaCha20-Poly1305 (192-bit nonces: random nonces
are safe)

  seal_in_place(key, nonce, aad, buf) -> tag          open_in_place(.., buf, &tag) -> ok
  seal(key, nonce, aad, plain) -> ciphertext ‖ tag    open(.., sealed) -> Some(plain)
  (x_* : the same with a 24-byte nonce)
```

```rust
KEY_LEN: usize = 32
NONCE_LEN: usize = 12
XNONCE_LEN: usize = 24
TAG_LEN: usize = 16
```

- **`fn seal_in_place(key: &[u8; 32], nonce: &[u8; 12], aad: &[u8], buf: &mut [u8]) -> [u8; 16]`**
  encrypt buf in place -> the tag

- **`fn open_in_place(key: &[u8; 32], nonce: &[u8; 12], aad: &[u8], buf: &mut [u8], t: &[u8; 16]) -> bool`**
  check the tag and decrypt buf in place -> false (buf unchanged) on a wrong tag

- **`fn seal(key: &[u8; 32], nonce: &[u8; 12], aad: &[u8], plain: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8>`**
  ciphertext ‖ tag

- **`fn open(key: &[u8; 32], nonce: &[u8; 12], aad: &[u8], sealed: &[u8]) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`**
  the plaintext of ciphertext ‖ tag (None: too short or forged)

- **`fn x_seal_in_place(key: &[u8; 32], nonce: &[u8; 24], aad: &[u8], buf: &mut [u8]) -> [u8; 16]`**
  XChaCha20-Poly1305: encrypt buf in place -> the tag

- **`fn x_open_in_place(key: &[u8; 32], nonce: &[u8; 24], aad: &[u8], buf: &mut [u8], t: &[u8; 16]) -> bool`**
  XChaCha20-Poly1305: check and decrypt in place

- **`fn x_seal(key: &[u8; 32], nonce: &[u8; 24], aad: &[u8], plain: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8>`**
  XChaCha20-Poly1305: ciphertext ‖ tag

- **`fn x_open(key: &[u8; 32], nonce: &[u8; 24], aad: &[u8], sealed: &[u8]) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`**
  XChaCha20-Poly1305: the plaintext of ciphertext ‖ tag

### `crypto/aes.rs`

Source: [`lib/crypto/aes.rs`](../lib/crypto/aes.rs)

```text
aes -- AES-128 / AES-256 encryption (FIPS 197) in constant time

  Aes::new(key) (16 or 32 bytes)    aes.encrypt4(&mut [[u8; 16]; 4])    aes.encrypt(&mut block)

Bitsliced: four blocks at a time in eight 64-bit words, word i holding bit i of all 64 bytes
(bit position = row * 16 + column * 4 + block).  SubBytes inverts in GF(2^8) with
multiplications on those bit planes (x^254 by four multiplications and seven squarings),
then applies the affine map; ShiftRows rotates inside the 16-bit row fields, MixColumns
rotates whole rows.  No table lookups, no branches on data or key: what GCM needs for
TLS.  Decryption is not needed there and not provided.
```

- **`struct Aes`**

**`impl Aes`**

- **`fn new(key: &[u8]) -> Option<Aes>`**
  a 16-byte (AES-128) or 32-byte (AES-256) key
- **`fn encrypt4(&self, blocks: &mut [[u8; 16]; 4])`**
  encrypt four blocks in place
- **`fn encrypt(&self, block: &mut [u8; 16])`**
  encrypt one block in place

### `crypto/argon2.rs`

Source: [`lib/crypto/argon2.rs`](../lib/crypto/argon2.rs)

```text
argon2 -- Argon2id (RFC 9106, version 0x13) and PHC strings

  argon2id(password, salt, &Params, out)          raw tag (secret / associated data empty)
  argon2id_ex(password, salt, secret, ad, &Params, out)
  hash(password, salt, &Params) -> "$argon2id$v=19$m=..,t=..,p=..$salt$hash"
  verify(phc, password) -> bool                   (constant-time comparison)

Lanes are filled one after the other in each slice (the result is the same as with
threads); memory is m KiB, allocated per call.
```

- **`struct Params`**
  cost parameters
  - `m_kib: u32` -- memory in KiB (at least 8 \* lanes)
  - `t: u32` -- passes over the memory
  - `p: u32` -- lanes (parallelism)
  - `out_len: u32` -- tag length in bytes

**`impl Params`**

- **`const INTERACTIVE: Params = Params { m_kib: 19 * 1024, t: 2, p: 1, out_len: 32 }`**
  the OWASP recommendation for interactive logins: 19 MiB, 2 passes, 1 lane
- **`const KEY: Params = Params { m_kib: 64 * 1024, t: 3, p: 1, out_len: 32 }`**
  for keys that protect data at rest (passphrases): 64 MiB, 3 passes, 1 lane

- **`fn argon2id(password: &[u8], salt: &[u8], params: &Params, out: &mut [u8]) -> bool`**
  Argon2id with an empty secret and no associated data

- **`fn argon2id_ex(password: &[u8], salt: &[u8], secret: &[u8], ad: &[u8], params: &Params, out: &mut [u8]) -> bool`**
  Argon2id: false for invalid parameters (out.len() must equal params.out_len)

- **`fn hash(password: &[u8], salt: &[u8], params: &Params) -> Option<String>`**
  a PHC string of the password with a caller-chosen random salt (>= 16 bytes recommended)

- **`fn parse(phc: &str) -> Option<(Params, Vec<u8>, Vec<u8>)>`**
  the parameters, salt and tag of a PHC string

- **`fn verify(phc: &str, password: &[u8]) -> bool`**
  whether the password matches a PHC string

```text
blake2b -- BLAKE2b (RFC 7693): 1..64 byte digests, optional key of up to 64 bytes;
Argon2 builds on it (with its long-output variant `long`)
```

- **`struct Blake2b`**
  BLAKE2b

**`impl Blake2b`**

- **`fn new(out_len: usize, key: &[u8]) -> Blake2b`**
  a hasher for `out_len` (1..=64) byte digests, keyed when `key` is not empty (&lt;= 64 bytes)
- **`fn update(&mut self, mut data: &[u8])`**
- **`fn finalize_into(mut self, out: &mut [u8])`**
  the digest into out[..out_len]
- **`fn finalize(self) -> Vec<u8>`**

- **`fn blake2b(out: &mut [u8], key: &[u8], parts: &[&[u8]])`**
  BLAKE2b of the concatenated parts, `out.len()` (1..=64) bytes

### `crypto/blake3.rs`

Source: [`lib/crypto/blake3.rs`](../lib/crypto/blake3.rs)

```text
blake3 -- BLAKE3: hash, keyed hash, key derivation, extendable output

  Blake3::new() / new_keyed(&key) / new_derive_key(context)
  update(data); finalize() -> [u8; 32]; finalize_xof(&mut out) (any length)
  hash(data) / keyed_hash(&key, data) / derive_key(context, material) -> [u8; 32]

The tree mode of the reference implementation: 1 KiB chunks, a stack of chaining values
merged by the number of finished chunks.  Portable code (no SIMD).
```

- **`struct Blake3`**
  an incremental BLAKE3 hasher

**`impl Blake3`**

- **`fn new() -> Blake3`**
  the plain hash function
- **`fn new_keyed(key: &[u8; 32]) -> Blake3`**
  a keyed hash (MAC / PRF) with a 32-byte key
- **`fn new_derive_key(context: &str) -> Blake3`**
  key derivation: `context` is a hard-coded, unique, application-specific string
- **`fn update(&mut self, mut input: &[u8]) -> &mut Blake3`**
- **`fn finalize(&self) -> [u8; OUT_LEN]`**
  the 32-byte digest (the hasher stays usable)
- **`fn finalize_xof(&self, out: &mut [u8])`**
  any number of output bytes (the first 32 equal finalize())
- **`fn finalize_xof_at(&self, offset: u64, out: &mut [u8])`**
  output bytes starting at byte `offset` of the output stream

- **`fn hash(data: &[u8]) -> [u8; 32]`**
  BLAKE3 of a byte string

- **`fn keyed_hash(key: &[u8; 32], data: &[u8]) -> [u8; 32]`**
  keyed BLAKE3 (a MAC / PRF)

- **`fn derive_key(context: &str, material: &[u8]) -> [u8; 32]`**
  a 32-byte key derived from key material for a context string

### `crypto/bn.rs`

Source: [`lib/crypto/bn.rs`](../lib/crypto/bn.rs)

```text
bn -- unsigned big integers for checking signatures (RSA, ECDSA)

  from_be / to_be                     big-endian bytes <-> little-endian 64-bit limbs
  Mont::new(n)                        arithmetic modulo an odd n: mul (Montgomery), add, sub,
                                      to_mont / from_mont, pow, inv (n prime)

Variable time on purpose: everything here is public (keys, signatures, digests).  Never use
it for secrets.
```

- **`type Limbs = Vec<u64>`**

- **`fn from_be(b: &[u8], k: usize) -> Limbs`**
  big-endian bytes -> limbs (at least `k` of them)

- **`fn to_be(a: &[u64], len: usize) -> Vec<u8>`**
  limbs -> exactly `len` big-endian bytes (higher bytes dropped)

- **`fn cmp(a: &[u64], b: &[u64]) -> Ordering`**

- **`fn is_zero(a: &[u64]) -> bool`**

- **`fn bits(a: &[u64]) -> usize`**
  the number of significant bits

- **`struct Mont`**
  arithmetic modulo an odd number
  - `n: Limbs`

**`impl Mont`**

- **`fn new(n: &[u64]) -> Option<Mont>`**
- **`fn limbs(&self) -> usize`**
- **`fn mul(&self, a: &[u64], b: &[u64]) -> Limbs`**
  a b R^-1 mod n (a, b &lt; n)
- **`fn to_mont(&self, a: &[u64]) -> Limbs`**
- **`fn from_mont(&self, a: &[u64]) -> Limbs`**
- **`fn add(&self, a: &[u64], b: &[u64]) -> Limbs`**
  (a + b) mod n (a, b &lt; n)
- **`fn sub(&self, a: &[u64], b: &[u64]) -> Limbs`**
  (a - b) mod n (a, b &lt; n)
- **`fn reduce_once(&self, a: &[u64]) -> Limbs`**
  a mod n for a &lt; 2 n
- **`fn pow(&self, base: &[u64], exp: &[u8]) -> Limbs`**
  base^exp mod n (normal form in and out; exp big-endian)
- **`fn inv_mont(&self, a: &[u64]) -> Limbs`**
  a^-1 mod n for a prime n (a != 0; Montgomery form in and out)

Source: [`lib/crypto/ecdsa.rs`](../lib/crypto/ecdsa.rs)

```text
ecdsa -- ECDSA verification on NIST P-256 and P-384 (FIPS 186-4, SEC 1)

  verify(curve, public_key, digest, r, s)   public key uncompressed (04 || x || y), checked
                                            to lie on the curve; r, s big-endian integers

Jacobian coordinates in the Montgomery domain (a = -3 formulas), u1 G + u2 Q by Shamir's
trick.  Public data only: variable time.
```

- **`enum Curve`**
  - `P256`
  - `P384`

**`impl Curve`**

- **`fn bytes(self) -> usize`**
  the size of a coordinate / scalar in bytes

- **`fn verify(curve: Curve, public_key: &[u8], digest: &[u8], r: &[u8], s: &[u8]) -> bool`**
  verify an ECDSA signature over `digest` (the hash of the message, any length)

### `crypto/ed25519.rs`

Source: [`lib/crypto/ed25519.rs`](../lib/crypto/ed25519.rs)

```text
ed25519 -- Ed25519 signatures (RFC 8032), strict verification

  Keypair::from_seed(&seed) -> Keypair { public }     sign(&msg) -> [u8; 64]
  verify(&public, &msg, &sig) -> bool

Verification is strict: the public key and R must be canonical points, S must be below the
group order, and [S]B == R + [k]A must hold exactly (no cofactor), so a signature is valid
or not the same way everywhere.  The curve constants (d, sqrt(-1), the base point) are
computed from their definitions instead of copied as long numbers.
Scalar multiplication: double and add-always with masked selects (no branches on bits).
```

- **`struct Keypair`**
  an Ed25519 key pair from its 32-byte seed (the private key of RFC 8032)
  - `public: [u8; 32]`

**`impl Keypair`**

- **`fn from_seed(seed: &[u8; 32]) -> Keypair`**
- **`fn seed(&self) -> &[u8; 32]`**
  the seed (to store the key)
- **`fn sign(&self, msg: &[u8]) -> [u8; 64]`**

- **`fn verify(public: &[u8; 32], msg: &[u8], sig: &[u8; 64]) -> bool`**
  strict verification of a signature

- **`fn valid_public_key(public: &[u8; 32]) -> bool`**
  whether 32 bytes are a valid (canonical, on-curve) public key

### `crypto/gcm.rs`

Source: [`lib/crypto/gcm.rs`](../lib/crypto/gcm.rs)

```text
gcm -- AES-GCM (NIST SP 800-38D) with 12-byte nonces and 16-byte tags, in constant time

  AesGcm::new(key)                          16 (AES-128-GCM) or 32 bytes (AES-256-GCM)
  seal_in_place(nonce, aad, data) -> tag    open_in_place(nonce, aad, data, tag) -> bool

GHASH runs on the bit-reversed form of the blocks, where multiplication in GF(2^128) is a
plain carry-less product modulo x^128 + x^7 + x^2 + x + 1.  The carry-less products come from
integer multiplications of operands with three-bit holes (32-bit halves: at most eight
partial products meet in one position, so no carry reaches the next used bit).
```

- **`struct AesGcm`**

**`impl AesGcm`**

- **`fn new(key: &[u8]) -> Option<AesGcm>`**
- **`fn seal_in_place(&self, nonce: &[u8; 12], aad: &[u8], data: &mut [u8]) -> [u8; 16]`**
  encrypt data in place -> the tag
- **`fn open_in_place(&self, nonce: &[u8; 12], aad: &[u8], data: &mut [u8], tag: &[u8; 16]) -> bool`**
  check the tag, then decrypt in place (false: data untouched)

### `crypto/hmac.rs`

Source: [`lib/crypto/hmac.rs`](../lib/crypto/hmac.rs)

```text
hmac -- HMAC (RFC 2104) over any Hash, HKDF (RFC 5869), PBKDF2 (RFC 8018)
```

- **`struct Hmac<H: Hash>`**
  HMAC with a hash function H

**`impl<H: Hash> Hmac<H>`**

- **`fn new(key: &[u8]) -> Hmac<H>`**
- **`fn update(&mut self, data: &[u8])`**

  the tag into out[..H::OUT]

  HMAC-SHA256 of a message

- **`fn hmac_sha512(key: &[u8], data: &[u8]) -> [u8; 64]`**
  HMAC-SHA512 of a message

- **`fn hkdf_extract<H: Hash>(salt: &[u8], ikm: &[u8], prk: &mut [u8])`**
  HKDF-Extract: the pseudo-random key (into prk[..H::OUT])

- **`fn hkdf_expand<H: Hash>(prk: &[u8], info: &[u8], out: &mut [u8])`**
  HKDF-Expand: fill `out` (at most 255 \* H::OUT bytes; panics beyond)

- **`fn hkdf_sha256(salt: &[u8], ikm: &[u8], info: &[u8], out: &mut [u8])`**
  HKDF-SHA256 (extract and expand)

- **`fn hkdf_sha512(salt: &[u8], ikm: &[u8], info: &[u8], out: &mut [u8])`**
  HKDF-SHA512 (extract and expand)

- **`fn pbkdf2_sha256(password: &[u8], salt: &[u8], iterations: u32, out: &mut [u8])`**

### `crypto/p256.rs`

Source: [`lib/crypto/p256.rs`](../lib/crypto/p256.rs)

```text
p256 -- Diffie-Hellman on NIST P-256 (SEC 1, RFC 8446 4.2.8.2) in constant time

  public_key(&secret) -> [u8; 65]          04 || x || y of secret * G
  shared(&secret, &peer_point) -> Option<[u8; 32]>   the x coordinate of secret * peer;
                                           None for a point that is not on the curve
  secret_from(random) -> Option<[u8; 32]>  a scalar in [1, n - 1] from 32 random bytes

Field elements in Montgomery form with four 64-bit limbs; multiplication, addition and
subtraction without branches on the values (masked final corrections).  Points in projective
coordinates with the complete addition formulas of Renes, Costello and Batina (2016,
algorithm 4, a = -3): no exceptional cases, so doubling uses the same formula.  Scalar
multiplication by four-bit windows with a table read in full for every window.
```

- **`fn secret_from(random: &[u8; 32]) -> Option<[u8; 32]>`**
  a scalar in [1, n - 1] from random bytes (None: draw again)

- **`fn public_key(secret: &[u8; 32]) -> [u8; 65]`**
  the public key of a secret scalar: 04 || x || y

- **`fn shared(secret: &[u8; 32], peer: &[u8]) -> Option<[u8; 32]>`**
  the shared secret (x coordinate) with a peer's public point


```text
rsa -- RSA signature verification (RFC 8017): PKCS #1 v1.5 and PSS with MGF1

  PublicKey::new(n, e)                      big-endian modulus (1024..8192 bits) and exponent
  key.verify_pkcs1(hash, digest, sig)       the encoding is rebuilt and compared whole
  key.verify_pss(hash, digest, sig, salt)   salt length given, or None: any

Public operations only (variable time is fine).
```

### `crypto/sealbox.rs`

Source: [`lib/crypto/sealbox.rs`](../lib/crypto/sealbox.rs)

```text
sealbox -- anonymous public-key encryption: whoever holds the recipient's X25519 public key
can seal, only the secret key opens.  (Recovery kits: the repository key sealed to an
offline key pair.)

  sealed = ephemeral public (32) ‖ nonce (24) ‖ XChaCha20-Poly1305(key, nonce, aad, plain)
  key    = HKDF-SHA256(salt = ephemeral public ‖ recipient public,
                       ikm = X25519(ephemeral secret, recipient public),
                       info = "JFLib sealbox v1")
```

```rust
OVERHEAD: usize = 32 + 24 + 16
```

- **`fn seal(recipient: &[u8; 32], random: &[u8; 56], aad: &[u8], plain: &[u8]) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`**
  seal `plain` for `recipient`; `random` = 32 bytes ephemeral secret ‖ 24 bytes nonce
  (None: a degenerate recipient key)

- **`fn open(secret: &[u8; 32], aad: &[u8], sealed: &[u8]) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`**
  open a sealed box with the recipient's secret key (None: not for this key or altered)

## compress

*LZ4 block and frame, xxHash32, codec ids* -- crate root [`lib/compress/lib.rs`](../lib/compress/lib.rs)

```text
compress -- lossless compression for backup data

  lz4::compress(data) -> Vec<u8>                  LZ4 block (greedy, 64 KiB window)
  lz4::decompress(block, max_out) -> Option<Vec>  bounds-checked; None on any corruption
  lz4::frame_compress / frame_decompress          the LZ4 frame format (lz4 / zstd --format=lz4
                                                  interoperable), xxHash32 checksums
  zstd::compress(data, level) / decompress        Zstandard frames (RFC 8878; the zstd tool
                                                  reads ours and we read its)
  xxh32(data, seed), xxh64(data, seed)
  Codec { None, Lz4, Zstd } with id(): the codec byte that formats on disk store per blob;
  Codec::pack(data, level) / unpack(data, max_out)
```

### `compress` crate root

- **`enum Codec`**
  the compression of a stored blob (the byte on disk)
  - `None = 0`
  - `Lz4 = 1`
  - `Zstd = 2`

**`impl Codec`**

- **`fn id(self) -> u8`**
- **`fn from_id(b: u8) -> Option<Codec>`**
- **`fn name(self) -> &'static str`**
- **`fn from_name(s: &str) -> Option<Codec>`**
- **`fn pack(self, data: &[u8], level: i32) -> Vec<u8>`**
  compress with this codec (`level`: zstd only); None stores the data as it is
- **`fn unpack(self, data: &[u8], max_out: usize) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`**
  the data of pack(); None when it is damaged or longer than max_out

### `compress/lz4.rs`

Source: [`lib/compress/lz4.rs`](../lib/compress/lz4.rs)

```text
lz4 -- the LZ4 block and frame formats

  compress(data) -> block                greedy matcher, 16K-entry hash table, skip
                                         acceleration on incompressible data
  decompress(block, max_out)             None on truncation, a bad offset, or more than
                                         max_out bytes; never reads or writes out of bounds
  decompress_into(block, &mut out, limit) appends (back references may reach into the
                                         earlier contents of out: linked frame blocks)
  frame_compress(data) / frame_decompress(frame, max_out)

Block rules kept by the compressor: the last 5 bytes are literals, no match starts in the
last 12 bytes, offsets 1..=65535.
```

- **`const fn bound(n: usize) -> usize`**
  the largest block compress() can produce for n input bytes

- **`fn compress(src: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8>`**
  compress into one LZ4 block

- **`fn decompress_into(src: &[u8], out: &mut Vec<u8>, limit: usize) -> bool`**
  decode one block, appending to out (out.len() stays &lt;= limit) -> false on corrupt input

- **`fn decompress(src: &[u8], max_out: usize) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`**
  decode one block of at most max_out bytes

- **`fn frame_compress(src: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8>`**
  an LZ4 frame: independent 4 MiB blocks, content size and content checksum

- **`fn frame_decompress(src: &[u8], max_out: usize) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`**
  decode one or more concatenated LZ4 frames (skippable frames are skipped) of at most
  max_out bytes in total; None on corruption, an unsupported feature (dictionary ids), a
  checksum mismatch, or too much output

### `compress/xxh.rs`

Source: [`lib/compress/xxh.rs`](../lib/compress/xxh.rs)

```text
xxh -- xxHash32 (the checksum of LZ4 frames) and xxHash64 (zstd frames); not cryptographic
```

- **`fn xxh32(data: &[u8], seed: u32) -> u32`**
  xxHash32 of a byte string

- **`fn xxh64(data: &[u8], seed: u64) -> u64`**
  xxHash64 of a byte string (zstd's content checksum is its low 32 bits)

### `compress/zstd/mod.rs`

Source: [`lib/compress/zstd/mod.rs`](../lib/compress/zstd/mod.rs)

```text
zstd -- the Zstandard format (RFC 8878), interoperable with the zstd tool both ways

  compress(data, level) -> frame        levels 1..=19 (LEVEL_DEFAULT 3); content size and
                                        checksum in the frame; window up to 8 MiB
  decompress(frames, max_out)           any conforming frames without a dictionary,
                                        concatenated and skippable ones too; None on
                                        corruption, a checksum mismatch or > max_out bytes
  content_size(frame)                   the size a frame header declares
  bound(n)                              the largest frame compress() makes from n bytes
```

```rust
LEVEL_DEFAULT: i32 = 3
LEVEL_MAX: i32 = 19
```

### `compress/zstd/dec.rs`

Source: [`lib/compress/zstd/dec.rs`](../lib/compress/zstd/dec.rs)

```text
dec -- the zstd frame decoder (RFC 8878): frames and skippable frames, raw / RLE /
compressed blocks, raw / RLE / Huffman / treeless literals with 1 or 4 streams, sequences
with predefined / RLE / FSE / repeat tables, repeat offsets, content size and checksum.
Dictionaries are not supported (a frame that names one is refused).  Every length, offset
and table is checked: corrupt input gives None, never a panic or more than max_out bytes.
```

- **`fn decompress(src: &[u8], max_out: usize) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`**
  decode all frames of src -> None on corruption, unsupported features or > max_out bytes

- **`fn content_size(src: &[u8]) -> Option<u64>`**
  the content size a frame header declares (None: not stated or not a zstd frame)

### `compress/zstd/enc.rs`

Source: [`lib/compress/zstd/enc.rs`](../lib/compress/zstd/enc.rs)

```text
enc -- the zstd encoder: one frame with content size and checksum, blocks of 128 KiB.

Matches come from a hash-chain finder over a window of up to 8 MiB (levels 1..=19 set the
search depth and how many positions ahead a lazy match may be preferred); the three repeat
offsets are tried first.  Literals are Huffman coded when that is smaller; each of the three
sequence codes takes the cheapest of RLE, the predefined distribution and its own table.
A block that does not get smaller is stored raw; a run of one byte value becomes an RLE
block.  The decoder's repeat offsets do not move on raw blocks, so the encoder rolls them back.
```

- **`const fn bound(n: usize) -> usize`**
  the largest frame compress() makes from n bytes

- **`fn compress(src: &[u8], level: i32) -> Vec<u8>`**
  one zstd frame

## datastore

*an embedded journal database: JSON collections, transactions, snapshots, crash recovery* -- crate root [`lib/datastore/lib.rs`](../lib/datastore/lib.rs)

```text
datastore -- an embedded, crash-safe store for one server process: collections of JSON
records (id -> value) kept in memory, made durable by a write-ahead log and snapshots, plus
append-only journals for logs that grow without bound (audit, session logs)

  Store::open(dir) / open_with(dir, Options) -> Result<Store, StoreError>
  get(coll, id) / list(coll) / count(coll) / ids(coll)          (clones of the values)
  write(|tx| { tx.put(coll, id, v); tx.delete(coll, id); }) -> Result<seq>
  update(coll, id, |v| ...)                                     read-modify-write in one txn
  compact()                                                     snapshot now
  Journal::open(dir) / append(name, &value) / tail(name, n) / read_month(name, yyyymm)

Files in `dir`: LOCK, snap-<seq>.bcs, wal-<first seq>.log; journal/<name>/<yyyymm>.jl.
  snapshot: "BCS1" seq:u64 | colls:u32 | per collection name, records:u32 | per record id,
            JSON | BLAKE3 of everything before (32 bytes)
  WAL frame: "BCW1" len:u32 seq:u64 payload BLAKE3(len ‖ seq ‖ payload)[..16]; the payload is
            a JSON array of ["put", coll, id, value] / ["del", coll, id]
A transaction is one frame, written and fsynced before it becomes visible.  Opening loads the
newest valid snapshot and replays the frames after it.  A torn frame at the very end (a crash
during a write) is cut off; a damaged frame with intact data behind it stops the open with
StoreError::Corrupt unless Options::repair is set (then the log is cut there).
Values are plain JSON: secrets are sealed by the caller (crypto::aead) before they get here.
```

### `datastore` crate root

- **`fn record_limits() -> Limits`**
  the limits of one stored record

- **`enum StoreError`**
  - `Locked` -- another process holds the store
  - `Io(String)` -- reading or writing a file failed
  - `Corrupt(String)` -- a damaged log frame with intact data behind it (open with repair to cut the log there)
  - `TooLarge` -- a record or transaction over the size limits

- **`type StoreResult<T> = Result<T, StoreError>`**

- **`struct Options`**
  - `snapshot_bytes: u64` -- write a snapshot (and start a new log) when the log grows beyond this
  - `repair: bool` -- cut a damaged log at the damage instead of refusing to open
  - `durable: bool` -- fsync every transaction (off only for tests and bulk imports)

- **`struct Recovery`**
  what open() found
  - `snapshot_seq: u64`
  - `frames_replayed: u64`
  - `truncated_bytes: u64` -- bytes cut from the end of the log (a torn last write)
  - `repaired: Option<String>` -- the damage that was cut away with Options::repair

- **`struct Tx`**
  the changes of one transaction

**`impl Tx`**

- **`fn put(&mut self, coll: &str, id: &str, v: Value)`**
- **`fn delete(&mut self, coll: &str, id: &str)`**
- **`fn is_empty(&self) -> bool`**

- **`type Data = BTreeMap<String, BTreeMap<String, Value>>`**
  the records of every collection: collection -> id -> value

- **`struct Store`**
  the store (share it between threads by reference or Arc)

**`impl Store`**

- **`fn open(dir: &str) -> StoreResult<Store>`**
- **`fn open_with(dir: &str, opts: Options) -> StoreResult<Store>`**
- **`fn recovery(&self) -> &Recovery`**
  what open() found and repaired
- **`fn seq(&self) -> u64`**
  the sequence number of the last transaction
- **`fn get(&self, coll: &str, id: &str) -> Option<Value>`**
- **`fn contains(&self, coll: &str, id: &str) -> bool`**
- **`fn list(&self, coll: &str) -> Vec<(String, Value)>`**
  every record of a collection, ordered by id
- **`fn filter(&self, coll: &str, mut f: impl FnMut(&str, &Value) -> bool) -> Vec<(String, Value)>`**
  the records for which `f` holds
- **`fn ids(&self, coll: &str) -> Vec<String>`**
- **`fn count(&self, coll: &str) -> usize`**
- **`fn write<E: From<StoreError>>(&self, f: impl FnOnce(&Data, &mut Tx) -> Result<(), E>) -> Result<u64, E>`**
  run a transaction: the changes `f` records become durable and visible together
  (f sees the current data; nothing is written when it returns Err or records nothing)
- **`fn put(&self, coll: &str, id: &str, v: Value) -> StoreResult<u64>`**
  put one record
- **`fn delete(&self, coll: &str, id: &str) -> StoreResult<u64>`**
  delete one record
- **`fn update(&self, coll: &str, id: &str, f: impl FnOnce(&mut Value)) -> StoreResult<Option<Value>>`**
  change one record in place (None: it does not exist)
- **`fn compact(&self) -> StoreResult<()>`**
  write a snapshot now and start a new log

### `datastore/journal.rs`

Source: [`lib/datastore/journal.rs`](../lib/datastore/journal.rs)

```text
journal -- append-only logs of JSON lines, one file per name and month
(<dir>/<name>/<yyyymm>.jl): audit entries, session logs.  A line is complete or ignored (a
crash during an append leaves at most one partial last line, which readers skip).
```

- **`struct Journal`**

**`impl Journal`**

- **`fn open(dir: &str, durable: bool) -> StoreResult<Journal>`**
  journals below `dir`; `durable`: fsync each append
- **`fn append(&self, name: &str, month: u32, v: &Value) -> StoreResult<()>`**
  append one entry to journal `name` in month `yyyymm` (e.g. 202610)
- **`fn months(&self, name: &str) -> Vec<u32>`**
  the months that have entries, oldest first
- **`fn read_month(&self, name: &str, month: u32) -> Vec<Value>`**
  the entries of one month, oldest first
- **`fn tail(&self, name: &str, n: usize) -> Vec<Value>`**
  the newest `n` entries (oldest first), across months
- **`fn prune_before(&self, name: &str, month: u32) -> usize`**
  remove the months before `month` (retention)
- **`fn remove(&self, name: &str)`**
  remove a whole journal (e.g. the log of a deleted session)

## netio

*HTTP/1.1 server and client, TLS 1.3 subset, X.509 of a private CA, SSH-2, DNS* -- crate root [`lib/netio/lib.rs`](../lib/netio/lib.rs)

```text
netio -- network protocols on top of plat::net

  dns        host names to addresses (Linux: /etc/hosts, resolv.conf, UDP / TCP queries;
             Windows: the system resolver)
  url        Url::parse("https://host:9443/api?x=1"), percent_encode / percent_decode,
             query_pairs
  http       Request, Response, Body (bytes or a stream sent chunked), Headers, status texts;
             the HTTP/1.1 wire format (bounded parsing, chunked transfer, keep-alive)
  server     Server::start(addr, ServerConfig, handler): accept thread + worker pool
  client     Client::request(...): blocking, timeouts, size limit
  tls        TLS 1.3 for own deployments (ChaCha20-Poly1305, X25519, Ed25519, mutual auth)
             and a client for public servers (AES-GCM, RSA / ECDSA, TLS 1.2 fallback):
             connect / accept -> TlsStream (a Stream)
  x509 / der / pem   certificates: Ed25519 of a private CA (parse, build, verify chains) and
             the RSA / ECDSA certificates of the web PKI
  roots      the system's certificate authorities (Windows: CryptoAPI; Linux: the bundle)
  ssh        SSH-2 client (curve25519, ssh-ed25519, chacha20-poly1305): run commands with
             stdin; the server side of the transport for tests
  trust      https to a server with its own CA: known CA, pinned fingerprint or trust on
             first use (the presented CA comes back for the user's decision); https to
             public servers with the system's CAs
  smtp       sending mail: STARTTLS / TLS, AUTH PLAIN / LOGIN, UTF-8 messages
  Stream     what HTTP runs over (plat::net::TcpStream, tls::TlsStream)

Everything is blocking with timeouts and size limits on every read; a malformed peer gets an
error answer or a closed connection, never a panic.
```

### `netio` crate root

- **`trait Stream: Send + Sync`**
  a byte stream HTTP runs over (shared between a reader and a writer: &self methods)
  - `fn read(&self, buf: &mut [u8]) -> NetResult<usize>`
    up to buf.len() bytes; Ok(0) at the end
  - `fn write_all(&self, buf: &[u8]) -> NetResult<()>`
  - `fn set_timeouts(&self, read_ms: Option<u32>, write_ms: Option<u32>)`
    timeouts for the following reads / writes (None: block)
  - `fn shutdown(&self)`
    no more writes from this side
  - `fn wait_readable(&self, _ms: u32) -> bool`
    whether bytes (or the end) can be read within `ms`

### `netio/client.rs`

Source: [`lib/netio/client.rs`](../lib/netio/client.rs)

```text
client -- a blocking HTTP/1.1 client: one connection per request, timeouts, a size limit,
an optional connector for https (TLS wraps the TCP connection)

  Client::new().request("GET", &url, &headers, body) -> Result<Response, HttpError>
  client.with_connector(Arc<dyn Fn(&Url, TcpStream) -> Result<Box<dyn Stream>, HttpError>>)
```

- **`enum HttpError`**
  - `Url(String)` -- not an http(s) URL, or https without a connector
  - `Net(NetError)`
  - `Tls(String)` -- the TLS layer refused (certificate, handshake)
  - `Bad` -- a malformed answer
  - `TooLarge` -- an answer over the size limit

- **`type Connector = Arc<dyn Fn(&Url, TcpStream) -> Result<Box<dyn Stream>, HttpError> + Send + Sync>`**

**`impl Client`**

### `netio/der.rs`

Source: [`lib/netio/der.rs`](../lib/netio/der.rs)

```text
der -- the DER subset X.509 needs: a bounded reader (definite lengths, no indefinite
forms, at most 64 KiB per element) and a writer

  Der::new(bytes).read(tag) -> Option<(content, rest)>, peek_tag, int, oid, ...
  writer: tlv(tag, content), seq(&[parts]), int_u(bytes), oid(&[arcs]), utf8(s) ...
```

```rust
BOOLEAN: u8 = 0x01
INTEGER: u8 = 0x02
BIT_STRING: u8 = 0x03
OCTET_STRING: u8 = 0x04
NULL: u8 = 0x05
OID: u8 = 0x06
UTF8: u8 = 0x0c
PRINTABLE: u8 = 0x13
IA5: u8 = 0x16
UTC_TIME: u8 = 0x17
GEN_TIME: u8 = 0x18
SEQUENCE: u8 = 0x30
SET: u8 = 0x31
```

- **`struct Der<'a>`**
  a cursor over DER bytes
  - `b: &'a [u8]`

**`impl<'a> Der<'a>`**

- **`fn new(b: &'a [u8]) -> Der<'a>`**
- **`fn is_empty(&self) -> bool`**
- **`fn peek_tag(&self) -> Option<u8>`**
- **`fn next(&mut self) -> Option<(u8, &'a [u8], &'a [u8])>`**
  the next element: (tag, content, the whole element)
- **`fn read(&mut self, tag: u8) -> Option<&'a [u8]>`**
  the next element with this tag -> its content
- **`fn read_raw(&mut self, tag: u8) -> Option<&'a [u8]>`**
  the next element with this tag -> the whole element (for signatures over it)
- **`fn optional(&mut self, tag: u8) -> Option<&'a [u8]>`**
  an optional element with this tag
- **`fn seq(&mut self) -> Option<Der<'a>>`**
- **`fn u64(&mut self) -> Option<u64>`**
  a non-negative INTEGER up to 64 bits
- **`fn bool(&mut self) -> Option<bool>`**
- **`fn bits(&mut self) -> Option<&'a [u8]>`**
  a BIT STRING without unused bits -> its bytes

- **`fn oid_bytes(arcs: &[u64]) -> Vec<u8>`**
  the encoded OID content of dotted arcs

- **`fn tlv(tag: u8, content: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8>`**

- **`fn seq(parts: &[&[u8]]) -> Vec<u8>`**

- **`fn set(parts: &[&[u8]]) -> Vec<u8>`**

- **`fn int_u(b: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8>`**
  an INTEGER of unsigned big-endian bytes (leading zeros dropped, a zero byte added when
  the top bit is set)

- **`fn small_int(v: u64) -> Vec<u8>`**

- **`fn oid(arcs: &[u64]) -> Vec<u8>`**

- **`fn utf8(s: &str) -> Vec<u8>`**

- **`fn octets(b: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8>`**

- **`fn bits(b: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8>`**

- **`fn boolean(v: bool) -> Vec<u8>`**

- **`fn explicit(n: u8, content: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8>`**
  [n] EXPLICIT

### `netio/dns.rs`

Source: [`lib/netio/dns.rs`](../lib/netio/dns.rs)

```text
dns -- host names to addresses

  resolve(host, port) -> NetResult<Vec<SocketAddr>>   numeric addresses and "localhost"
                    directly; Windows: the system resolver (plat); Linux: /etc/hosts, then
                    the name servers of /etc/resolv.conf (search domains, ndots)
  resolve_with(host, port, &Config)   the same with a given configuration (tests)
  Config::system()                    /etc/hosts + /etc/resolv.conf

Queries: A and AAAA over UDP (2 s per server and attempt, two rounds), over TCP when the answer
is truncated; an answer must come from the server asked and carry the query's id and question.
IPv4 addresses come first.  Not here: DNSSEC validation, mDNS, caching.
```

- **`struct Config`**
  - `hosts: String` -- the text of a hosts file
  - `nameservers: Vec<SocketAddr>`
  - `search: Vec<String>`
  - `ndots: usize` -- names with at least this many dots are tried as they are first

**`impl Config`**

- **`fn system() -> Config`**
  /etc/hosts and /etc/resolv.conf of this machine
- **`fn from_resolv_conf(text: &str) -> Config`**

- **`fn hosts_lookup(hosts: &str, name: &str) -> Vec<IpAddr>`**
  the addresses of `name` in a hosts file (names compare case-insensitively)

- **`fn query(id: u16, name: &str, qtype: u16) -> Option<Vec<u8>>`**
  a query for one name and type

- **`enum Answer`**
  what an answer says
  - `Found(Vec<IpAddr>)` -- the addresses of the asked type (maybe none: the name exists without them)
  - `NoName` -- the name does not exist
  - `Truncated` -- too large for UDP: ask over TCP
  - `Bad` -- a server failure or an answer that does not fit the question

- **`fn parse_answer(m: &[u8], q: &[u8], qtype: u16) -> Answer`**
  read an answer to `q` (the query it answers)

- **`fn resolve_with(host: &str, port: u16, cfg: &Config) -> NetResult<Vec<SocketAddr>>`**

- **`fn resolve(host: &str, port: u16) -> NetResult<Vec<SocketAddr>>`**
  the addresses of a host (see the top of this file)

### `netio/http.rs`

Source: [`lib/netio/http.rs`](../lib/netio/http.rs)

```text
http -- HTTP/1.1 messages and their wire format

  Headers (case-insensitive names), Request, Response, Body
  Reader: buffered reading of a Stream (lines, heads, bodies by Content-Length or chunked)
  write_response / write_request

Limits: a message head of at most 16 KiB and 100 header lines, bodies up to the caller's
limit, chunk sizes checked before use.
```

```rust
MAX_HEAD: usize = 16 * 1024
MAX_HEADERS: usize = 100
```

- **`struct Headers(pub Vec<(String, String)>)`**
  header lines; names compare case-insensitively

**`impl Headers`**

- **`fn new() -> Headers`**
- **`fn get(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&str>`**
- **`fn set(&mut self, name: &str, value: &str)`**
  replace (or add) a header
- **`fn add(&mut self, name: &str, value: &str)`**
- **`fn remove(&mut self, name: &str)`**
- **`fn has_token(&self, name: &str, token: &str) -> bool`**
  whether a comma-separated header contains a token (Connection: close, ...)

- **`struct Request`**
  a request as the server sees it (and as the client sends it)
  - `method: String`
  - `target: String` -- path and query, as sent ("/api/jobs?limit=5")
  - `headers: Headers`
  - `body: Vec<u8>`
  - `peer: Option<SocketAddr>` -- the remote address (server side)
  - `client_id: Option<String>` -- the identity the transport proved (TLS client certificate), if any

**`impl Request`**

- **`fn new(method: &str, target: &str) -> Request`**
- **`fn path(&self) -> &str`**
  the path without the query
- **`fn query(&self, key: &str) -> Option<String>`**
  a query parameter, decoded
- **`fn header(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&str>`**
- **`fn json(&self, limits: json::Limits) -> Option<Value>`**
  the body as JSON (None: not JSON or over the limits)
- **`fn bearer(&self) -> Option<&str>`**
  the bearer token of the Authorization header

- **`enum Body`**
  a response body
  - `Empty`
  - `Bytes(Vec<u8>)`
  - `Stream { len: Option<u64>, next: Box<dyn FnMut(&mut Vec<u8>) -> bool + Send> }` -- produced piece by piece and sent chunked: the callback appends the next piece to the
    buffer and returns false after the last one (Content-Length when `len` is known)

- **`struct Response`**
  - `status: u16`
  - `headers: Headers`
  - `body: Body`

**`impl Response`**

- **`fn new(status: u16) -> Response`**
- **`fn bytes(status: u16, content_type: &str, body: Vec<u8>) -> Response`**
- **`fn text(status: u16, s: &str) -> Response`**
- **`fn json(status: u16, v: &Value) -> Response`**
- **`fn header(mut self, name: &str, value: &str) -> Response`**
- **`fn body_bytes(&self) -> &[u8]`**
  the body bytes (client side: always Bytes)
- **`fn body_json(&self, limits: json::Limits) -> Option<Value>`**

- **`fn reason(status: u16) -> &'static str`**
  the reason phrase of a status code

- **`enum ReadError`**
  why reading a message failed
  - `Closed` -- the connection ended before a message started (normal end of keep-alive)
  - `Net(NetError)`
  - `Bad` -- malformed (400)
  - `HeadTooLarge` -- head over 16 KiB or too many header lines (431)
  - `BodyTooLarge` -- body over the limit (413)

- **`struct Reader<'a>`**
  buffered reading of a stream

**`impl<'a> Reader<'a>`**

- **`fn new(s: &'a dyn Stream) -> Reader<'a>`**
- **`fn has_buffered(&self) -> bool`**
  whether unread bytes are buffered (a pipelined next request)
- **`fn head(&mut self) -> Result<(String, Headers), ReadError>`**
  the start line and the headers of a message
- **`fn body(&mut self, h: &Headers, max: usize, until_close: bool) -> Result<Vec<u8>, ReadError>`**
  a message body by Content-Length or chunked transfer (`until_close`: a response without
  either reads to the end)

- **`fn write_response(s: &dyn Stream, r: &mut Response, head_only: bool, keep_alive: bool) -> bool`**
  send a response; `head_only` for HEAD requests; false when the connection broke

- **`fn write_request(s: &dyn Stream, method: &str, target: &str, h: &Headers, body: &[u8]) -> bool`**
  send a request (the body as Content-Length)
